Sciences (RIMS), an International Joint Usage/Research Center located in Kyoto
University.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Admissible coverings and admissible fundamental groups

In this subsection, we recall some definitions and results concerning admissible coverings and admissible fundamental groups.

2.1.1.

We recall some notation concerning semi-graphs (
[
M
, Section 1]
). Let

\mathbf{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(v(\mathbf{G}),e(\mathbf{G}),\{\zeta^{\mathbf{G}}_{e}\}_{e\in e(\mathbf{G})})

be a semi-graph. Here,
v(\mathbf{G})
,
e(\mathbf{G})
, and
\{\zeta^{\mathbf{G}}_{e}\}_{e\in e(\mathbf{G})}
denote the set of vertices of
\mathbf{G}
, the set of edges of
\mathbf{G}
, and the set of coincidence maps of
\mathbf{G}
, respectively.

Let
e\in e(\mathbf{G})
be an edge. Then
e\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{b_{e}^{1},b_{e}^{2}\}
is a set of cardinality
2
for each
e\in e(\mathbf{G})
. The set
e(\mathbf{G})
consists of closed edges and open edges. If
e
is a closed edge, then the coincidence map
\zeta_{e}^{\mathbf{G}}
is a map from
e
to the set of vertices to which
e
abuts. If
e
is an open edge, then the coincidence map
\zeta_{e}^{\mathbf{G}}
is a map from
e
to the set which consists of the unique vertex to which
e
abuts and the one element set
\{v(\mathbf{G})\}
(i.e., either
\zeta_{e}^{\mathbf{G}}(b_{e}^{1})
or
\zeta_{e}^{\mathbf{G}}(b^{2}_{e})
is not contained in
v(\mathbf{G})
).

We shall write
e^{\rm op}(\mathbf{G})\subseteq e(\mathbf{G})
for the set of open edges of
\mathbf{G}
and
e^{\rm cl}(\mathbf{G})\subseteq e(\mathbf{G})
for the set of closed edges of
\mathbf{G}
. Note that we have

e(\mathbf{G})=e^{\rm op}(\mathbf{G})\cup e^{\rm cl}(\mathbf{G}).

Moreover, we denote by
e^{\rm lp}(\mathbf{G})\subseteq e^{\rm cl}(\mathbf{G})
the subset of closed edges such that
\#(\zeta_{e}^{\mathbf{G}}(e))=1
for each
e\in e^{\rm lp}(\mathbf{G})
(i.e., a closed edge which abuts to a unique vertex of
\mathbf{G}
), where “lp” means “loop”. For each
e\in e(\mathbf{G})
, we denote by
v^{\mathbf{G}}(e)\subseteq v(\mathbf{G})
the set of vertices of
\mathbf{G}
to which
e
abuts. For each
v\in v(\mathbf{G})
, we denote by
e^{\mathbf{G}}(v)\subseteq e(\mathbf{G})
the set of edges of
\mathbf{G}
to which
v
is abutted.

We shall say that
\mathbf{G}
is a
tree
if the Betti number
\text{dim}_{\mathbb{Q}}(H^{1}(\mathbf{G},\mathbb{Q}))
of
\mathbf{G}
is equal to
0
, where
\mathbb{Q}
denotes the rational number field.

Example 2.1
.

Let us give an example of semi-graph to explain the above definitions. We use the notation “
\bullet
” and “
\circ
with a line segment” to denote a vertex and an open edge, respectively.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a semi-graph as follows:

:

Then we see that
v(\mathbf{G})=\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
,
e^{\rm cl}(\mathbf{G})=\{e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}\}
,
e^{\rm op}(\mathbf{G})=\{e_{4}\}
,
\zeta_{e_{1}}^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{1})=\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
,
\zeta_{e_{2}}^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{2})=\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
,
\zeta_{e_{3}}^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{3})=\{v_{1}\}
, and
\zeta_{e_{4}}^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{4})=\{v_{2},\{v(\mathbf{G})\}\}
. Moreover, we have
e^{\rm lp}(\mathbf{G})=\{e_{3}\}
,
v^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{1})=\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
,
v^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{2})=\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
,
v^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{3})=\{v_{1}\}
,
v^{\mathbf{G}}(e_{4})=\{v_{2}\}
,
e^{\mathbf{G}}(v_{1})=\{e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}\}
, and
e^{\mathbf{G}}(v_{2})=\{e_{1},e_{2},e_{4}\}
.

2.1.2.

Let
p
be a prime number, and let

X^{\bullet}=(X,D_{X})

be a pointed stable curve over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p
, where
X
denotes the underlying curve,
D_{X}
denotes a finite set of marked points satisfying
[
K
, Definition 1.1 (iv)]
. Write
g_{X}
for the genus of
X
and
n_{X}
for the cardinality
\#(D_{X})
of
D_{X}
. We call the pair
(g_{X},n_{X})
the
topological type
(or type for short) of
X^{\bullet}
.

Recall that the
dual semi-graph

\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}),e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}),\{\zeta^{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}}_{e}\}_{e\in e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})})
of
X^{\bullet}
is a semi-graph associated to
X^{\bullet}
defined as follows: (i)
v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
is the set of irreducible components of
X
; (ii)
e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
is the set of marked points
D_{X}
; (iii)
e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
is the set of nodes of
X
; (iv)
\zeta_{e}^{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}}(e)
,
e\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, consists of the unique irreducible component containing
e
and the set
\{v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\}
; (v)
\zeta_{e}^{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}}(e)
,
e\in e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, consists of the irreducible components containing
e
.

Moreover, we write
r_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{\mathbb{Q}}(H^{1}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}},\mathbb{Q}))
for the Betti number of the semi-graph
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
.

Example 2.2
.

We give an example to explain dual semi-graphs of pointed stable curves. Let
X^{\bullet}
be a pointed stable curve over
k
whose irreducible components are
X_{v_{1}}
and
X_{v_{2}}
, whose node is
x_{e_{1}}
, and whose marked point is
x_{e_{2}}\in X_{v_{2}}
. We use the notation “
\bullet
” and “
\circ
” to denote a node and a marked point, respectively. Then
X^{\bullet}
is as follows:

:

We write
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
for the vertices of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
corresponding to
X_{v_{1}}
and
X_{v_{2}}
, respectively,
e_{1}
for the closed edge corresponding to
x_{e_{1}}
, and
e_{2}
for the open edge corresponding to
x_{e_{2}}
. Moreover, we use the notation “
\bullet
” and “
\circ
with a line segment” to denote a vertex and an open edge, respectively. Then the dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
of
X^{\bullet}
is as follows:

:

2.1.3.

Let
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
and
e\in e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
. We write
X_{v}
for the irreducible component of
X
corresponding to
v
,
x_{e}
for the node of
X
corresponding to
e
if
e\in e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, and
x_{e}
for the marked point of
X
corresponding to
e
if
e\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
. Note that
X^{\bullet}
is allowed to have components with self-intersections in general (i.e.,
e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\neq\emptyset
). Moreover, write
\widetilde{X}_{v}
for the
smooth
compactification of
U_{X_{v}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}X_{v}\setminus X_{v}^{\rm sing}
, where
(-)^{\rm sing}
denotes the singular locus of
(-)
. We call

\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet}=(\widetilde{X}_{v},D_{\widetilde{X}_{v}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(\widetilde{X}_{v}\setminus U_{X_{v}})\cup(D_{X}\cap X_{v}))

the smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{v},n_{v})
over
k
associated to
v
(or the smooth pointed stable curve associated to
v
for short). Note that
\widetilde{X}_{v}
is the normalization of
X_{v}
.

2.1.4.

Let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n,\mathbb{Z}}
be the moduli stack parameterizing pointed stable curves of type
(g,n)
over
\mathrm{Spec}\,\mathbb{Z}
,
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
the algebraically closure of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
in
k
,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n,\mathbb{Z}}\times_{\mathbb{Z}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, and
\overline{M}_{g,n}
the coarse moduli space of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
. Then
X^{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,k
determines a morphism
c_{X}:\mathrm{Spec}\,k\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
and
\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathrm{Spec}\,k
,
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, determines a morphism
c_{v}:\mathrm{Spec}\,k\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{v},n_{v}}
. Moreover, we have a clutching morphism of moduli stacks (
[
K
, Definition 3.8]
)

c:\prod_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{v},n_{v}}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}

such that
c\circ(\prod_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}c_{v})=c_{X}
. We shall say that
X^{\bullet}
is a
component-generic
pointed stable curve over
k
if the image of

\prod_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}c_{v}:\mathrm{Spec}\,k\rightarrow\prod_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{v},n_{v}}

is a generic point in
\prod_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}\overline{M}_{g_{v},n_{v}}
.

2.1.5.

We recall the definition of admissible coverings of pointed stable curves. Let
Y^{\bullet}=(Y,D_{Y})
be a pointed stable curve over
k
and
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
the dual semi-graph of
Y^{\bullet}
. Let

f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}

be a
surjective, generically étale, finite
morphism of pointed stable curves over
k
such that
f(y)
is a smooth (resp. singular) point of
X
if
y
is a smooth (resp. singular) point of
Y
. Write
f:Y\rightarrow X
for the morphism of underlying curves induced by
f^{\bullet}
and
f^{\rm sg}:\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
for the map of dual semi-graphs induced by
f^{\bullet}
. Let
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
and
w\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v)\subseteq v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}})
. Then
f^{\bullet}
induces a morphism of smooth pointed stable curves

\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{w,v}:\widetilde{Y}^{\bullet}_{w}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet}

associated to
w
and
v
(
2.1.3
).

Definition 2.3
.

We shall say that
f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
is a
Galois admissible covering
over
k
with Galois group
G
if the following conditions are satisfied: (i) There exists a finite group
G\subseteq\text{Aut}_{k}(Y^{\bullet})
such that
Y^{\bullet}/G=X^{\bullet}
, and
f^{\bullet}
is equal to the quotient morphism
Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow Y^{\bullet}/G
. (ii)
\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{w,v}
is a tame covering over
k
for each
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
and each
w\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v)
. (iii) For each
y\in Y^{\rm sing}
, we write
D_{y}\subseteq G
for the decomposition group of
y
and
\tau
a generator of
D_{y}
. Then the local morphism between singular points induced by
f
is

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{X,f(y)}\cong k[[u,v]]/uv&\rightarrow&\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y,y}\cong k[[s,t]]/st\\
u&\mapsto&s^{\#(D_{y})}\\
v&\mapsto&t^{\#(D_{y})},\end{array}

and that
\tau(s)=\zeta_{\#(D_{y})}s
and
\tau(t)=\zeta^{-1}_{\#(D_{y})}t
, where
\zeta_{\#(D_{y})}
is a primitive
\#(D_{y})
th root of unity.

Moreover, we shall say that
f^{\bullet}
is an
admissible covering
if there exists a morphism of pointed stable curves
h^{\bullet}:W^{\bullet}\rightarrow Y^{\bullet}
over
k
such that the composite morphism
f^{\bullet}\circ h^{\bullet}:W^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
is a Galois admissible covering over
k
.

Let
Z^{\bullet}
be a disjoint union of finitely many pointed stable curves over
k
. We shall say that a morphism
f_{Z}^{\bullet}:Z^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
over
k
is a
multi-admissible covering
if the restriction of
f_{Z}^{\bullet}
to each connected component of
Z^{\bullet}
is admissible. Moreover, we shall say that
f_{Z}^{\bullet}
is
étale
if the underlying morphism of curves
f_{Z}
induced by
f_{Z}^{\bullet}
is an étale morphism.

2.1.6.

We recall some facts concerning admissible fundamental groups. Let
\mathcal{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
be the open substack of the moduli stack
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
parameterizing smooth pointed stable curves. Write
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\log}
for the log stack obtained by equipping
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
with the natural log structure associated to the divisor with normal crossings
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}\setminus\mathcal{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
. Then we obtain a morphism
s\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathrm{Spec}\,k\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
determined by
X^{\bullet}\rightarrow s
. Write
s_{X}^{\log}
for the log scheme whose underlying scheme is
\mathrm{Spec}\,k
, and whose log structure is the pulling back log structure induced by the morphism
s\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
. We obtain a natural morphism
s_{X}^{\log}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\log}
induced by the morphism
s\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
and a stable log curve

X^{\log}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}s_{X}^{\log}\times_{\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\log}}\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}+1}^{\log}

over
s_{X}^{\log}
whose underlying scheme is
X
.

Let
\widetilde{x}^{\log}\rightarrow X^{\log}
be a log geometric point over a smooth point of
X
. Write
x\rightarrow X
for the geometric point induced by the log geometric point. Then we have a surjective homomorphism of log étale fundamental groups
\pi_{1}(X^{\log},\widetilde{x}^{\log})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{1}(s_{X}^{\log},\widetilde{x}^{\log})
. We call

\pi_{1}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet},x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ker}(\pi_{1}(X^{\log},\widetilde{x}^{\log})\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{1}(s_{X}^{\log},\widetilde{x}^{\log}))

the
admissible fundamental group
of
X^{\bullet}
(i.e., the geometric log étale fundamental group of
X^{\log}
). It is well known that
\pi_{1}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet},x)
independents the log structures of
X^{\log}
, and that there is a bijection between the set of open (resp. open normal) subgroups of
\pi_{1}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet},x)
and the set of admissible (resp. Galois admissible) coverings of
X^{\bullet}
over
k
.

2.1.7.

Since we only focus on the isomorphism class of
\pi_{1}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet},x)
in the present paper, for simplicity of notation, we omit the base point and denote by

\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}

the admissible fundamental group
\pi_{1}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet},x)
. Note that, by the definition of admissible coverings, the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
is naturally isomorphic to the tame fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
when
X^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
. The structure of the maximal prime-to-
p
quotient of
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
is well-known, and is isomorphic to the prime-to-
p
completion of the following group (
[
V
, Théorème 2.2 (c)]
)

\langle a_{1},\dots,a_{g_{X}},b_{1},\dots,b_{g_{X}},c_{1},\dots,c_{n_{X}}\ |\ \prod_{i=1}^{g_{X}}[a_{i},b_{i}]\prod_{j=1}^{n_{X}}c_{j}=1\rangle.

We denote by
\Pi^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X^{\bullet}}
and
\Pi^{\rm top}_{X^{\bullet}}
the étale fundamental group of the underlying curve
X
of
X^{\bullet}
and the profinite completion of the topological fundamental group of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
, respectively. We have the following natural continuous surjective homomorphisms (for suitable choices of base points)

\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi^{\text{\'{e}t}}_{X^{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm top}.

Moreover, for each
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, we denote by

\Pi_{\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet}}

the admissible fundamental group of
\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}
(i.e., the tame fundamental group of the smooth pointed stable curve associated to
v
). Then we have a natural (outer) injective homomorphism
\Pi_{\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
.

2.2.
Generalized Hasse-Witt invariants

In this subsection, we recall some notation concerning generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of cyclic admissible coverings of arbitrary pointed stable curves. On the other hand, in the case of
smooth
pointed stable curves, the generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of cyclic tame coverings have been studied by I. Bouw (
[
B
, Section 2]
) and Tamagawa (
[
T2
, Section 3]
).

2.2.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
. Moreover, let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
be the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
.

2.2.2.

We define the
p
-rank
(or
Hasse-Witt invariant
) of
X^{\bullet}
to be

\sigma_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X,\mathbb{F}_{p}))=\text{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}),

where
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}
denotes the abelianization of
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
. We shall say that
X^{\bullet}
is
ordinary
if
g_{X}=\sigma_{X}
. Moreover, we have the following:

\sigma_{X}=\sum_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}\sigma_{\widetilde{X}_{v}}+r_{X},

where
r_{X}
denotes the Betti number of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
(
2.1.2
).

2.2.3.

Let
n
be an arbitrary positive natural number prime to
p
and
\mu_{n}\subseteq k^{\times}
the group of
n
th roots of unity. Fix a primitive
n
th root
\zeta
, we may identify
\mu_{n}
with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
via the homomorphism
\zeta^{i}\mapsto i
. Let
\alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
. We denote by
X^{\bullet}_{\alpha}=(X_{\alpha},D_{X_{\alpha}})\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
the Galois multi-admissible covering with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
corresponding to
\alpha
. Write
F_{X_{\alpha}}
for the absolute Frobenius morphism on
X_{\alpha}
. Then there exists a decomposition (
[
S1
, Section 9]
)

H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})=H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})^{\rm st}\oplus H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})^{\rm ni},

where
F_{X_{\alpha}}
is a bijection on
H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})^{\rm st}
and is nilpotent on
H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})^{\rm ni}
. Moreover, we have
H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})^{\rm st}=H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})^{F_{X_{\alpha}}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k,
where
H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})^{F_{X_{\alpha}}}
denotes the subspace of
H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})
on which
F_{X_{\alpha}}
acts trivially. Then Artin-Schreier theory implies that we may identify

H_{\alpha}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X_{\alpha},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k

with the largest subspace of
H^{1}(X_{\alpha},\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}})
on which
F_{X_{\alpha}}
is a bijection.

The finite dimensional
k
-linear space
H_{\alpha}
is a finitely generated
k[\mu_{n}]
-module induced by the natural action of
\mu_{n}
on
X_{\alpha}
. Then we have the following canonical decomposition

H_{\alpha}=\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}H_{\alpha,i},

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts on
H_{\alpha,i}
as the
\zeta^{i}
-multiplication.

2.2.4.

We call

\gamma_{\alpha,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{k}(H_{\alpha,i}),\ i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},

a
generalized Hasse-Witt invariant
(see
[
B
]
,
[
N
]
,
[
T2
]
for the case of étale or tame coverings of smooth pointed stable curves) of the cyclic multi-admissible covering
X^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
. In particular, we call

\gamma_{\alpha,1}

the
first
generalized Hasse-Witt invariant of the cyclic multi-admissible covering
X_{\alpha}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
. Note that the above decomposition implies that

\text{dim}_{k}(H_{\alpha})=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\gamma_{\alpha,i}.

In particular, if
X_{\alpha}
is connected, then
\text{dim}_{k}(H_{\alpha})=\sigma_{X_{\alpha}}
.

2.2.5.

We write
\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]
for the group of divisors whose supports are contained in
D_{X}
. Note that
\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]
is a free
\mathbb{Z}
-module with basis
D_{X}
. We define the following

c^{\prime}_{n}:\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\ D\text{ mod}\ n\mapsto\text{deg}(D)\text{ mod }n.

Write
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}
for the set
\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}
and
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]
for the subset of
\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]
consisting of the elements whose coefficients are contained in
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}
. Then we have a natural bijection
\iota_{n}:(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}].

We put

(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\iota^{-1}_{n}(\text{ker}(c_{n}^{\prime})).

Note that we have
n|\text{deg}(D)
for all
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
. Moreover, we put

s(D)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{\text{deg}(D)}{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

Since every
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
can be regarded as a ramification divisor associated to some cyclic admissible covering, the structure of the maximal prime-to-
p
quotient of
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
(
2.1.7
) implies the following:

\displaystyle 0\leq s(D)\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\leq 1,\\
n_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\geq 2.\end{array}\right.

2.2.6.

We put

\widehat{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\varprojlim_{H\subseteq\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}\ \text{open}}X_{H},\ D_{\widehat{X}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\varprojlim_{H\subseteq\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}\ \text{open}}D_{X_{H}},\ \Gamma_{\widehat{X}^{\bullet}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\varprojlim_{H\subseteq\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}\ \text{open}}\Gamma_{X_{H}^{\bullet}}.

We call

\widehat{X}^{\bullet}=(\widehat{X},D_{\widehat{X}})

the universal admissible covering of
X^{\bullet}
corresponding to
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
, and
\Gamma_{\widehat{X}^{\bullet}}
the dual semi-graph of
\widehat{X}^{\bullet}
. Note that
\text{Aut}(\widehat{X}^{\bullet}/X^{\bullet})=\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
, and that
\Gamma_{\widehat{X}^{\bullet}}
admits a natural action of
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
.

Let
\widehat{X}^{\bullet}=(\widehat{X},D_{\widehat{X}})\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
be a universal admissible covering corresponding to
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
. For every
e\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, write
\widehat{e}\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{\widehat{X}^{\bullet}})
for an open edge over
e
and
x_{e}
for the marked point corresponding to
e
.

We denote by
I_{\widehat{e}}\subseteq\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
the stabilizer of
\widehat{e}
. The definition of admissible coverings (
2.1.5
) implies that
I_{\widehat{e}}\cong\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)^{p^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to the Galois group
\text{Gal}(K^{\rm t}_{x_{e}}/K_{x_{e}})
, where
K_{x_{e}}
denotes the quotient field of
\mathcal{O}_{X,x_{e}}
,
K^{\rm t}_{x_{e}}
denotes a maximal tamely ramified extension, and
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)^{p^{\prime}}
denotes the maximal prime-to-
p
quotient of
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)
. Suppose that
x_{e}
is contained in
X_{v}
. Then we have an injection

\phi_{\widehat{e}}:I_{\widehat{e}}\hookrightarrow\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}}

which factors through
I_{\widehat{e}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}^{\rm ab}
induced by the composition of (outer) injective homomorphisms
I_{\widehat{e}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
, where
\Pi_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}
denotes the admissible fundamental group of the smooth pointed stable curve
\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}
associated to
v
(
2.1.3
). Since the image of
\phi_{\widehat{e}}
depends only on
e
, we may write
I_{e}
for the image
\phi_{\widehat{e}}(I_{\widehat{e}})
. Moreover, the structure of maximal prime-to-
p
quotients of admissible fundamental groups of pointed stable curves (
2.1.7
) implies that the following holds: There exists a generator
[s_{e}]
of
I_{e}
for each
e\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
such that

\sum_{e\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}[s_{e}]=0

in
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}
. In the remainder of the present paper, we
fix
a set of generators
\{[s_{e}]\}_{e\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}
of
I_{e}
satisfying the above condition.

Definition 2.4
.

We maintain the notation introduced above.

(i) We put

D_{\alpha}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{e\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}\alpha([s_{e}])^{\sim}x_{e},\ \alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}),

where
\alpha([s_{e}])^{\sim}
denotes the element of
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}
corresponding to
\alpha([s_{e}])
via the natural bijection
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Note that we have
D_{\alpha}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
. On the other hand, for each
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
, we put

\text{Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{\alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\ |\ D_{\alpha}=D\}.

Moreover, we put

\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\gamma_{\alpha,1}\ (\ref{ghw}).

(ii) Let
t\in\mathbb{N}
be an arbitrary positive natural number,
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
, and

u=\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}u_{j}p^{j},\ u\in\{0,\dots,n\},

the
p
-adic expansion with
u_{j}\in\{0,\dots,p-1\}
. We identify
\{0,\dots,t-1\}
with
\mathbb{Z}/t\mathbb{Z}
naturally. Then
\{0,\dots,t-1\}
admits an additional structure induced by the natural additional structure of
\mathbb{Z}/t\mathbb{Z}
. We put

u^{(i)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}u_{i+j}p^{j},\ i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}.

Let
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
. We put

D^{(i)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{x\in D_{X}}\bigl(\text{ord}_{x}(D)\bigl)^{(i)}x,\ i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\},

which is an effective divisor on
X
.

Roughly speaking,
D^{(i)}
is the ramification divisor associated to a suitable Frobenius action of a Galois admissible covering whose ramification divisor is
D
(see the “in particular” part of Remark
2.4.1
below for the relationship between the generalized Hasse-Witt invariants associated to
D
and
D^{(i)}
).

Remark 2.4.1
.

Let
l\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
. We put

D(l)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}lD-\bigl(\sum_{x\in D_{X}}n\cdot\left[\frac{l\cdot\text{ord}_{x}(D)}{n}\right]x\bigl),

where
[(-)]
denotes the largest integer
\leq(-)
. Then we see that the following holds (see
2.2.4
for the definition of
\gamma_{\alpha,l}
)

\gamma_{\alpha,l}=\gamma_{l\alpha,1}=\gamma_{(l\alpha,D(l))}.

In particular, if
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
, we have
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma_{\alpha,1}=\gamma_{\alpha,p^{t-i}}=\gamma_{p^{t-i}\alpha,1}=\gamma_{(p^{t-i}\alpha,D(p^{t-i}))}=\gamma_{(p^{t-i}\alpha,D^{(i)})},\ i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}.

2.3.
Generalized Hasse-Witt invariants via line bundles

The generalized Hasse-Witt invariants can be also described in terms of line bundles and divisors.

2.3.1.
Settings

We maintain the settings introduced in
2.2.1
. Moreover, in the present subsection, we suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is
smooth
over
k
.

2.3.2.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be an arbitrary natural number prime to
p
. We denote by
\text{Pic}(X)
the Picard group of
X
. Consider the following complex of abelian groups:

\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]\overset{a_{n}}{\rightarrow}\text{Pic}(X)\oplus\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]\overset{b_{n}}{\rightarrow}\text{Pic}(X),

where
a_{n}(D)=([\mathcal{O}_{X}(-D)],nD)
,
b_{n}(([\mathcal{L}],D))=[\mathcal{L}^{n}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)]
. We denote by

\mathscr{P}_{X^{\bullet},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ker}(b_{n})/\text{Im}(a_{n})

the homology group of the complex. Moreover, we have the following exact sequence

0\rightarrow\text{Pic}(X)[n]\overset{a^{\prime}_{n}}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{P}_{X^{\bullet},n}\overset{b^{\prime}_{n}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]\overset{c^{\prime}_{n}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},

where
\text{Pic}(X)[n]
denotes the
n
-torsion subgroup of
\text{Pic}(X)
, and

a^{\prime}_{n}([\mathcal{L}])=([\mathcal{L}],0)\ \text{mod Im}(a_{n}),\ b^{\prime}_{n}(([\mathcal{L}],D))\ \text{mod Im}(a_{n}))=D\ \text{mod}\ n,

c_{n}^{\prime}(D\ \text{mod}\ n)=\text{deg}(D)\ \text{mod}\ n.

We shall define

\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}

to be the inverse image of
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}\subseteq(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]\subseteq\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]
under the projection
\text{ker}(b_{n})\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]
. It is easy to see that
\mathscr{P}_{X^{\bullet},n}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}
are free
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
-groups with rank
2g_{X}+n_{X}-1
if
n_{X}\neq 0
and with rank
2g_{X}
if
n_{X}=0
, and that there is a natural isomorphism
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{P}_{X^{\bullet},n}
.

On the other hand, let
\alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
and
f_{\alpha}^{\bullet}:X^{\bullet}_{\alpha}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
corresponding to
\alpha
. Fix a primitive
n
th root
\zeta
, we may identify
\mu_{n}
with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
via the map
\zeta^{i}\mapsto i
. Then we see

f_{\alpha,*}\mathcal{O}_{X_{\alpha}}\cong\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,i},

where locally
\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,i}
is the eigenspace of the natural action of
i
with eigenvalue
\zeta^{i}
. Moreover, we have the following natural isomorphism (
[
T2
, Proposition 3.5]
):

\text{Hom}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n},\ \alpha\mapsto([\mathcal{L}_{\alpha,1}],D_{\alpha}).

Then every element of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering of
X^{\bullet}
over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.

2.3.3.
Assumptions

In the remainder of the present paper,
we may assume that

n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1

for some positive natural number
t\in\mathbb{N}
unless indicated otherwise
.

2.3.4.

Let
([\mathcal{L}],D)\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}
. We fix an isomorphism
\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}(-D)
. Note that
D
is an effective divisor on
X
. We have the following composition of morphisms of line bundles

\mathcal{L}\overset{p^{t}}{\rightarrow}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes p^{t}}=\mathcal{L}^{\otimes n}\otimes\mathcal{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{X}(-D)\otimes\mathcal{L}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{L}.

This composite morphism induces a homomorphism
\phi_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}:H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L})\rightarrow H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}).
We denote by

\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{k}\bigl(\bigcap_{r\geq 1}\text{Im}(\phi_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}^{r})\bigl).

Write
\alpha_{\mathcal{L}}\in\text{Hom}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
for the element corresponding to
([\mathcal{L}],D)
and
F_{X}
for the absolute Frobenius morphism on
X
. Then we see that
\gamma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{L}},1}
(
2.2.4
) is equal to the dimension over
k
of the largest subspace of
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L})
on which
F_{X}^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{X}\circ\dots\circ F_{X}
is a bijection. Moreover, we have

\gamma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{L}},1}=\text{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L})^{F_{X}^{t}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k),

where
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L})^{F_{X}^{t}}
denotes the subspace of
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L})
on which
F^{t}_{X}
acts trivially. It is easy to see

H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L})^{F^{t}_{X}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k=\bigcap_{r\geq 1}\text{Im}(\phi_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}^{r}).

Then we obtain
\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}=\gamma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{L}},1}.
Moreover, since
D_{\alpha_{\mathcal{L}}}=D
, we have

\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}=\gamma_{(\alpha_{\mathcal{L}},D)}\ (\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\gamma_{\alpha_{\mathcal{L}},1}).

We have the following lemma.

Lemma 2.5
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
. Then we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha_{\mathcal{L}},D)}\leq{\rm dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X},&\text{if}\ ([\mathcal{L}],D)=([\mathcal{O}_{X}],0),\\
g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ s(D)=0,\ [\mathcal{L}]\neq[\mathcal{O}_{X}],\\
g_{X}+s(D)-1,&\text{if}\ s(D)\geq 1,\end{array}\right.

where
s(D)
is the integer defined in
2.2.5
.

Proof.

The first inequality follows from the definition of generalized Hasse-Witt invariants. On the other hand, the Riemann-Roch theorem implies that

\text{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}))=g_{X}-1-\text{deg}(\mathcal{L})+\text{dim}_{k}(H^{0}(X,\mathcal{L}))

=g_{X}-1+\frac{1}{n}\text{deg}(D)+\text{dim}_{k}(H^{0}(X,\mathcal{L}))=g_{X}-1+s(D)+\text{dim}_{k}(H^{0}(X,\mathcal{L})).

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

2.4.
Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisors

In this subsection, we recall the theory of theta divisors which was introduced by Raynaud in the case of étale coverings (
[
R1
]
), and which was generalized by Tamagawa in the case of tame coverings (
[
T2
]
).

2.4.1.
Settings

We maintain the settings introduced in
2.3.1
.

2.4.2.

Let
F_{k}
be the absolute Frobenius morphism on
\mathrm{Spec}\,k
,
F_{X/k}
the relative Frobenius morphism
X\rightarrow X_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}X\times_{k,F_{k}}k
over
k
, and
F_{k}^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{k}\circ\dots\circ F_{k}
. We put
X_{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}X\times_{k,F^{t}_{k}}k,
and define a morphism

F^{t}_{X/k}:X\rightarrow X_{t}

over
k
to be
F_{X/k}^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{X_{t-1}/k}\circ\dots\circ F_{X_{1}/k}\circ F_{X/k}.

Let
([\mathcal{L}],D)\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}
, and let
\mathcal{L}_{t}
be the pulling back of
\mathcal{L}
by the natural morphism
X_{t}\rightarrow X
. Note that
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathcal{L}_{t}
are line bundles of degree
-s(D)
(
2.2.5
). We put

\mathcal{B}_{D}^{t}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(F_{X/k}^{t})_{*}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)\bigl)/\mathcal{O}_{X_{t}},\ \mathcal{E}_{D}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathcal{B}_{D}^{t}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{t}.

Write
\text{rk}(\mathcal{E}_{D})
for the rank of
\mathcal{E}_{D}
. Then we obtain

\chi(\mathcal{E}_{D})=\text{deg}(\text{det}(\mathcal{E}_{D}))-(g_{X}-1)\text{rk}(\mathcal{E}_{D}).

Moreover, we have
\chi(\mathcal{E}_{D})=0
(
[
T2
, Lemma 2.3 (ii)]
).

2.4.3.

Let
J_{X_{t}}
be the Jacobian variety of
X_{t}
and
\mathcal{L}_{X_{t}}
a universal line bundle on
X_{t}\times J_{X_{t}}
. Let
\text{pr}_{X_{t}}:X_{t}\times J_{X_{t}}\rightarrow X_{t}
and
\text{pr}_{J_{X_{t}}}:X_{t}\times J_{X_{t}}\rightarrow J_{X_{t}}
be the natural projections. We denote by
\mathcal{F}
the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X_{t}}
-module
\text{pr}_{X_{t}}^{*}(\mathcal{E}_{D})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{X_{t}}
, and by

\chi_{\mathcal{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{k}(H^{0}(X_{t}\times_{k}k(y),\mathcal{F}\otimes k(y)))-\text{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X_{t}\times_{k}k(y),\mathcal{F}\otimes k(y)))

for each
y\in J_{X_{t}}
, where
k(y)
denotes the residue field of
y
. Note that since
\text{pr}_{J_{X_{t}}}
is flat,
\chi_{\mathcal{F}}
is independent of
y\in J_{X_{t}}
. Write
(-\chi_{\mathcal{F}})^{+}
for
\text{max}\{0,-\chi_{\mathcal{F}}\}
. We denote by

\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}\subseteq J_{X_{t}}

the closed subscheme of
J_{X_{t}}
defined by the
(-\chi_{\mathcal{F}})^{+}
th Fitting ideal
\text{Fitt}_{(-\chi_{\mathcal{F}})^{+}}\bigl(R^{1}(\text{pr}_{J_{X_{t}}})_{*}(\mathcal{F})\bigl).
The definition of
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
is independent of the choice of
\mathcal{L}_{t}
. Moreover, we have
\text{codim}(\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}})\leq 1
.

2.4.4.

In
[
R1
]
, Raynaud investigated the following property of the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}_{D}
on
X
.

Condition 2.6
.

We shall say that
\mathcal{E}_{D}
satisfies
(\star)
if there exists a line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{t}^{\prime}
of degree
0
on
X_{t}
such that

0={\rm min}\{{\rm dim}_{k}({H}^{0}(X_{t},\mathcal{E}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{t}^{\prime})),{\rm dim}_{k}({H}^{1}(X_{t},\mathcal{E}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{t}^{\prime}))\}.

Moreover,
[
T2
, Proposition 2.2 (i) (ii)]
implies that
[\mathcal{L}^{\prime}]\not\in\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
if and only if
\mathcal{E}_{D}
satisfies
(\star)
for
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, where
[\mathcal{L}^{\prime}]
denotes the point of
J_{X_{t}}
corresponding to
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. Namely,
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
is a
divisor
of
J_{X_{t}}
when
\mathcal{E}_{D}
satisfies
(\star)
. Then we have the following definition:

Definition 2.7
.

We shall say that
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}\subseteq J_{X_{t}}
is the
Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor
associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
if
\mathcal{E}_{D}
satisfies
(\star)
.

Remark 2.7.1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{E}_{D}
satisfies
(\star)
(i.e., Condition
2.6
).
[
R1
, Proposition 1.8.1]
implies that
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
is algebraically equivalent to
\text{rk}(\mathcal{E}_{D})\Theta
, where
\Theta
is the classical theta divisor (i.e., the image of
X^{g_{X}-1}_{t}
in
J_{X_{t}}
).

Remark 2.7.2
.

The definition of
\mathcal{E}_{D}
implies that the following natural exact sequence

0\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{t}\rightarrow(F_{X/k}^{t})_{*}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)\bigl)\otimes\mathcal{L}_{t}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{D}\rightarrow 0.

Let
[\mathcal{I}]\in\text{Pic}(X)[n]
. Write
\mathcal{I}_{t}
for the pulling back of
\mathcal{I}
by the natural morphism
X_{t}\rightarrow X
. We obtain the following exact sequence

\dots\rightarrow H^{0}(X_{t},\mathcal{E}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t})\rightarrow H^{1}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t})\overset{\phi_{\mathcal{L}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t}}}{\rightarrow}H^{1}(X_{t},(F_{X/k}^{t})_{*}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)\bigl)\otimes\mathcal{L}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t})

\rightarrow H^{1}(X_{t},\mathcal{E}_{D}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t})\rightarrow\dots.

Note that we have

H^{1}(X_{t},\mathcal{L}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t})\cong H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}),

H^{1}(X_{t},(F_{X/k}^{t})_{*}\bigl(\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)\bigl)\otimes\mathcal{L}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t})\cong H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)\otimes(F_{X/k}^{t})^{*}(\mathcal{L}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t}))

\cong H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(D)\otimes(\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I})^{\otimes p^{t}})\cong H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}).

Moreover, it is easy to see that the homomorphism
H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I})\rightarrow H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I})
induced by
\phi_{\mathcal{L}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{t}}
coincides with
\phi_{([\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}],D)}
. Thus, we obtain that if

\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}],D)}=\text{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}))

for some
[\mathcal{I}]\in\text{Pic}(X)[n]
, then the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
exists (i.e.,
[\mathcal{I}_{t}]\not\in\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
).

2.4.5.

Let
N
be an arbitrary non-negative integer. We put

\displaystyle C(N)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{if}\ N=0,\\
3^{N-1}N!,&\text{if}\ N\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Then we have the following proposition.

Proposition 2.8
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
exists, and that

n=p^{t}-1>C(g_{X})+1.

Then there exists a line bundle
\mathcal{I}
of degree
0
on
X
such that
[\mathcal{I}]\neq[\mathcal{O}_{X}]
, that
[\mathcal{I}^{\otimes n}]=[\mathcal{O}_{X}]
, and that
\gamma_{(([\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}],D))}={\rm dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}))
(i.e.,
[\mathcal{I}_{t}]\not\in\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
).

Proof.

By applying similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of
[
T2
, Corollary 3.10]
, the proposition follows from Remark
2.7.2
.
∎

Namely, if the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
exists, then there exists a line bundle
\mathcal{I}
of degree
0
with order
n>>0
such that the following hold:

•

[\mathcal{I}_{t}]\not\in\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}

•

the first generalized Hasse-Witt invariant (
2.2.4
) of the Galois multi-admissible covering with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
corresponding to
([\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{I}],D)
(
2.3.2
) is as large as possible.

2.4.6.

The following fundamental theorem of theta divisors was proved by Raynaud when
s(D)=0
(
[
R1
, Théorème 4.1.1]
), and by Tamagawa when
s(D)\leq 1
(
[
T2
, Theorem 2.5]
).

Theorem 2.9
.

Suppose that
s(D)\in\{0,1\}
(
2.2.5
). Then the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
exists.

We may ask whether or not the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
exists for an arbitrary
s(D)
when
X^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
. However, the following example shows that the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor
does not exist
in general.

Example 2.10
.

Let
X=\mathbb{P}_{k}^{1}
,
D_{X}=\{0,1,\infty,\lambda\}
such that
\lambda\not\in\{0,1\}
, and

D=\sum_{x\in D_{X}}\frac{p-1}{2}x

an effective divisor on
X
. Then we have
s(D)=2
. Let
([\mathcal{L}],D)
be an arbitrary element of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}
. We see that
\mathcal{E}_{D}
satisfies
(\star)
if and only if the elliptic curve defined by the equation

y^{2}=x(x-1)(x-\lambda)

is ordinary. Thus, we cannot expect that
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
exists in general.

Note that since the existence of
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
implies that
\mathcal{E}_{D}
is a semi-stable bundle, we see that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D)
holds for each
i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}
(
[
T2
, Lemma 2.15]
), where
D^{(i)}
is the divisor defined in Definition
2.4
(ii). We have the following open problem posed by Tamagawa (
[
T2
, Question 2.20]
).

Problem 2.11
.

Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is a smooth component-generic pointed stable curve (
2.1.4
) of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over
k
. Let
([\mathcal{L}],D)
be an arbitrary element of
\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}
. Moreover, suppose that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D)
holds for each
i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}
. Does the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
exist?

In Section
4
, we solve Problem
2.11
under the assumption
s(D)=n_{X}-1
(see Corollary
4.6
below).

3.
Maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariants

In this section, we introduce the maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariants for cyclic prime-to-
p
admissible coverings. The main result of the present section is Theorem
3.9
which says that the first generalized Hasse-Witt invariant of a prime-to-
p
cyclic admissible covering attains maximum
if and only if
the first generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of the induced admissible coverings of irreducible components attain maximum.

3.1.
Generalized Hasse-Witt invariants for coverings of dual semi-graphs

In this subsection, we prove two technical propositions (Proposition
3.5
and Proposition
3.8
) concerning generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of coverings of dual semi-graphs induced by a cyclic prime-to-
p
admissible coverings, which will be used in the proof of Theorem
3.9
. The readers who would like to start the proofs of Theorem
3.9
quickly may skip this subsection, after glancing at the statements of Proposition
3.5
and Proposition
3.8
.

3.1.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
. Moreover, in this subsection, let
n
be an
arbitrary positive natural number prime to
p
and
\mu_{n}\subseteq k^{\times}
the group of
n
th roots of unity. Fix a primitive
n
th root
\zeta
, we may identify
\mu_{n}
with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
via the homomorphism
\zeta^{i}\mapsto i
. Let

f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}

be a Galois admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
,
f
the underlying morphism of
f^{\bullet}
,
\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et}
the set of nodes of
X
over which
f
is étale, and

f^{\rm sg}:\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}

the map of dual semi-graphs of
Y^{\bullet}
and
X^{\bullet}
induced by
f^{\bullet}
, where “sg” means “semi-graph”. Note that
Y^{\bullet}
is connected.

We put
M_{Y^{\bullet}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}H^{1}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k
. Then
M_{Y^{\bullet}}
is a
k[\mu_{n}]
-module and admits the following canonical decomposition

M_{Y^{\bullet}}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{Y^{\bullet}}(j),

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
(or
1\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
) acts on
M_{Y^{\bullet}}(j)
as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication. Note that
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{Y^{\bullet}}(j))
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, is a generalized Hasse-Witt invariant of the Galois admissible covering
f^{\bullet}
(
2.2.4
).

3.1.2.

The dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
admits a natural action of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by the Galois admissible covering
f^{\bullet}
. We write
M_{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}}
,
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
, and
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}^{\vee}
for
H^{1}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k
,
H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k
, and the dual vector space
\pi_{1}^{\rm top}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}})^{\rm ab}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{F}_{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k
of
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
, respectively. Moreover, we
fix
a basis
\{\lambda_{q}\}_{q}
for
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
. Then we have a dual basis
\{\lambda_{q}^{\vee}\}_{q}
for
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}^{\vee}
.

Let
l\subseteq\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
be a loop and
\alpha_{l}^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{q}a_{q}\lambda_{q}^{\vee}
the vector of
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}^{\vee}
corresponding to
l
. We shall call

\alpha_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{q}a_{q}\lambda_{q}\in M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}

the vector of
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
corresponding to
l
.

Moreover, we shall say that
l
is
a minimal loop of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
if, for any loop
l^{\prime}\subseteq l\subseteq\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
such that
l
and
l^{\prime}
are homotopic, then
l=l^{\prime}
.

3.1.3.

Let
r\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. We denote by
r\cdot l
and
r\cdot\alpha_{l}^{\vee}
the natural actions of
r
on
l
and
\alpha_{l}^{\vee}
, respectively. Note that we have
r\cdot\alpha_{l}^{\vee}=\alpha_{r\cdot l}^{\vee}
.

On the other hand, we denote by
r*\alpha_{l}
the action of
r\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
on
\alpha_{l}\in M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
induced by the action of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
on
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}^{\vee}
. Then we have
(-r)*\alpha_{l}=\alpha_{r\cdot l}
. For convenience, we put

r\cdot\alpha_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(-r)*\alpha_{l}.

Then we have
r\cdot\alpha_{l}=\alpha_{r\cdot l}
and
r^{\prime\prime}\cdot(r^{\prime}\cdot\alpha_{l})=(r^{\prime}+r^{\prime\prime})\cdot\alpha_{l}
for all
r^{\prime},r^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover,
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
is a
k[\mu_{n}]
-module and admits the following canonical decomposition

M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j),

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
(or
1\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
) acts on
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j)
as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication.

3.1.4.

Firstly, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 3.1
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Then the following statements hold:

(i) Suppose that
f^{\bullet}:X^{\bullet}\rightarrow Y^{\bullet}
is a Galois étale covering (i.e.,
f
is étale) with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, and that
X^{\bullet}
and
Y^{\bullet}
are ordinary (
2.2.2
). Then we have

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{Y^{\bullet}}(j))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X},&\text{if}\ j=0,\\
g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\},\end{array}\right.

(ii) Suppose that
f^{\rm sg}:\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a Galois topological covering with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}r_{X},&\text{if}\ j=0,\\
r_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\},\end{array}\right.

where
r_{X}
denotes the Betti number of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
(
2.1.2
).

Proof.

(i) Since the underlying morphism
f
is étale, there exists a line bundle
\mathcal{L}
on
X
whose degree is
0
, and whose order is
n
, such that

f_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Y}\cong\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{L}^{\otimes j}.

Then we have
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{Y^{\bullet}}(j))=\text{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L}))=g_{X}-1
for all
j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{Y^{\bullet}}(0))=\text{dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}))=g_{X}
. This completes the proof of (i).

(ii) Since this is a topological question, to verify (ii), by adding certain marked points, we may assume that
X^{\bullet}
is an
ordinary
pointed stable curve such that the normalization of every irreducible component is
isomorphic to

\mathbb{P}_{k}^{1}
. Then
Y^{\bullet}
is also an
ordinary
pointed stable curve such that the normalizations of irreducible components are isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}_{k}^{1}
. Then (ii) follows immediately from (i). This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

3.1.5.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that
f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
is a Galois
étale
covering with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, that the set of irreducible components of
X^{\bullet}
is
\{X_{1},X_{2}\}
, and that
X_{1}
,
X_{2}
are non-singular. Write
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
for the vertices of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
corresponding to
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, respectively. Let
f^{\bullet}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{v_{i}}:Y_{i}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\widetilde{Y}_{v_{i}}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{i}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\widetilde{X}_{v_{i}}^{\bullet}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, be the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
induced by
f^{\bullet}
(see
2.1.3
for
\widetilde{X}_{v_{i}}^{\bullet}
). Moreover, we suppose that the following conditions are satisfied:

•

X_{1}^{\bullet}
and
X^{\bullet}_{2}
are ordinary pointed stable curves of type
(1,1)
. This implies that the connected components of
Y_{i}
are non-singular curves of genus (resp.
p
-rank)
1
.

•

Y_{1}
is connected and
\#(\pi_{0}(Y_{2}))=t
, where
\pi_{0}(Y_{2})
denotes the set of connected components of
Y_{2}
. Then the decomposition group of a connected component of
Y_{2}
is a subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
with order
s\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}n/t
.

Note that the structures of maximal prime-to-
p
quotients of admissible fundamental groups (
2.1.7
) imply that the above Galois admissible covering exists.

Write
w_{1}\in v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}})
for the vertex corresponding to
Y_{1}
and
w_{2,b}\in v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}})
,
b\in\{1,\dots,t\}
for the vertex corresponding to a connected component of
Y_{2}
. Then we see
v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}})=\{w_{1},w_{2,1},\dots,w_{2,t}\}
. Moreover, we write
\{e_{1,b},\dots,e_{s,b}\}
for the set of closed edges of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
connecting
w_{1}
and
w_{2,b}
. We define a minimal loop as following (for instance, see Example
3.3
below):

l_{a,b}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}w_{1}e_{a,b}w_{2,b}e_{a+1b}w_{1},\ a\in\{1,\dots,s-1\},\ b\in\{1,\dots,t\}.

Then we have
1\cdot\alpha_{l_{a,b}}=\alpha_{l_{a,b+1}}
if
1\leq b\leq t-1
, and
1\cdot\alpha_{l_{a,t}}=\alpha_{l_{a+1,1}}
if
1\leq a\leq s-2
, and

1\cdot\alpha_{l_{s-1,t}}=-\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}\alpha_{l_{a,1}}.

Note that the set of vectors
\{\alpha_{l_{a,b}}\}_{a\in\{1,\dots,s-1\},b\in\{1,\dots,t\}}
is to be linearly independent and is a basis for
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
.

Lemma 3.2
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Then we have the following:

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{if}\ j\in\{0,s,2s,\dots,(t-1)s\},\\
1,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

We put

M_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}H^{1}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}(Y_{1},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k,\ M_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigoplus_{b=1}^{t}H^{1}_{\text{\rm\'{e}t}}(Y_{w_{2,b}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}k.

Then since all the connected components of
Y_{1}
and
Y_{2}
are non-singular curves of genus (resp.
p
-rank)
1
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{1}(j))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\text{if}\ j=0,\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{2}(j))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\text{if}\ j\in\{0,s,2s,\dots,(t-1)s\},\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
M_{1}(j)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j)\cap M_{1}
and
M_{2}(j)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j)\cap M_{2}
. On the other hand, Lemma
3.1
(i) implies

{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{Y^{\bullet}}(j))={\rm dim}_{k}(M_{1}(1))+{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{2}(j))+{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j))

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2,&\text{if}\ j=0,\\
1,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Then we complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

Example 3.3
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. If
n=4
and
b=2
, we have the following

:

:

Moreover, Lemma
3.2
implies immediately the following result of linear algebras.

Corollary 3.4
.

Let
n=st\in\mathbb{N}
be a positive natural number prime to
p
,
V_{b}
,
b\in\{1,\dots,t\}
, a
k
-linear space of dimension
s-1
, and
\{\alpha_{1,b},\dots,\alpha_{s-1,b}\}
a basis for
V_{b}
. We put

V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigoplus_{b=1}^{t}V_{b},

and let
1\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. We define an action of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
on
V
as follows:
1\cdot\alpha_{a,b}=\alpha_{a,b+1}
if
1\leq a\leq s-1
and
1\leq b\leq t-1
,
1\cdot\alpha_{a,t}=\alpha_{a+1,1}
if
1\leq a\leq s-2
, and

1\cdot\alpha_{s-1,t}=-\sum_{a=1}^{s-1}\alpha_{a,1}.

Then we have the following:

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(V(j))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{if}\ j\in\{0,s,2s,\dots,(t-1)s\},\\
1,&\text{otherwise},\end{array}\right.

where
V(j)\subseteq V
denotes the
k
-linear subspace on which
1\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
acts as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication.

3.1.6.

Next, we calculate generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of dual semi-graphs when
X^{\bullet}
is irreducible.

Proposition 3.5
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that
v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})=\{v_{X}\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1,&\text{if}\ \#(v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}))=n,\\
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et}),&\text{if}\ \#(v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}))\neq n.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Suppose that
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}))=n
. Then we have that
f^{\rm sg}:\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a Galois topological covering with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Then Lemma
3.1
(ii) implies

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(i))=r_{X}-1=\#(e^{\rm cl}(X^{\bullet}))-\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))+1-1=\#(e^{\rm cl}(X^{\bullet}))-1=\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1

for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(0))=r_{X}
. Thus, we obtain the proposition when
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}))=n
. To verify the proposition, we may assume
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}))\neq n

Since
\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))=1
, we have that the linear space
M_{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}}
is spanned by the vectors corresponding to the closed edges of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
, and that
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}})=\#(e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))=\#(e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))
. Write
E^{\rm lp}\subseteq e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
for the subset of closed edges such that
(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(E^{\rm lp})\subseteq e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}})
and
E^{\rm tr}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\setminus E^{\rm lp}
, where “tr” means “tree”. Note that for every
e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr}
, every closed edge
e_{Y}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(e_{X})
abuts exactly to two different vertices of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
. Moreover, we write
E^{\rm lp,et}
(resp.
E^{\rm tr,et}
) for the subset of
E^{\rm lp}
(resp.
E^{\rm tr}
) such that
f
is étale over all the nodes corresponding to the elements of
E^{\rm lp,et}
(resp.
E^{\rm tr,et}
).

Let
e_{X}\in E^{\rm lp}
,
e_{Y}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(e_{X})
, and let
D_{e_{Y}}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
be the decomposition group of
e_{Y}
and
m_{e_{Y}}
the order of
D_{e_{Y}}
. Note that
e_{Y}
is a minimal loop of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
, and that
D_{e_{Y}}
does not depend on the choice of
e_{Y}
(i.e.,
D_{e^{\prime}_{Y}}=D_{e^{\prime\prime}_{Y}}
for all
e^{\prime}_{Y},e^{\prime\prime}_{Y}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(e_{X})
). Write
n_{e_{Y}}
for
n/m_{e_{Y}}
and
M_{e_{X}}
for the subspace of
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
spanned by
\{\alpha_{e_{Y}}\}_{e_{Y}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(e_{X})}
. Then we see that
M_{e_{X}}
is a
k[(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})/D_{e_{Y}}]
-module, that

M_{e_{X}}\subseteq\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n_{e_{Y}}-1}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(jm_{e_{Y}}),

and that
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{e_{X}})=n_{e_{Y}}.
Write
M_{e_{X}}(1)\subseteq M_{e_{X}}
for the subspace on which
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts as the
\zeta
-multiplication. Thus, we obtain
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{e_{X}}\cap M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=1
if and only if
m_{e_{Y}}=1
(i.e.,
f
is étale over the node of
X
corresponding to
e_{X}
).

Suppose that
E^{\rm tr}=\emptyset
. We have

M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1)=\bigoplus_{e_{X}\in E^{\rm lp,et}}M_{e_{X}}(1).

Then we obtain

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=\#(E^{\rm lp,et})=\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})

if
E^{\rm tr}=\emptyset
and
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}))\neq n
.

Suppose that
E^{\rm tr}\neq\emptyset
. Then we have
n\neq 2
. Let
v_{Y}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v_{X})
, and let
D_{v_{Y}}
be the decomposition group of
v_{Y}
which does not depend on the choice of
v_{Y}
(i.e.,
D_{v^{\prime}_{Y}}=D_{v^{\prime\prime}_{Y}}
for all
v^{\prime}_{Y},v^{\prime\prime}_{Y}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v_{X})
),
1<m_{v_{Y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\#(D_{v_{Y}})
, and
n_{v_{Y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}n/m_{v_{Y}}
. We put

(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v_{X})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{v_{Y,0},\dots,v_{Y,n_{v_{Y}}-1}\},

which admits a natural action of
r\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
such that
r\cdot v_{Y,0}=v_{Y,\overline{r}}
, where
\overline{r}
denotes the image of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})/D_{v_{Y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{Z}/n_{v_{Y}}\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover, without loss of generality, we may assume
Y_{v_{Y,i}}\cap Y_{v_{Y,i+1}}\neq\emptyset
for all
i\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-2\}
,
Y_{v_{Y,n_{Y}-1}}\cap Y_{v_{Y,0}}\neq\emptyset
, and
Y_{v_{Y,j^{\prime}}}\cap Y_{v_{Y,j^{\prime\prime}}}=\emptyset
for all
j^{\prime},j^{\prime\prime}\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\}
.

Let

T_{Y,e_{X}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{e_{Y,0},\dots,e_{Y,m_{v_{Y}-1}}\}\subseteq(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(e_{X})\subseteq e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}),\ e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et},

be the subset of closed edges such that
v^{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(e_{Y,i})=\{v_{Y,0},v_{Y,1}\}
for all
i\in\{0,\dots,m_{v_{Y}}-1\}
(see
2.1.1
for
v^{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(e_{Y,i})
). Then

l_{e_{X},i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}v_{Y,0}e_{Y,i}v_{Y,1}e_{Y,i+1}v_{Y,0},\ i\in\{0,\dots,m_{v_{Y}}-2\},

can be regarded as a minimal loop of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
(for instance, see Example
3.6
(a) below). Moreover, the set of vectors

\{j\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i}}\}_{i\in\{0,\dots,m_{v_{Y}}-2\},j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\},e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}

is to be linearly independent. We denote by
M_{T_{Y,e_{X}}}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
,
e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}
, the subspace spanned by

\{j\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i}}\}_{i\in\{0,\dots,m_{v_{Y}}-2\},j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\}}.

Then we see that
M_{T_{Y,e_{X}}}
,
e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}
, is a
k[\mu_{n}]
-module. Moreover, we have

1\cdot\bigl(j\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i}})=(j+1)\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i}}

if
0\leq j\leq n_{v_{Y}}-2
and
0\leq i\leq m_{v_{Y}}-2
,

1\cdot\bigl((n_{v_{Y}}-1)\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i}}\bigl)=\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i+1}}

if
0\leq i\leq m_{v_{Y}}-3
, and

1\cdot\bigl((n_{v_{Y}}-1)\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{Y},m_{v_{Y}}-2}}\bigl)=-\sum_{i=0}^{m_{v_{Y}}-2}\alpha_{l_{e_{X}},i}.

Then Corollary
3.4
implies

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{T_{Y,e_{X}}}(1))=1,

where
M_{T_{Y,e_{X}}}(1)
denotes the subspace on which
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts as the
\zeta
-multiplication.

Let
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\#(E^{\rm tr,et})
and
E^{\rm tr,et}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{e_{X,1},\dots,e_{X,m}\}
. Let
e_{Y}^{i}\in T_{Y,e_{X,i}}
,
i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
, be an arbitrary element. Note that
v^{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(e_{Y}^{i})=\{v_{Y,0},v_{Y,1}\}
. Then

l_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}v_{Y,0}e_{Y}^{i}v_{Y,1}e^{i+1}_{Y}v_{Y,0},\ i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\},

can be regarded as a minimal loop of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
(for instance, see Example
3.6
(b) below). Note that
e^{i+1}_{Y}\in T_{Y,e_{X,i+1}}
. Let

\alpha_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{j=0}^{m_{v_{Y}}-1}jn_{v_{Y}}\cdot\alpha_{l_{i}}.

Note that the decomposition group of
\alpha_{i}
is
D_{v_{Y}}
. Then the set of vectors

\{j\cdot\alpha_{i}\}_{i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\},j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\}}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}

is to be linearly independent. We denote by
M_{i}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
,
i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
, the subspace spanned by
\{j\cdot\alpha_{i}\}_{j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\}}.
Then
M_{i}
is a
k[(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})/D_{v_{Y}}]
-module. Thus, we obtain that

M_{i}\subseteq\bigoplus_{0\leq j<n_{v_{Y}}-1}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(jm_{v_{Y}}),\ i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}.

Moreover, we see that the set of vectors

\{j\cdot\alpha_{i}\}_{i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\},j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\}}\cup\{j\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i}}\}_{i\in\{0,\dots,m_{v_{Y}}-2\},j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\},e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}

is to be linearly independent.

Let
e_{Y}\in T_{Y,e_{X}}
for some
e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}
. Then we see that
j\cdot e_{Y}
,
j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-2\}
, abuts to
v_{Y,j}
and
v_{Y,j+1}
(i.e.,
v^{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j\cdot e_{Y})=\{v_{Y,j},v_{Y,j+1}\}
), and that
(n_{v_{Y}}-1)\cdot e_{Y}
abuts to
v_{Y,n_{Y}-1}
and
v_{Y,0}
(i.e.,
v^{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}((n_{v_{Y}}-1)\cdot e_{Y})=\{v_{Y,n_{v_{Y}}-1},v_{Y,0}\}
). Thus, we have that

l_{Y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}v_{Y,0}(0\cdot e_{Y})v_{Y,1}\cdots v_{Y,n_{Y}-1}((n_{v_{Y}}-1)\cdot e_{Y})v_{Y,0}

can be regarded as a minimal loop of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
(for instance, see Example
3.6
(c) below). We put

\alpha_{\pi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{j=0}^{m_{v_{Y}}-1}jn_{v_{Y}}\cdot\alpha_{l_{Y}}\in M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}.

Let us prove that the decomposition group of
\alpha_{\pi}
is
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Note that
\alpha_{\pi}
corresponds to the loop

\pi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}l_{Y}(n_{v_{Y}}\cdot l_{Y})\cdots((m_{v_{Y}}-1)n_{v_{Y}}\cdot l_{Y}).

Let
e
be an arbitrary closed edge which is contained in
\pi
. Then there exists
r\in\mathbb{Z}/n_{v_{Y}}\mathbb{Z}
such that
e
abuts to
v_{Y,r}
and
v_{Y,r+1}
. Thus,
e
can be regraded as an oriented edge induced by the oriented loop
\pi
. The starting of
e
is
v_{Y,r}
(resp.
v_{Y,n_{v_{Y}}-1}
), and the ending of
e
is
v_{Y,r+1}
if
0\leq r\leq n_{Y}-2
(resp.
v_{Y,1}
if
r=n_{Y}-1
). Consider the action of
1\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
on
e
. We see that
1\cdot e
is an oriented edge which is contained in
\pi
. Moreover, the starting of
1\cdot e
is
v_{Y,r+1}
, and the ending of
1\cdot e
is
v_{Y,r+2}
, where
r+1,r+2\in\mathbb{Z}/n_{v_{Y}}\mathbb{Z}
. Namely, we have
1\cdot\pi=\pi
. Thus, the stabilizer of
\alpha_{\pi}
is
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Write
M_{\pi}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
for the subspace spanned by
\alpha_{\pi}
. Then we have that

M_{\pi}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(0),

and that

\{j\cdot\alpha_{i}\}_{i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\},j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\}}\cup\{j\cdot\alpha_{l_{e_{X},i}}\}_{i\in\{1,\dots,m_{v_{Y}}-1\},j\in\{0,\dots,n_{v_{Y}}-1\},e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}}\cup\{\alpha_{\pi}\}

is to be linearly independent.

Let
l\subseteq\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
be an arbitrary minimal loop of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
and
V_{l}\subseteq M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}
the subspace spanned by
\{j\cdot\alpha_{l}\}_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}
. Then we have

V_{l}\subseteq\bigl(\bigoplus_{e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}}M_{T_{Y,e_{X}}}\bigl)\oplus\bigl(\bigoplus_{e_{X}\in E^{\rm lp,et}}M_{e_{X}}\bigl)\oplus\bigl(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}M_{i}\bigl)\oplus M_{\pi}.

Moreover, we see that

V_{l}\subseteq\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n/m}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(jm)

if all the decomposition groups of the elements of
e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}})\cap l
are not trivial, where
m>1
is a natural number such that
m|n
. Thus, we obtain

M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1)=\bigl(\bigoplus_{e_{X}\in E^{\rm tr,et}}M_{T_{Y,e_{X}}}(1)\bigl)\oplus\bigl(\bigoplus_{e_{X}\in E^{\rm lp,et}}M_{e_{X}}(1)\bigl).

This implies

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=\#(E^{\rm tr,et})+\#(E^{\rm lp,et})=\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})

if
E^{\rm tr}\neq\emptyset
and
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}))\neq n
. We complete the proof of the proposition.
∎

.

3.5
.

n=4
,
m_{v_{Y}}=2
and
\#(E^{\rm tr,et})=2
, we have the following:

:

:

where
v_{Y,1}=1\cdot v_{Y,0}
.

(b) For instance, if
n=4
,
m_{v_{Y}}=2
and
\#(E^{\rm tr,et})=2
, we have the following:

:

v_{Y,1}=1\cdot v_{Y,0}
.

(c) If
n=4
,
m_{v_{Y}}=2
and
\#(E^{\rm tr,et})=2
, we have the following:
