v_{Y,1}=1\cdot v_{Y,0}
.

3.1.7.

We calculate generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of dual semi-graphs when
X^{\bullet}
is not irreducible.

Lemma 3.7
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that the set of irreducible components of
X^{\bullet}
is
\{X_{1},X_{2}\}
, and that
X_{1}
,
X_{2}
are non-singular. Write
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
for the vertices of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
corresponding to
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, respectively. Let
f^{\bullet}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{v_{i}}:Y_{i}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\widetilde{Y}_{v_{i}}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{i}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\widetilde{X}_{v_{i}}^{\bullet}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, be the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
induced by
f^{\bullet}
. Then the following statements hold:

(i) Suppose that there exists
i\in\{1,2\}
such that
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))=n
. Then we have

{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1.

(ii) Suppose that
\#(X_{1}\cap X_{2})=1
(i.e.,
\#(e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))=1
). Then we have

{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1,&\text{if there exists}\ i\in\{1,2\}\ \text{such that}\ \#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))=n,\\
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et}),&\text{if for each}\ i\in\{1,2\},\ \#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))\neq n.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

(i) Suppose that either
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{1}^{\bullet}}))=n
and
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{2}^{\bullet}}))=\#(v(\Gamma_{X_{2}^{\bullet}}))=1
or
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{1}^{\bullet}}))=\#(v(\Gamma_{X_{1}^{\bullet}}))=1
and
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{2}^{\bullet}}))=n
holds. Then either
f_{1}^{\bullet}
or
f_{2}^{\bullet}
is a trivial Galois multi-admissible covering with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Then we see

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=r_{X}-r_{X_{1}}-r_{X_{2}}=r_{X}=\#(X_{1}\cap X_{2})-1=\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1,

where
r_{X_{i}}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, denotes the Betti number of the dual semi-graph of
X^{\bullet}_{i}
.

Suppose that
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{1}^{\bullet}}))=n
and
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{2}^{\bullet}}))=n
. Then
f^{\rm sg}
is a Galois topological covering of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Then Lemma
3.1
(ii) implies

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=r_{X}-1=\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1.

This completes the proof of (i).

(ii) Suppose that there exists
i\in\{1,2\}
such that
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))=n
. Then (ii) follows immediately from (i).

Suppose that
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))\neq n
for all
i\in\{1,2\}
. Let
w_{1}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v_{1})
and
w_{2}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v_{2})
be arbitrary vertices of
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
. We denote by
D_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
and
D_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
the decomposition groups of
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
, respectively, which do not depend on the choices of
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
. Since
\#(X_{1}\cap X_{2})=1
(i.e.,
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a tree), the Galois topological coverings of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
with cyclic Galois groups do not exist. Namely, either
D_{1}=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
or
D_{2}=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
holds.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
D_{1}=\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\supseteq D_{2}
. We put
m_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\#(D_{2})
and
n_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}n/m_{2}
.

Let
e_{X}
be the unique closed edge of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
,
(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{w_{1}\}
,
w_{2,0}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(v_{2})
, and
e_{Y,0}\in(f^{\rm sg})^{-1}(e_{X})
such that
e_{Y,0}
abuts to
w_{1}
and
w_{2,0}
(i.e.,
v^{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(e_{Y,0})=\{w_{1},w_{2,0}\}
). Let
D_{e_{Y,0}}
be the decomposition group of
e_{Y,0}
,
m_{e_{Y,0}}=\#(D_{e_{Y,0}})
and
n_{e_{Y,0}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}n/m_{e_{Y,0}}
. Then we see that

M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}=\bigoplus_{0\leq j\leq n_{e_{Y,0}}-1}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(jm_{e_{Y,0}}).

Then we obtain that
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=0
if
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})=0
.

Suppose that
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})=1
. Then
f^{\rm sg}:\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is equal to the covering of dual semi-graphs induced by the Galois admissible covering constructed in
3.1.5
. Thus, Lemma
3.2
implies
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=1=\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proposition 3.8
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that the set of irreducible components of
X^{\bullet}
is
\{X_{1},X_{2}\}
, and that
X_{1}
,
X_{2}
are non-singular. Then we have

{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1,&\text{if there exists}\ i\in\{1,2\}\ \text{such that}\ \#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))=n,\\
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et}),&\text{if for each}\ i\in\{1,2\},\ \#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))\neq n.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Suppose that there exists
i\in\{1,2\}
such that
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))=n
. Then the proposition follows from Lemma
3.7
(i). To verify the proposition, we may assume that
\#(v(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}}))\neq n
for all
i\in\{1,2\}
. Moreover, if
\#(X_{1}\cap X_{2})=1
, then the proposition follows from Lemma
3.7
(ii). Thus, we may assume that
\#(X_{1}\cap X_{2})\geq 2
.

First, let us construct two Galois admissible coverings associated to
f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
. Let
R
be a complete discrete valuation ring with residue field
k
,
K
the quotient field of
R
,
\overline{K}
an algebraic closure of
K
,
e\in e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
an arbitrary closed edge, and
x_{e}
the node of
X
corresponding to
e
. By deforming
X
along
x_{e}
, we obtain a pointed stable curve
\mathcal{X}
over
R
whose special fiber is
X^{\bullet}
, and whose generic fiber
X_{K}^{\bullet}
is an irreducible pointed stable curve over
K
such that
\#(e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X_{K}^{\bullet}}))=\#(e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{X_{K}^{\bullet}}))=\#(e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))-1
, where
\Gamma_{X_{K}^{\bullet}}
denotes the dual semi-graph of
X_{K}^{\bullet}
. Moreover, since the specialization homomorphism of admissible fundamental groups is a surjection, by replacing
R
by a finite extension of
R
,
f^{\bullet}
can be lifted to a finite morphism
f_{K}^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}_{K}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}_{K}
over
K
such that

f_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f^{\bullet}_{K}\times_{K}\overline{K}:Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}Y^{\bullet}_{K}\times_{K}\overline{K}\rightarrow X_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}X^{\bullet}_{K}\times_{K}\overline{K}

is a Galois admissible covering over
\overline{K}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Note that
X^{\bullet}_{\setminus e}
is irreducible. We write
\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}
for the dual semi-graph of
Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}
and denote by
M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k.
Then
M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}
is a
k[\mu_{n}]
-module and admits the following canonical decomposition

M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}(j),

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts on
M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}(j)
as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication.

On the other hand, let
\text{norm}_{e}:X_{e}\rightarrow X
be the normalization morphism of
X
over the nodes corresponding to the closed edges which are contained in
e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\setminus\{e\}
. Then we obtain a pointed stable curve

X_{e}^{\bullet}=(X_{e},D_{X_{e}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{norm}_{e}^{-1}(D_{X})\cup\{\text{norm}^{-1}(x_{e^{\prime}})\}_{e^{\prime}\in e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\setminus\{e\}})

over
k
. Note that
X_{e}
has two non-singular irreducible components
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, and that
X_{1}\cap X_{2}=\{x_{e}\}
in
X_{e}
. Then
f^{\bullet}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{e}^{\bullet}:Y_{e}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{e}^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. We write
\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}
for the dual semi-graph of
Y_{e}^{\bullet}
and denote by
M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k.
Then
M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}
is a
k[\mu_{n}]
-module and admits the following canonical decomposition

M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}(j),

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts on
M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}(j)
as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication.

Write
\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X_{\setminus e}}
and
\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X_{e}}
for the sets of nodes of
X_{\setminus e}
and
X_{e}
over which
f_{\setminus e}
and
f_{e}
are étale, respectively. Then we see immediately
\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X_{\setminus e}})+\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X_{e}})=\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X}).
The constructions of
\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}
and
\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}
imply
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}=M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}\oplus M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}
as
k[\mu_{n}]
-modules. Then we obtain

M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1)=M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}(1)\oplus M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}(1).

On the other hand, Proposition
3.5
and Lemma
3.7
imply that

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{\setminus e}^{\bullet}}}(1))+\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{e}^{\bullet}}}(1))

=\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X_{\setminus e}})+\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X_{e}})=\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X}).

This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

3.2.
Generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of curves and their irreducible components

3.2.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
and the assumption introduced in
2.3.3
(i.e.,
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
).

3.2.2.

The main result of the present section is as follows:

Theorem 3.9
.

Let
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
(
2.2.5
) and
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
(Definition
2.4
(i)). Let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
be the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
and

f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}=(Y,D_{Y})\rightarrow X^{\bullet}

the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
\alpha
. For each
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
,
f^{\bullet}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering for the smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{v},n_{v})
associated to
v
(
2.1.3
)

\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{v}:\widetilde{Y}^{\bullet}_{v}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\in{\rm Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
be the homomorphism
\Pi^{\rm ab}_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}\rightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, where
\Pi^{\rm ab}_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}\rightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}
is the natural homomorphism induced by the natural (outer) injection
\Pi_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
. Then we have (see Definition
2.4
(i) for
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
and
2.2.5
for
s(D)
)

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ {\rm Supp}(D)=\emptyset,\\
g_{X}+s(D)-1,&\text{if}\ {\rm Supp}(D)\neq\emptyset,\end{array}\right.

if and only if, for each
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{v},D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{v},&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}=0,\\
g_{v}-1,&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\neq 0,\ {\rm Supp}(D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})=\emptyset,\\
g_{v}+s(D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})-1,&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\neq 0,\ {\rm Supp}(D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})\neq\emptyset,\end{array}\right.

where
{\rm Supp}(-)
denotes the support of
(-)
.

Proof.

We prove the theorem by induction on the cardinality
\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))
of
v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
. Suppose that
\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))=1
(i.e.,
X
is irreducible). Then we have
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}|_{U_{X_{v}}}=D
and

g_{v}=g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing}),

where
U_{X_{v}}
is the open subset of
X_{v}
defined in
2.1.3
. Moreover, since
\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
, we write
([\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}],D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v},n}
for the pair induced by
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}
(
2.3.2
). Write
\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm ra}\subseteq X^{\rm sing}
for the subset of nodes over which
f
is ramified and
\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et}\subseteq X^{\rm sing}
for the subset of nodes over which
f
is étale. Then we have
s(D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})=s(D)+\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm ra})
.

On the other hand, by Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(H^{1}(\widetilde{X}_{v},\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{v},&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}=0,\\
g_{v}-1,&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\neq 0,\ {\rm Supp}(D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})=\emptyset,\\
g_{v}+s(D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})-1,&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\neq 0,\ {\rm Supp}(D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})\neq\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

Write
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}
for the dual semi-graph of
Y^{\bullet}
. The natural
k[\mu_{n}]
-submodule

H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k\subseteq H^{1}_{\rm\text{\'{e}t}}(Y,\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k

admits the following canonical decomposition

H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j),

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts on
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j)
as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication. By Proposition
3.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et})-1,&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}=0,\\
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X}^{\rm et}),&\text{if}\ \widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm dim}_{k}(H^{1}(\widetilde{X}_{v},\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}))+\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)=\emptyset,\\
g_{X}+s(D)-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)\neq\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

Since
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}],D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})}+\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))
and
\#(X^{\rm sing})=\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm ra}_{X})+\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm et}_{X})
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)=\emptyset,\\
g_{X}+s(D)-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)\neq\emptyset\end{array}\right.

if and only if
\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{v},D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})}=\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}],D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})}={\rm dim}_{k}(H^{1}(\widetilde{X}_{v},\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})).
This completes the proof of the proposition when
\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))=1
.

Suppose
m\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))\geq 2
. Let
v_{0}\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
be a vertex such that
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}\setminus\{v_{0},e^{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}}(v_{0})\}
(see
2.1.1
for
e^{\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}}(v_{0})
) is connected (note that
v_{0}
always exists). Write
X_{1}
for the topological closure of
X\setminus X_{v_{0}}
in
X
and
X_{2}
for
X_{v_{0}}
. Note that
X_{1}
is connected. We define a pointed stable curve

X_{i}^{\bullet}=(X_{i},D_{X_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(X_{i}\cap D_{X})\cup(X_{1}\cap X_{2})),\ i\in\{1,2\},

of type
(g_{X_{i}},n_{X_{i}})
over
k
. Then
f^{\bullet}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{i}^{\bullet}:Y_{i}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{i}^{\bullet},\ i\in\{1,2\},

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Moreover, we denote by
\alpha_{i}\in\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}_{i}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}),\ i\in\{1,2\},
the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\rightarrow\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, where
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{i}}
denotes the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}_{i}
.

Write
\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}^{\rm ra}\subseteq X_{1}\cap X_{2}
for the subset of nodes over which
f
is ramified and
\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}^{\rm et}\subseteq X_{1}\cap X_{2}
for the subset of nodes over which
f
is étale. Then we have
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}^{\rm ra})+\#(\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}^{\rm et})=\#(X_{1}\cap X_{2})
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha_{i},D_{\alpha_{i}})}\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X_{i}},&\text{if}\ \alpha_{i}=0,\\
g_{X_{i}}-1,&\text{if}\ \alpha_{i}\neq 0,\ \text{Supp}(D_{\alpha_{i}})=\emptyset,\\
g_{X_{i}}+s(D_{\alpha_{i}})-1,&\text{if}\ \alpha_{i}\neq 0,\ \text{Supp}(D_{\alpha_{i}})\neq\emptyset,\end{array}\right.

for all
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Note that the definition of admissible coverings implies that
s(D_{\alpha_{1}})+s(D_{\alpha_{2}})=s(D)+\#(\mathscr{N}^{\rm ra}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}})
.

On the other hand, the natural
k[\mu_{n}]
-modules
H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k
and
H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y_{i}^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, admit the following canonical decomposition

H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j),

H^{1}(\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}_{i}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}_{i}}}(j),\ i\in\{1,2\},

respectively, where
\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}_{i}}
denotes the dual semi-graph of
Y_{i}^{\bullet}
, and
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts on
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(j)
and
M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}_{i}}}(j)
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication, respectively. We put

\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}}(1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y^{\bullet}}}(1))-\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{1}^{\bullet}}}(1))-\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{2}^{\bullet}}}(1)).

Let us compute
\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}}(1))
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
are non-singular. Then Proposition
3.8
implies that the following holds:

{\rm dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}}(1))=

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\#(\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}^{\rm et})-1,&\text{if there exists}\ i\in\{1,2\}\ \text{such that}\ \alpha_{i}=0,\\
\#(\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}^{\rm et}),&\text{if for each}\ i\in\{1,2\},\ \alpha_{i}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Since
\alpha\neq 0
, we obtain

\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma_{(\alpha_{1},D_{\alpha_{1}})}+\gamma_{(\alpha_{2},D_{\alpha_{2}})}+\text{dim}_{k}(M_{\Gamma_{Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}}(1))

\leq g_{X_{1}}+s(D_{\alpha_{1}})+g_{X_{2}}+s(D_{\alpha_{2}})+\#(\mathscr{N}_{X_{1}\cap X_{2}}^{\rm et})-2=g_{X}+s(D)-1

\displaystyle=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)=\emptyset,\\
g_{X}+s(D)-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)\neq\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)=\emptyset,\\
g_{X}+s(D)-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(D)\neq\emptyset\end{array}\right.

if and only if

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha_{i},D_{\alpha_{i}})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X_{i}},&\text{if}\ \alpha_{i}=0,\\
g_{X_{i}}-1,&\text{if}\ \alpha_{i}\neq 0,\ \text{Supp}(D_{\alpha_{i}})=\emptyset,\\
g_{X_{i}}+s(D_{\alpha_{i}})-1,&\text{if}\ \alpha_{i}\neq 0,\ \text{Supp}(D_{\alpha_{i}})\neq\emptyset,\end{array}\right.

for all
i\in\{1,2\}
. By induction, the theorem follows from the theorem when
\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))=m-1
and
\#(v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))=1
. This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

3.2.3.

We define the maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariants as follows:

Definition 3.10
.

(i) We put

\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{max}_{t\in\mathbb{N}}\{\gamma_{(\alpha,D_{\alpha})}\ |\ \alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}},\mathbb{Z}/(p^{t}-1)\mathbb{Z})\ \text{and}\ \alpha\neq 0\}

=\text{max}_{m\in\mathbb{N}\ \text{s.t.}\ (m,p)=1}\{\gamma_{(\alpha,D_{\alpha})}\ |\ \alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}},\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})\ \text{and}\ \alpha\neq 0\},

and call
\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}

the maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariant of prime-to-
p
cyclic admissible coverings
of
X^{\bullet}
. Note that Theorem
3.9
implies that

\displaystyle\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

(ii) Let
m
be a natural number prime to
p
and
\alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}},\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})
. We shall say that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D_{\alpha})}
attains maximum
if

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D_{\alpha})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

We shall say that
\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}
attains maximum
if there exist a prime-to-
p
natural number
m^{\prime}
and an element
\alpha^{\prime}\in\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}},\mathbb{Z}/m^{\prime}\mathbb{Z})
such that
\gamma_{(\alpha^{\prime},D_{\alpha^{\prime}})}
attains maximum.

3.3.
An easy case of main results

3.3.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
.

3.3.2.

By applying Nakajima and Raynaud’s results concerning ordinariness of cyclic étale coverings, we have the following result.

Proposition 3.11
.

(i) Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
be an arbitrary natural number prime to
p
,
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
, and
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
. Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is an irreducible component-generic pointed stable curve over
k
, and that
n_{X}\leq 1
. Then
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum. Namely, the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=g_{X}-1.

(ii) Let
X^{\bullet}
be an arbitrary pointed stable curve over
k
. Suppose that
n_{X}\leq 1
. Then
\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}
attains maximum. Namely, the following holds:

\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=g_{X}-1.

Proof.

Since
n_{X}\leq 1
, the structures of maximal prime-to-
p
quotients of admissible fundamental groups (
2.1.7
) imply
D=0
. Namely, all of the prime-to-
p
cyclic Galois admissible coverings of
X^{\bullet}
are étale over
D_{X}
(in particular, are étale if
X^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
).

(i) This follows immediately from
[
N
, Proposition 4]
and Theorem
3.9
.

(ii) Let
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, and let
\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}
be the smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{v},n_{v})
over
k
associated to
v
(
2.1.3
). We denote by

\mathscr{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\ |\ g_{v}>0\}.

Suppose that
\mathscr{V}=\emptyset
. Then
n_{X}\leq 1
implies that
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is not a tree. Namely,
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm top}
is not trivial. Let
\alpha^{\prime}:\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm top,ab}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
be a surjection and
\alpha:\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
the composite of the homomorphisms
\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm top,ab}\overset{\alpha^{\prime}}{\twoheadrightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Then the theorem follows from Lemma
3.1
(ii).

Suppose that
\mathscr{V}\neq\emptyset
. Let
v\in\mathscr{V}
. Then Proposition
2.8
and Theorem
2.9
imply that there exists an element
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\in\text{Rev}_{0}^{\rm adm}(\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet})
such that
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}:\Pi^{\rm ab}_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is a surjective, and that

\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{v},0)}=g_{v}-1.

Write
\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{v}:\widetilde{Y}^{\bullet}_{v}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}
for the connected Galois étale covering with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be the set of connected components of
\overline{X\setminus\bigcup_{v\in\mathscr{V}}X_{v}}
and
C\in\mathscr{C}
, where
\overline{X\setminus\bigcup_{v\in\mathscr{V}}X_{v}}
denotes the topological closure of
X\setminus\bigcup_{v\in\mathscr{V}}X_{v}
in
X
. We define

C^{\bullet}=(C,D_{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(C\cap\bigcup_{v\in\mathscr{V}}X_{v})\cup(D_{X}\cap C))

to be a pointed stable curve over
k
. Note that the normalization of each irreducible component of
C
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k}
. We put

Y^{\bullet}_{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}C_{i}^{\bullet},

where
C_{i}^{\bullet}
is a copy of
C^{\bullet}
. Then we obtain a Galois multi-admissible covering
f_{C}^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}_{C}\rightarrow C^{\bullet}
over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, where the restriction morphism
f_{C}^{\bullet}|_{C^{\bullet}_{i}}
is an identity, and the Galois action is
j(C_{i})=C_{i+j}
for all
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. By gluing
\{\widetilde{Y}_{v}^{\bullet}\}_{v\in\mathscr{V}}\ \text{and}\ \{Y^{\bullet}_{C}\}_{C\in\mathscr{C}}
along
\{D_{\widetilde{X}_{v}}\}_{v\in\mathscr{V}}
and
\{D_{C}\}_{C\in\mathscr{C}}
in a way that is compatible with the gluing of
\{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}\}_{v\in\mathscr{V}}\cup\{C^{\bullet}\}_{C\in\mathscr{C}}
that gives rise to
X^{\bullet}
, we obtain a Galois (étale) admissible covering

f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Write
\alpha\in\text{Rev}_{0}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
for an element induced by
f^{\bullet}
such that the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi^{\rm ab}_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}}\rightarrow\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
,
v\in\mathscr{V}
, is equal to
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}
. Then (ii) follows from Theorem
3.9
.
∎

3.3.3.

If
X^{\bullet}
is an arbitrary
component-generic pointed stable curve
(i.e., possibly singular) over
k
, we see that Proposition
3.11
(i)
does not hold in general
. For instance, we have the following example:

Example 3.12
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be a component-generic pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},0)
over
k
with two smooth irreducible components
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
of genus
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
, respectively. Moreover, suppose that
X_{1}\cap X_{2}=\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}
. We put

X_{j}^{\bullet}=(X_{j},D_{X_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}X_{1}\cap X_{2}),\ j\in\{1,2\}.

Let
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
, and let
D_{1}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{1}}]^{0}
be an effective divisor on
X_{1}
such that
s(D_{1})=2
, and that
s(D_{1}^{(i)})=1<s(D_{1})=2
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,t-1\}
(see Definition
2.4
(ii) for
D_{1}^{(i)}
). We put

D_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(n-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{1}))x_{1}+(n-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{1}))x_{2}+(n-\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{1}))x_{3}.

Then
D_{2}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{2}}]^{0}
is an effective divisor on
X_{2}
with degree
n
(i.e.,
s(D_{2})=1
).

Let
\alpha_{j}\in\text{Rev}_{D_{j}}^{\rm adm}(X_{j}^{\bullet})
,
j\in\{1,2\}
, and let
f_{j}^{\bullet}:Y_{j}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{j}^{\bullet}
be the Galois multi-admissible covering induced by
\alpha_{j}
. Then by gluing
\{X^{\bullet}_{j}\}_{j=1,2}
and
\{Y^{\bullet}_{j}\}_{j=1,2}
, we obtain a Galois multi-admissible covering

f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}

over
k
. Write
\alpha\in\text{Rev}_{0}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
for the element induced by
f^{\bullet}
. Then Theorem
3.9
implies that
\gamma_{(\alpha,0)}=g_{X}-1=g_{1}+g_{2}+1
if and only if
\gamma_{(\alpha_{1},D_{1})}=g_{1}+1
and
\gamma_{(\alpha_{2},D_{2})}=g_{2}
. On the other hand, since
s(D_{1}^{(i)})=1
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,t-1\}
, we have that
\gamma_{(\alpha_{1},D_{1})}=g_{1}
. Then we have

\gamma_{(\alpha,0)}\neq g_{X}-1.

Namely,
\gamma_{(\alpha,0)}
does not attain maximum.

On the other hand, if
X^{\bullet}

is not a component-generic pointed stable curve
, then Proposition
3.11
(i) does not hold in general. In fact, if
k
is an algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
, then Proposition
3.11
(i)
does not hold for any pointed stable curves
over
k
(see Proposition
4.14
(ii)).

3.3.4.

The main goals of the next two sections are to generalize Proposition
3.11
to the case of (possibly singular) pointed stable curves of an arbitrary type
(g_{X},n_{X})
. More precisely, we will generalize Proposition
3.11
as follows:

The first main theorem: a generalization of Proposition
3.11
(i)

Let
n
be a natural number prime to
p
and
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
. Suppose that
D
satisfies a certain condition (i.e.,
a necessary condition
that
\gamma_{(\alpha^{\prime},D)}
attains maximum for some
\alpha^{\prime}\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
). We will prove that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum for
all

\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
when
X^{\bullet}
is a certain component-generic pointed stable curve (Theorem
4.12
(i)). Moreover, we will prove that
\gamma_{(\beta,D)}
attains maximum for
some

\beta\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
when
X^{\bullet}
is an
arbitrary
component-generic pointed stable curve (Theorem
4.12
(ii)).

The second main theorem: a generalization of Proposition
3.11
(ii)

We will prove that
\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}
attains maximum for
arbitrary
pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over
k
(Theorem
5.4
).

4.
Maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariants for generic curves

In the present section, we discuss the maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of cyclic admissible coverings for generic curves. The main result of this section is Theorem
4.12
.

4.1.
Idea

We briefly explain the idea of our proof of Theorem
4.12
.

4.1.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
. Moreover, we suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is a
component-generic
pointed stable curve over
k
(
2.1.4
).

4.1.2.

Let
n
be a natural number prime to
p
,
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
(
2.2.5
),
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
(Definition
2.4
(i)), and
f_{\alpha}^{\bullet}:X_{\alpha}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
the Galois multi-admissible covering corresponding to
\alpha
. Note that there exists a natural number
t\in\mathbb{N}
such that
p^{t}=1
in
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. Write
m^{\prime}
for
(p^{t}-1)/n
. To compute
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
, by replacing
\alpha
and
D
by the composition of homomorphisms
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\overset{m^{\prime}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/(p^{t}-1)\mathbb{Z}
and
m^{\prime}D
, we may assume that
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
.

A necessary condition
(see
4.2
) that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum (Definition
3.10
(ii)) is the following (see Definition
2.4
(ii) for
D^{(i)}
):

\text{deg}(D^{(i)})=\text{deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n,\ i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}.

Then we may assume that
D
satisfies the above condition.

4.1.3.
Irreducible case

Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is irreducible. Moreover, by Proposition
3.11
, we may assume
n_{X}\geq 2
. First, if
(g_{X},n_{X})=(0,3)
, then the first main result follows from a result of I. Bouw (Lemma
4.3
). Next, for the case of an arbitrary
(g_{X},n_{X})
, since
X^{\bullet}
is component-generic, we introduce certain degeneration data (i.e., degenerations of
X^{\bullet}
) concerning
X^{\bullet}
(see
4.3.2
) such that, for instance, the irreducible components of the degeneration data are either type of
(0,3)
or
(1,0)
. Then by applying Lemma
4.3
, Proposition
3.11
(i), and Theorem
3.9
, we see that the first generalized Hasse-Witt invariants (
2.2.4
) of Galois multi-admissible coverings of the degenerations of
X^{\bullet}
induced by
f_{\alpha}^{\bullet}
attain maximum. Moreover, the specialization homomorphisms of admissible fundamental groups implies that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum. This completes the proof of Theorem
4.12
(i) when
X^{\bullet}
is irreducible (Proposition
4.5
).

4.1.4.
General case

We have been seen that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
does not attain maximum for all
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
in general if
X^{\bullet}
is an arbitrary component-generic pointed stable curve (e.g. Example
3.12
). To avoid such situation, we introduce a kind of semi-graph associated to the sets of marked points of pointed stable curves, which we call
a minimal quasi-tree
(
4.4
). Roughly speaking, a minimal quasi-tree is a minimal tree-like “sub-semi-graph” of the dual semi-graph of a pointed stable curve which contains all open edges. We see that, if the dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a minimal quasi-tree, then the ramifications over nodes of a Galois multi-admissible covering can be completely determined by the ramifications over the marked points
D_{X}
. Thus, by applying Theorem
3.9
, we can reduce Theorem
4.12
(i) to the case where
X^{\bullet}
is irreducible. This completes Theorem
4.12
(i) (Proposition
4.11
). Moreover, by gluing certain Galois multi-admissible coverings, Theorem
4.12
(ii) follows from Theorem
3.9
, Proposition
3.11
(i), and Theorem
4.12
(i).

4.2.
A necessary condition

4.2.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
and the assumption introduced in
2.3.3
(i.e.,
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
). Moreover, let
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
, and let

{\rm ord}_{x}(D)=\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}d_{x,j}p^{j},\ x\in D_{X}

be the
p
-adic expansion. For any
x\in D_{X}
, we put
d_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}{\rm ord}_{x}(D)
and
d_{x}^{(i)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(\text{ord}_{x}(D))^{(i)}
(Definition
2.4
(ii)).

4.2.2.

First, we have the following necessary condition that the first generalized Hasse-Witt invariants attain maximum.

Lemma 4.1
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that
n_{X}\geq 2
. Then the following statements hold:

(i) Suppose that
s(D)=n_{X}-1
. Then
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D)
for all
i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}
if and only if

\sum_{x\in D_{X}}d_{x,j}=(n_{X}-1)(p-1),\ j\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}.

Note that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n
is equivalent to
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})={\rm deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n
.

(ii) Suppose that there exists an element
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
such that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum. Then we have
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})={\rm deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n
for all
i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}
.

(iii) Suppose that
n>n_{X}-1
. Then there exists
D^{\prime}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
such that
s(D^{\prime})=n_{X}-1
, and that

{\rm deg}((D^{\prime})^{(i)})={\rm deg}(D^{\prime})=(n_{X}-1)n,\ i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}.

Proof.

(i) The “if” part of (i) is trivial. We only prove the “only if” part of (i). Since
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D)
for all
i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}
and
n|{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})
, we have

{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})=\sum_{x\in D_{X}}(\text{ord}_{x}(D))^{(i)}=\sum_{x\in D_{X}}d_{x}^{(i)}=(n_{X}-1)n.

Moreover, for each
i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}
, we have

d^{(i+1)}_{x}=d_{x,i}p^{t-1}+\frac{d_{x}^{(i)}-d_{x,i}}{p}=\frac{1}{p}d^{(i)}_{x}+\frac{p^{t}-1}{p}d_{x,i}=\frac{1}{p}d^{(i)}_{x}+\frac{n}{p}d_{x,i}.

Thus, we obtain

(n_{X}-1)n=\sum_{x\in D_{X}}d_{x}^{(i+1)}=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{x\in D_{X}}d_{x}^{(i)}+\frac{n}{p}\sum_{x\in D_{X}}d_{x,i}

=\frac{1}{p}(n_{X}-1)n+\frac{n}{p}\sum_{x\in D_{X}}d_{x,i}.

Then we have

\sum_{x\in D_{X}}d_{x,i}=(n_{X}-1)(p-1),\ i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}.

(ii) This follows from Definition
3.10
(ii) and Remark
2.4.1
.

(iii) Note that there exists
D^{\prime\prime}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
such that
s(D^{\prime\prime})=(n_{X}-1)n
if
n>n_{X}-1
. Then (iii) follows from (i). We complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

4.2.3.

The following lemma will be used in the constructions of Galois admissible coverings of degenerations of
X^{\bullet}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that
n_{X}\geq 3
, and that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})={\rm deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n
for all
i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}
. Moreover, we put
D_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{X}}\}
and

a_{l,l+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}[\sum_{r=l+1}^{n_{X}}d_{x_{r}}],\ b_{l,l+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}[\sum_{r=1}^{l}d_{x_{r}}],\ l\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-2\},

where
[(-)]
denotes the integer which is equal to the image of
(-)
in
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
when we identify
\{0,\dots,n-1\}
with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
naturally. Then, for each
i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}
, we have

\ a_{l,l+1}^{(i)}+b_{l,l+1}^{(i)}=n,\ l\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-2\},

d_{x_{1}}^{(i)}+d_{x_{2}}^{(i)}+a_{2,3}^{(i)}=2n,

b_{n_{X}-2,n_{X}-1}^{(i)}+d_{x_{n_{X}-1}}^{(i)}+d_{x_{n_{X}}}^{(i)}=2n,

b_{l,l+1}^{(i)}+d_{x_{l+1}}^{(i)}+a_{l+1,l+2}^{(i)}=2n,\ l\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-3\}.

Proof.

First, let us treat the first equality. Let
l\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-2\}
and
i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}
. Since

\sum_{r=l+1}^{n_{X}}d_{x_{r}}+\sum_{r=1}^{l}d_{x_{r}}=\text{deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n,

we see
n|(a_{l,l+1}+b_{l,l+1})
. Note that if
a_{l,l+1}+b_{l,l+1}=0
, then
\text{deg}(D)<(n_{X}-1)n
. Thus,
a_{l,l+1}+b_{l,l+1}\neq 0
. Moreover, since
a_{l,l+1}+b_{l,l+1}\leq n
, we obtain that
a_{l,l+1}+b_{l,l+1}=n
, and that
a^{(i)}_{l,l+1}+b^{(i)}_{l,l+1}
is divided by
n
. On the other hand, since
0<a^{(i)}_{l,l+1},b^{(i)}_{l,l+1}<n
, we have
a_{l,l+1}^{(i)}+b_{l,l+1}^{(i)}=n
. This completes the proof of the first equality.

Let
i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}
. We denote by

S^{(i)}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}d^{(i)}_{x_{1}}+d^{(i)}_{x_{2}}+a^{(i)}_{2,3},

S^{(i)}_{l}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b^{(i)}_{l,l+1}+d^{(i)}_{x_{l+1}}+a^{(i)}_{l+1,l+2},\ l\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-3\},

S^{(i)}_{n_{X}-2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b^{(i)}_{n_{X}-2,n_{X}-1}+d^{(i)}_{x_{n_{X}-1}}+d^{(i)}_{x_{n_{X}}}.

We have
S_{l}^{(i)}\leq 2n
,
\ l\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}-2\}
. Moreover, if
i=0
, the definitions of
a_{l,l+1}
and
b_{l,l+1}
imply

S_{l}^{(0)}=2n,\ l\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}-2\}.

Then we have that
S_{l}^{(i)}
is divided by
n
for all
i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}
. Since
s(D^{(i)})=n_{X}-1
, the first equality implies that

\sum_{l=1}^{n_{X}-2}S_{l}^{(i)}=n(n_{X}-3)+(n_{X}-1)n=2n(n_{X}-2).

On the other hand, since
S_{l}^{(i)}\leq 2n
, we have

S_{l}^{(i)}=2n,\ l\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}-2\},\ i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

4.2.4.

The following lemma follows immediately from
[
B
, Corollary 6.8]
.

Lemma 4.3
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that
X^{\bullet}=(X,D_{X})
is a smooth component-generic pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})=(0,3)
over
k
. Moreover, we suppose that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})={\rm deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n=2n
for all
i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}.
Then the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
exists (see Definition
2.7
). Moreover, we have (see
2.3.4
for
\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}
)

\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}],D)}={\rm dim}_{k}(H^{1}(X,\mathcal{L})),\ ([\mathcal{L}],D)\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{X^{\bullet},n}.

Remark 4.3.1
.

Note that, if
n_{X}=3
, then we have
s(D)\in\{0,1,2\}
.

4.3.
Degenerations

In this subsection, we introduce certain degenerations of
X^{\bullet}
and prove the first main result in the case of
irreducible
component-generic pointed stable curves (Proposition
4.5
).

4.3.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
and the assumption introduced in
2.3.3
(i.e.,
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
). Moreover, we assume that
X^{\bullet}=(X,D_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{X}}\})
is an
irreducible
component-generic pointed stable curve over
k
.

4.3.2.
Degeneration data

We introduce some degeneration data for
X^{\bullet}
. Let
R
be a discrete valuation ring with algebraically closed residue field
k_{R}
,
K_{R}
the quotient field of
R
, and
\overline{K}_{R}
an algebraic closure of
K_{R}
. Suppose that
k\subseteq K_{R}
. Let
\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}=(\mathcal{X},D_{\mathcal{X}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{e_{1},\dots,e_{n_{X}}\})
be a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over
R
. We put

\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{\eta}=(\mathcal{X}_{\eta},D_{\mathcal{X}_{\eta}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{e_{\eta,1},\dots,e_{\eta,n_{X}}\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}\times_{R}K_{R},

\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}}=(\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}},D_{\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{e_{\overline{\eta},1},\dots,e_{\overline{\eta},n_{X}}\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}\times_{R}\overline{K}_{R},

\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{s}=(\mathcal{X}_{s},D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{e_{s,1},\dots,e_{s,n_{X}}\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}\times_{R}k_{R}.

We shall say that
X^{\bullet}

admits a (DEG)
if
(g_{X},n_{X})\neq(1,1)
and there exists
\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}
such that the following conditions hold, where “(DEG)” means “degeneration”:

(i) We have that
\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}}
is
\overline{K}_{R}
-isomorphic to
X^{\bullet}\times_{k}\overline{K}_{R}
, and that
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}
is a
component-generic
pointed stable curve over
k_{R}
. Then without loss of generality, we may identify
e_{\overline{\eta},r},\ r\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}\}
, with
x_{r}\times_{k}\overline{K}_{R}
via this isomorphism.

(iii) Let
\mathscr{T}
be a set with cardinality
\#(\mathscr{T})=\#(X^{\rm sing})
which consists of irreducible singular projective semi-stable curves of genus
1
(i.e., the normalizations are
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k_{R}}
). Let
\mathscr{P}
be a set which consists of smooth semi-stable curves of genus
0
(i.e., a set of
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{k_{R}}
). Let
C_{1}
be either an empty set or a smooth semi-stable curve of genus
g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing})
. We have the set of irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}_{s}
is

\{T\}_{T\in\mathscr{T}}\cup\{C_{1}\}\cup\{P\}_{P\in\mathscr{P}}.

Moreover, one of the following conditions is satisfied (see Example
4.4
below for examples of (a) (b) (c)):

(a) Suppose that
n_{X}\leq 1
and
\#(X^{\rm sing})\geq 1
. Then the following conditions hold:

•

C_{1}=\emptyset
when
n_{X}=0
and
\#(X^{\rm sing})=2
; otherwise,
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
.

•

When
C_{1}=\emptyset
, we have
\mathscr{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{T_{1},T_{2}\}
such that
\#(T_{1}\cap T_{2})=1
.

•

When
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
, we have that
T^{\prime}\cap T^{\prime\prime}\not\neq\emptyset
if and only if
T^{\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}
for all
T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{T}
, that
\#(T\cap C_{1})=1
for all
T\in\mathscr{T}
, and that
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\subseteq C_{1}
.

•

\mathscr{P}=\emptyset
.

(b) Suppose that
n_{X}=2
. Then the following conditions hold:

•

T^{\prime}\cap T^{\prime\prime}\not\neq\emptyset
if and only if
T^{\prime}=T^{\prime\prime}
for all
T^{\prime},T^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{T}
.

•

C_{1}=\emptyset
when
g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing})=0
; otherwise,
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
when
g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing})\geq 1
.

•

\mathscr{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{P\}
such that
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\subseteq P
.

•

When
C_{1}=\emptyset
, we have
\#(P\cap T)=1
for all
T\in\mathscr{T}
.

•

When
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
, we have that
\#(C_{1}\cap T)=1
, that
\#(C_{1}\cap P)=1
, and that
P\cap T=\emptyset
for all
T\in\mathscr{T}
.

(c) Suppose that
n_{X}\geq 3
. Then the following conditions hold:

•

The first and the second conditions of (b) hold.

•

\mathscr{P}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{P_{v}\}_{v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-1\}}
such that
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\subseteq\bigcup_{v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-1\}}P_{v}
.

•

When
C_{1}=\emptyset
, we have
\#(P_{2}\cap T)=1
and
P_{v}\cap T=\emptyset
for all
T\in\mathscr{T}
and all
v\neq 2
.

•

When
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
, we have
\#(C_{1}\cap T)=1
,
\#(C_{1}\cap P_{2})=1
,
C_{1}\cap P_{v}=\emptyset
, and
P_{v}\cap T=\emptyset
for all
T\in\mathscr{T}
and all
v\neq 2
.

•

If
n_{X}\geq 4
, for each
v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-2\}
, we have
\#(P_{v}\cap P_{v+1})=1
and
P_{v}\cap P_{v^{\prime}}=\emptyset
when
v^{\prime}\not\in\{v-1,v,v+1\}
.

•

If
n_{X}=3
, we have
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\cap P_{2}=\{e_{s,1},e_{s,2},e_{s,3}\}
.

•

If
n_{X}=4
, we have
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\cap P_{2}=\{e_{s,1},e_{s,2}\}
and
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\cap P_{3}=\{e_{s,3},e_{s,4}\}
.

•

If
n_{X}\geq 5
, we have
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\cap P_{2}=\{e_{s,1},e_{s,2}\}
,
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\cap P_{n_{X}-1}=\{e_{s,n_{X}-1},e_{s,n_{X}}\}
, and
D_{\mathcal{X}_{s}}\cap P_{v}=\{e_{s,v}\}
,
v\in\{3,\dots,n_{X}-2\}
.

Note that since generic curves admit all degeneration types, we have that
X^{\bullet}
admits a (DEG) when
X^{\bullet}
is a component-generic pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})\neq(1,1)
.

4.3.3.

Next, we give some examples to explain the degeneration data introduced above. For simplicity, we assume that
\#(X^{\rm sing})=2
,
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
, and
n_{X}\neq 0
.

Example 4.4
.

We use the notation “
\bullet
” and “
\circ
with a line segment” to denote a vertex and an open edge, respectively. Moreover, we use
v_{(-)}
to denote the vertex corresponding to the irreducible component
(-)
.

(a) If
n_{X}=1
, then the dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}
of
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}
is as follows (see
4.3.2
(iii)-(a)):

:

(b) If
n_{X}=2
, then the dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}
of
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}
is as follows (see
4.3.2
(iii)-(b)):

:

(c) If
n_{X}=5
, then the dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}
of
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}
is as follows (see
4.3.2
(iii)-(c)):

:

4.3.4.

The main result of the present subsection is as follows:

Proposition 4.5
.

Let
X^{\bullet}=(X,D_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{X}}\})
be an irreducible component-generic pointed stable curve over
k
,
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
, and
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
. Suppose that
{\rm deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n
(i.e.,
s(D)=n_{X}-1
) if
n_{X}\neq 0
, and that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})={\rm deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)n
for all
i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}.
Then
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum. Namely, the following holds:

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Let
f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}=(Y,D_{Y})\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
be the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
\alpha
. Firstly, we note that, to verify the proposition, we may assume that
Y^{\bullet}
is connected.

Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
, and that
n_{X}\leq 1
. Then the proposition follows immediately from Proposition
3.11
(i). Thus, to verify the proposition, it is sufficient to assume that one of the following conditions holds: (1)
\#(X^{\rm sing})\geq 1
and
n_{X}\leq 1
; (2)
n_{X}\geq 2
.

Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is a singular curve of type
(1,1)
(i.e.,
\#(X^{\rm sing})=1
). Since
f^{\bullet}
is étale, the proposition follows immediately from Lemma
3.1
(i). Thus, to verify the proposition, we may assume
(g_{X},n_{X})\neq(1,1)
. Now, we can use the degeneration data introduced in
4.3.2
.

Since
X^{\bullet}
is a component-generic pointed stable curve,
X^{\bullet}
admits a (DEG). We maintain the notation introduced in
4.3.2
. Furthermore, we write
Q_{\overline{\eta}}
(resp.
Q_{s}
) for the effective divisor on
\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}}
(resp.
\mathcal{X}_{s}
) induced by
D
and
\alpha_{\overline{\eta}}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{Q_{\overline{\eta}}}(\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}})
for the element induced by
\alpha
. Then we have
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma_{(\alpha_{\overline{\eta}},Q_{\overline{\eta}})}.
Write
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}}
and
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}
for the admissible fundamental groups of
\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}}
and
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}
, respectively. Then we have a specialization surjective homomorphism

sp_{R}:\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}.

We suppose that
X^{\bullet}
satisfies (DEG)-(iii)-(c) (
4.3.2
). Moreover, we suppose that
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
and
n_{X}\geq 5
(see Example
4.4
(c)).

Step 1:
We define certain smooth pointed stable curves associated to irreducible components of
\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{s}
.

We write
y_{v,v+1},\ z_{v,v+1}
,
v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-2\}
, for the inverse image of
P_{v}\cap P_{v+1}
of the natural closed immersion
P_{v}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{s}
and the inverse image of
P_{v}\cap P_{v+1}
of the natural closed immersion
P_{v+1}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{s}
, respectively. We define

P_{2}^{\bullet}=(P_{2},D_{P_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{e_{s,1},e_{s,2},y_{2,3}\}\cup(C_{1}\cap P_{2})),

P_{n_{X}-1}^{\bullet}=(P_{n_{X}-1},D_{P_{n_{X}-1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{z_{n_{X}-2,n_{X}-1},e_{s,n_{X}-1},e_{s,n_{X}}\}),

P_{v}^{\bullet}=(P_{v},D_{P_{v}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{z_{v-1,v},e_{s,v},y_{v,v+1}\}),\ v\in\{3,\dots,n_{X}-2\},

to be smooth pointed stable curves of types
(0,4)
,
(0,3)
, and
(0,3)
over
k_{R}
, respectively. Moreover, we define

C_{1}^{\bullet}=(C_{1},D_{C_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(C_{1}\cap P_{2})\cup((\bigcup_{T\in\mathscr{T}}T)\cap C_{1})),

T^{\bullet}=(T,D_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}T\cap C_{1}),\ T\in\mathscr{T},

to be a smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing}),1+\#(X^{\rm sing}))
and a singular pointed stable curve of type
(1,1)
over
k_{R}
, respectively. Note that since
C_{1}
is generic, we have
\sigma_{C_{1}}=g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing})
(i.e.,
C_{1}^{\bullet}
is ordinary (
2.2.2
)).

Step 2:
We construct a Galois admissible covering of
\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}
by using specialization isomorphisms of maximal prime-to-
p
quotients of admissible fundamental groups.

Let
f^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f^{\bullet}\times_{k}\overline{K}_{R}:\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}=(\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}},D_{\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}Y^{\bullet}\times_{k}\overline{K}_{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}
be the Galois admissible covering over
\overline{K}_{R}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
f^{\bullet}
, and
\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}}\subseteq\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}}\cong\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
the admissible fundamental group of
\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}
. By the specialization theorem of maximal prime-to-
p
quotients of admissible fundamental groups (
[
V
, Théorème 2.2 (c)]
), we have

sp^{p^{\prime}}_{R}:\Pi_{\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}}}^{p^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Pi_{\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{s}}^{p^{\prime}}.

Then we obtain a normal open subgroup
\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}^{\bullet}}^{p^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}sp^{p^{\prime}}_{R}(\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}}^{\bullet}}^{p^{\prime}})\subseteq\Pi_{\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{s}}^{p^{\prime}}
. Write
\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}^{\bullet}}\subseteq\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}
for the inverse image of
\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}^{\bullet}}^{p^{\prime}}
of the natural surjection
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}^{p^{\prime}}
. Then
\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}^{\bullet}}
determines a Galois admissible covering

f^{\bullet}_{s}:\mathcal{Y}_{s}^{\bullet}=(\mathcal{Y}_{s},D_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}})\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}

over
k_{R}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. We denote by
\alpha_{s}\in\text{Rev}_{Q_{s}}^{\rm adm}(\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet})
the element induced by the composition homomorphism
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{s}}^{p^{\prime},\rm ab}\overset{(sp_{R}^{p^{\prime},\rm ab})^{-1}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}}\Pi_{\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}}}^{p^{\prime},\rm ab}\overset{\alpha_{\overline{\eta}}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.

Step 3:
We compute the generalized Hasse-Witt invariant
\gamma_{(\alpha_{s},D_{\alpha_{s}})}
by applying Theorem
3.9
, Lemma
4.2
, and Lemma
4.3
.

The structure of
\Pi_{\mathcal{X}_{s}^{\bullet}}^{p^{\prime}}
(
2.1.7
) implies that
f_{s}
is étale over
(\bigcup_{T\in\mathscr{T}}T)\cap C_{1}
. Then we obtain that
f_{s}
is étale over
C_{1}\cap P_{2}
. Thus,
f_{s}
is étale over
D_{C_{1}}
. Let
Y_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f^{-1}_{s}(P_{v})
,
v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-1\}
. We put

Y_{v}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(Y_{v},D_{Y_{v}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f^{-1}_{s}(D_{P_{v}})),\ v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-1\}.

Then
f^{\bullet}_{s}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{v}^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}_{v}\rightarrow P_{v}^{\bullet},\ v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-1\},

over
k_{R}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. We maintain the notation introduced in Lemma
4.2
. Then we see that the ramification divisor on
P_{v}
,
v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-1\}
, induced by
f_{v}^{\bullet}
and
\alpha_{s}
is as follows:

Q_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}d_{x_{1}}e_{s,1}+d_{x_{2}}e_{s,2}+a_{2,3}y_{2,3},

Q_{n_{X}-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b_{n_{X}-2,n_{X}-1}z_{n_{X}-2,n_{X}-1}+d_{x_{n_{X}-1}}e_{s,n_{X}-1}+d_{x_{n_{X}}}e_{s,n_{X}},

Q_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b_{v-1,v}z_{v-1,v}+d_{x_{v}}e_{s,v}+a_{v,v+1}y_{v,v+1},\ v\in\{3,\dots,n_{X}-2\}.

Since
f_{s}
is étale over
C_{1}\cap P_{2}
, we see that
f_{v}^{\bullet},\ v\in\{2,\dots,n_{X}-1\},
induces a pair
([\mathcal{L}_{v}],Q_{v})\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{P_{v}^{\bullet},n}
. Moreover, the
k_{R}[\mu_{n}]
-module
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y_{v},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}
admits the following canonical decomposition

H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Y_{v},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{Y_{v}}(j),

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts on
M_{Y_{v}}(j)
as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication. On the other hand, lemma
4.2
implies
\text{deg}(Q_{v}^{(i)})=\text{deg}(Q_{v})=2n
,
i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}
. Then by applying Lemma
4.3
, we have

\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}_{v}],Q_{v})}=\text{dim}_{k_{R}}(M_{Y_{v}}(1))=\text{dim}_{k_{R}}(H^{1}(P_{v},\mathcal{L}_{v}))=1.

Let
Z_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f^{-1}_{s}(C_{1})
. Then
f^{\bullet}_{s}
induces a Galois étale covering (not necessarily connected)

f^{\bullet}_{C_{1}}:Z^{\bullet}_{1}=(Z_{1},D_{Z_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f^{-1}_{s}(D_{C_{1}}))\rightarrow C_{1}^{\bullet}

over
k_{R}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Write
\alpha_{C_{1}}\in\text{Rev}_{0}^{\rm adm}(C_{1}^{\bullet})
for the element induced by
f_{C_{1}}^{\bullet}
and
\alpha_{s}
. The
k_{R}[\mu_{n}]
-module
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Z_{1},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}
admits the following canonical decomposition

H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(Z_{1},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{Z_{1}}(j),

where
\zeta\in\mu_{n}
acts on
M_{Z_{1}}(j)
as the
\zeta^{j}
-multiplication. Then Proposition
3.11
(i) implies

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha_{C_{1}},0)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing}),&\text{if}\ \alpha_{C_{1}}=0,\\
g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing})-1,&\text{if}\ \alpha_{C_{1}}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Let
V_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f^{-1}_{s}(T)
,
T\in\mathscr{T}
, and
\widetilde{T}
the smooth compactification of
U_{T}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}T\setminus T^{\rm sing}
. Then
f^{\bullet}_{s}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{T}^{\bullet}:V_{T}^{\bullet}=(V_{T},D_{V_{T}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}f_{s}^{-1}(D_{T}))\rightarrow T^{\bullet}

over
k_{R}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Then
f_{T}^{\bullet}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{\widetilde{T}}^{\bullet}:V^{\bullet}_{\widetilde{T}}=(V_{\widetilde{T}},D_{V_{\widetilde{T}}})\rightarrow\widetilde{T}^{\bullet}

over
k_{R}
. Write
\alpha_{\widetilde{T}}\in\text{Rev}_{0}(\widetilde{T}^{\bullet})
for the element induced by
f_{\widetilde{T}}^{\bullet}
and
\alpha_{s}
. By using the proposition of the case where
X^{\bullet}
is a singular curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})=(1,1)
, we obtain that
\gamma_{(\alpha_{\widetilde{T}},0)}=0
. Thus, Theorem
3.9
implies that

\gamma_{(\alpha_{s},Q_{s})}=g_{X}+n_{X}-2.

Step 4:
We compute
\gamma_{(\alpha_{\overline{\eta}},Q_{\overline{\eta}})}
by using specialization surjective homomorphisms of admissible fundamental groups.

The
k_{R}[\mu_{n}]
-modules
H^{1}_{\rm\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}
and
H^{1}_{\rm\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{Y}_{s},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}
admit the following canonical decompositions

H^{1}_{\rm\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}}}(j),

H^{1}_{\rm\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{Y}_{s},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}=\bigoplus_{j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}M_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}}(j),

respectively. Moreover, we have an injection as
k_{R}[\mu_{n}]
-modules

H^{1}_{\rm\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{Y}_{s},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}\hookrightarrow H^{1}_{\rm\text{\'{e}t}}(\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}},\mathbb{F}_{p})\otimes k_{R}

induced by the specialization map
\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}^{\bullet}_{\overline{\eta}}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}^{\bullet}}
. Thus, we have

g_{X}+n_{X}-2=\gamma_{(\alpha_{s},Q_{s})}=\text{dim}_{k_{R}}(M_{\mathcal{Y}_{s}}(1))

\leq\gamma_{(\alpha_{\overline{\eta}},Q_{\overline{\eta}})}=\text{dim}_{k_{R}}(M_{\mathcal{Y}_{\overline{\eta}}}(1))\leq g_{X}+n_{X}-2.

This means
\gamma_{(\alpha_{\overline{\eta}},Q_{\overline{\eta}})}=g_{X}+n_{X}-2.
We complete the proof of the proposition when
X^{\bullet}
satisfies (DEG)-(iii)-(c),
C_{1}\neq\emptyset
, and
n_{X}\geq 5
.

By applying similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof above, one can prove the proposition when
X^{\bullet}
satisfies (DEG)-(iii)-(c) and either
C_{1}=\emptyset
or
n_{X}\leq 4
holds. Moreover, similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof above imply that the proposition holds when
X^{\bullet}
satisfies either (DEG)-(iii)-(a) or (DEG)-(iii)-(b). We complete the proof of the proposition.
∎

In the remainder of this section, under certain assumptions, we generalize Proposition
4.5
to the case where
X^{\bullet}
is not necessary to be irreducible.

either (DEG)-(v) or (DEG)-(vi). We complete the proof of the proposition.

4.3.5.

By applying Proposition
4.5
, we solve Problem
2.11
under certain assumptions as follows:

Corollary 4.6
.

Let
X^{\bullet}=(X,D_{X})
be a smooth component-generic pointed stable curve over
k
and
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
. Suppose that
s(D)=n_{X}-1
if
n_{X}\neq 0
, and that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D),\ i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}.
Then the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor
\Theta_{\mathcal{E}_{D}}
associated to
\mathcal{E}_{D}
exists.

Proof.

Since
X^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
, the corollary follows immediately from Proposition
4.5
and Remark
2.7.2
.
∎

4.4.
Minimal quasi-trees

In this subsection, we introduce the so-called
minimal quasi-trees
which play an important role in the remainder of the present paper.

4.4.1.
Settings

Let
W^{\bullet}=(W,D_{W})
be a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{W},n_{W})
over
k
and
\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}}
the dual semi-graph of
W^{\bullet}
.

4.4.2.

Let
Z^{\bullet}
be a pointed sable curve over
k
,
\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}}
the dual semi-graph of
Z^{\bullet}
such that
\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}}\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}})
is a
tree
(see
2.1.1
for
e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}})
), and that
E_{Z}\subseteq e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}})
is a subset of open edges.

We put

V_{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{v\in v(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}})\ |\ \#(e^{\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}}}(v)\cap(e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}})\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}})))=1,\ Z_{v}\cap E_{Z}=\emptyset\}\subseteq v(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}}),

where
Z_{v}
denotes the irreducible component corresponding to
v
. We call
V_{Z}

the set of terminal vertices avoiding to
E_{Z}
.

For instance, in Example
4.9
(b) below, we have
Z^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}W_{1}^{\bullet}
,
E_{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{Z^{\bullet}})=e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{W_{1}^{\bullet}})
, and
V_{Z}=\{v_{3}\}
.

4.4.3.
Constructions of minimal quasi-trees associated to
D_{W}

Next, we define a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{W}
. Let
E\subseteq e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})
be a (possibly empty) subset of closed edges such that
\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}}\setminus(E\cup e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}}))
is a connected tree. Note that it is easy to see that
E
exists.

Write
N
for the set of nodes of
W
corresponding to the closed edges which are contained in
E
, and write
\text{norm}_{N}:W_{N}\rightarrow W
for the normalization morphism of the underlying curve
W
over
N
. We define a pointed stable curve over
k
to be

W_{1}^{\bullet}=(W_{1},D_{W_{1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}W_{N}^{\bullet}=(W_{N},D_{W_{N}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{norm}_{N}^{-1}(D_{W}\cup N)).

Note that the above construction implies
D_{W}\subseteq D_{W_{1}}
(i.e.,
e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})\subseteq e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{W_{1}^{\bullet}})
). Write
\Gamma_{W_{1}^{\bullet}}
for the dual semi-graph of
W_{1}^{\bullet}
. Then the construction of
W_{1}^{\bullet}
implies that
\Gamma_{W_{1}^{\bullet}}\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{W_{1}^{\bullet}})
is a
tree
.

Suppose that
n_{W}\neq 0
. Then for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, if
W_{i}^{\bullet}
has already been defined, we may define
W_{i+1}^{\bullet}
as follows: Let
V_{i}\subseteq v(\Gamma_{W_{i}^{\bullet}})
be the subset of terminal vertices avoiding to
e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})\subseteq e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{W_{i}^{\bullet}})
. If
V_{i}=\emptyset
, then we put
W^{\bullet}_{i+1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}W^{\bullet}_{i}
. If
V_{i}\neq\emptyset
, we write
W_{i+1}
for the topological closure of

W_{i}\setminus(\bigcup_{v\in V_{i}}W_{v})

in
W_{i}
. Note that the definition of
V_{i}
implies that
W_{i+1}
is connected, and that
D_{W}
is contained in
W_{i+1}
. Then we define a pointed stable curve over
k
to be

W_{i+1}^{\bullet}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(W_{i+1},D_{W_{i+1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(D_{W_{i}}\cap W_{i+1})\cup((\bigcup_{v\in V_{i}}W_{v})\cap W_{i+1}))).

Note that we have
e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})\subseteq e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{W_{i+1}^{\bullet}})
.

Let
i_{0}
be the minimal natural number such that
V_{i_{0}}=\emptyset
. Note that the above construction implies that
W_{i_{0}}^{\bullet}=W_{j}^{\bullet}
for all
j\geq i_{0}
. We put

W^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}=(W_{\Gamma},D_{W_{\Gamma}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}W_{i_{0}}^{\bullet},\ \Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma_{W_{i_{0}}^{\bullet}}.

Then we have that
D_{W}\subseteq D_{W_{\Gamma}}
, and that
\Gamma\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma)
is a
tree
. Moreover, we see
v(\Gamma)\subseteq v(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})
and
e(\Gamma)\subseteq e(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})
, where
e(-)
denotes the set of edges of
(-)
(
2.1.1
). Note that an open edge of
\Gamma
is
not
an open edge of
\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}}
in general, and that a closed edge of
\Gamma
is a closed edge of
\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}}
.

Moreover, the above constructions imply the following:

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
W^{\bullet}=(W,D_{W})
be a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{W},n_{W})
over
k
. Suppose that
n_{W}\neq 0
. We denote by
\mathscr{Q}_{W^{\bullet}}
the set of
\Gamma
constructed as above. Then we have
\mathscr{Q}_{W^{\bullet}}\neq\emptyset.

Note that the construction of
\Gamma

depends
on the choice of
E
(i.e., a subset of closed edges of
e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}})
such that
\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}}\setminus(E\cup e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{W^{\bullet}}))
is a tree).

Definition 4.8
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. We shall say that a semi-graph

\Gamma_{D_{W}}

is
a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{W}
if
\Gamma_{D_{W}}=\Gamma
for some
\Gamma
constructed above when
n_{W}\neq 0
, and
\Gamma_{D_{W}}=\emptyset
when
n_{W}=0
.

. Let us give some examples to explain the above constructions.
