4.5.
The first main theorem

We are going to prove the first main theorem of the present paper.

4.5.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
and the assumption introduced in
2.3.3
(i.e.,
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
). Moreover, we assume that
X^{\bullet}
is a component-generic pointed stable curve over
k
.

4.5.2.

We have the following key proposition.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
be an effective divisor on
X
such that
s(D)=n_{X}-1
if
n_{X}\neq 0
, and that
{\rm deg}(D^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D),\ i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}.
Then the following statements hold:

(i) Suppose that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

•

n_{X}=0
and
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
is a tree.

•

n_{X}\neq 0
and
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}=\Gamma_{D_{W}}
is a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
(see Definition
4.8
).

Then
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum for all
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
. Namely, the following holds:

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

(ii) There exists an element
\beta\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
such that
\gamma_{(\beta,D)}
attains maximum. Namely, the following holds:

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\beta,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

(i) Let
f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}=(Y,D_{Y})\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
be a Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
\alpha
. To verify (i), we may assume that
Y^{\bullet}
is connected.

Suppose that
n_{X}=0
. Since
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
is a tree. We see immediately that
f
is étale. Then (i) follows from Theorem
3.9
and Proposition
4.5
.

Suppose that
n_{X}\neq 0
. Let
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
and
\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{v}})
the set of connected components of
\overline{X\setminus X_{v}}
, where
\overline{X\setminus X_{v}}
denotes the topological closure of
{X\setminus X_{v}}
in
X
. We put

D_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(D_{X}\cap X_{v})\cup(\bigcup_{C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{v}})}(C\cap X_{v})).

Let
X_{v}^{\bullet}=(X_{v},D_{X_{v}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{v})
,
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}),
be a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X_{v}},n_{X_{v}})
over
k
. Then
f^{\bullet}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{v}^{\bullet}:Y_{v}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{v}^{\bullet},\ v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}),

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.

Note that since
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
, we have that
C\cap X_{v}=\{x_{C}\}
is a closed point of
X
for all
C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{v}})
. Moreover, since
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
is a tree, the ramification divisor

Q_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{x\in D_{X}\cap X_{v}}\text{ord}_{x}(Q_{v})x+\sum_{C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{v}})}\text{ord}_{x_{C}}(Q_{v})x_{C}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{v}]^{0},\ v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}),

on
X_{v}
induced by
f_{v}
satisfies the following:

\text{ord}_{x}(Q_{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x}(D),\ x\in D_{X}\cap X_{v},

\text{ord}_{x_{C}}(Q_{v})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}[\sum_{c\in D_{X}\cap C}\text{ord}_{c}(D)],\ C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{v}}),

where
[(-)]
denotes the integer which is equal to the image of
(-)
in
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
when we identify
\{0,\dots,n-1\}
with
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
naturally. By applying similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of Lemma
4.2
, we have

\text{deg}(Q_{v})=(\#(D_{v})-1)n\ \text{and}\ \text{deg}(Q_{v}^{(i)})=\text{deg}(Q_{v}),\ i\in\{0,\dots,t-1\}.

Let
\Pi_{X_{v}^{\bullet}}
,
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, be the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}_{v}
. Write
\alpha_{v}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{Q_{v}}(X_{v}^{\bullet})
for the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi_{X_{v}^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Note that
\alpha_{v}\neq 0
for all
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
. Then Proposition
4.5
implies

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha_{v},Q_{v})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X_{v}}-1,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(Q_{v})=\emptyset,\\
g_{X_{v}}+s(Q_{v})-2,&\text{if}\ \text{Supp}(Q_{v})\neq\emptyset.\end{array}\right.

Thus, Theorem
3.9
implies that

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

This completes the proof of (i).

(ii) Suppose that
n_{X}\leq 1
. (ii) follows from Proposition
3.11
(ii). Then to verify (ii), we may assume that
n_{X}\geq 2
.

Let
\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma_{D_{X}}
be a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
,
\Gamma^{\rm im}
the image of the natural morphism
\phi_{\Gamma}:\Gamma\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
, and

X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}=(X_{\Gamma},D_{X_{\Gamma}}),\ X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}=(X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}},D_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})

the pointed stable curves over
k
associated to
\Gamma,\ \Gamma^{\rm im}
, respectively (
4.4.3
,
4.4.5
). Note that
D
is also an effective divisor on
X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
.

Write
D_{\Gamma}
for the pulling back divisor
\text{norm}_{\Gamma}^{*}(D)
(see
4.4.5
for the definition of
\text{norm}_{\Gamma}
). Let
\alpha_{\Gamma}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D_{\Gamma}}(X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma})
be an arbitrary element such that
\alpha_{\Gamma}\neq 0
. Then similar arguments to the arguments given in (i) imply

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma},D_{\Gamma})}=g_{X_{\Gamma}}+n_{X}-2
, where
g_{X_{\Gamma}}
denotes the genus of
X_{\Gamma}
. We denote by

g_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}:Z_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}

the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
\alpha_{\Gamma}
.
By gluing
Z_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
along
g_{\Gamma}^{-1}(D_{X_{\Gamma}}\setminus\text{norm}_{\Gamma}^{-1}(D_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}))
in a way that is compatible with the gluing of
X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
that gives rise to
X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}
, we obtain a pointed stable curve
Z_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}
over
k
. Moreover,
g_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

g_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}:Z_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}}
,
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
be the admissible fundamental groups of
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
,
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
, respectively. Write
\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
for an element of
\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
induced by
g^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
such that the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}}^{\rm ab}\rightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}^{\rm ab}\overset{\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is equal to
\alpha_{\Gamma}
. We put
D_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
. Then Theorem
3.9
implies that

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}},D_{\Gamma^{\rm im}})}=g_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}+n_{X}-2,

where
g_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
denotes the genus of
X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
.

On the other hand, we write
\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})
for the set of connected components of
\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
, where
\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
denotes the topological closure of
X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
in
X
. We define the following pointed stable curve

C^{\bullet}=(C,D_{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}C\cap X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}),\ C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}),

over
k
. Note that since
X^{\bullet}
is component-generic, we have that
C^{\bullet}
is also component-generic. Then the
p
-rank
\sigma_{C}
is equal to the genus of
C^{\bullet}
.

Let
C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})
. We put

Z^{\bullet}_{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{i\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}}C_{i}^{\bullet},

where
C_{i}^{\bullet}
is a copy of
C^{\bullet}
, and
\sqcup
means a disjoint union. Then we obtain a Galois multi-admissible covering

g_{C}^{\bullet}:Z^{\bullet}_{C}\rightarrow C^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, where the restriction morphism
g_{C}^{\bullet}|_{C^{\bullet}_{i}}
is an identity, and the Galois action is
j(C_{i})=C_{i+j}
for all
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. By gluing
Z_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}
and
\{Z_{C}^{\bullet}\}_{C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}
along
g^{-1}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}(X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}\cap(\bigcup_{C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}C))
and
\{g^{-1}_{C}(X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}\cap C)\}_{C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}
in a way that is compatible with the gluing of
\{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}\}\cup\{C^{\bullet}\}_{C\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}
that gives rise to
X^{\bullet}
, we obtain a Galois multi-admissible covering

g^{\bullet}:Z^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
be the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
. Moreover, we write
\beta\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D}(X^{\bullet})
for an element induced by
g^{\bullet}
such that the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}^{\rm ab}\rightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\overset{\beta}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is equal to
\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
. By applying Theorem
3.9
, we see

\gamma_{(\beta,D)}=\gamma_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm max}=g_{X}+n_{X}-2.

We complete the proof of (ii).
∎

4.5.3.

Now, the main result of the present section is as follows.

Theorem 4.12
.

Let
X^{\bullet}=(X,D_{X})
be a component-generic pointed stable curve over
k
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be an arbitrary positive natural number prime to
p
and
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
(
2.2.5
). Let
t\in\mathbb{N}
be a positive natural number such that
p^{t}=1
in
(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. Write
n
for
p^{t}-1
,
m^{\prime}
for
n/m
, and
D^{\prime}
for the divisor
m^{\prime}D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
when we identify
\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}
with the unique subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
of order
m
. Then the following statements hold:

(i) Suppose that one of the following conditions is satisfied:

•

n_{X}=0
and
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
is a tree.

•

n_{X}\neq 0
and
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}=\Gamma_{D_{W}}
is a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
(
4.4.3
).

Then the following are equivalent:

(a)
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum for all
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
(Definition
2.4
(i) and Definition
3.10
(ii)). Namely, the following holds (see Definition
3.10
(i) for
\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}
):

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

(b) We have (see
2.2.5
and Definition
2.4
for
(D^{\prime})^{(i)}
)

\displaystyle s(D)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
n_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0\end{array}\right.

and
{\rm deg}((D^{\prime})^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D^{\prime}),\ i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}
.

(ii) The following are equivalent:

(a) There exists an element
\beta\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
such that
\gamma_{(\beta,D)}
attains maximum. Namely, the following holds:

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\beta,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

(b) We have

\displaystyle s(D)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
n_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0,\end{array}\right.

and
{\rm deg}((D^{\prime})^{(i)})\geq{\rm deg}(D^{\prime}),\ i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}
.

Proof.

(i) Write
\alpha^{\prime}\in\text{Rev}_{D^{\prime}}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
for the element induced by
\alpha
. Then we have
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma_{(\alpha^{\prime},D^{\prime})}
. The “only if” part of (i) follows from Lemma
4.1
. Moreover, the “if” part of (i) follows immediately from Proposition
4.11
(i).

(ii) The “only if” part of (ii) follows from Lemma
4.1
. We prove the “if” part of (ii). Let
\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma_{D_{X}}
be a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
,
\Gamma^{\rm im}
the image of the natural morphism
\phi_{\Gamma}:\Gamma\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
,
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}=(X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}},D_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})
the pointed stable curve over
k
associated to
\Gamma^{\rm im}
(
4.4.5
), and
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
. Note that
D
is also an effective divisor on
X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
. Let
\beta_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
be an arbitrary element of
\text{Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}})\setminus\{0\}
. Write
\beta_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\prime}\in\text{Rev}_{D^{\prime}}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}})
for the element induced by
\beta_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
.

Let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
be the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
. Proposition
4.11
(ii) implies that there exists
\beta^{\prime}\in\text{Rev}_{D^{\prime}}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
such that the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\rightarrow\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}\overset{\beta^{\prime}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is equal to
\beta_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\prime}
, and that
\gamma_{(\beta^{\prime},D^{\prime})}
attains maximum. Moreover, the construction of
\beta^{\prime}
given in the proof of Proposition
4.11
(ii) (i.e., the Galois multi-admissible covering of
\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}
,
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\setminus v(\Gamma^{\rm im})
, induced by
\beta^{\prime}
is trivial) implies that
\beta^{\prime}
can be induced by an element of
\beta\in\text{Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})
. Then
\gamma_{(\beta,D)}=\gamma_{(\beta^{\prime},D^{\prime})}
attains maximum. This completes the proof of (ii).
∎

4.6.
(m,n_{X})
-ordinary curves

4.6.1.

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
. Moreover, let
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
be the moduli stack parameterizing pointed stable curves of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
(
2.1.4
) and
\mathcal{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}\subseteq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
the open substack parameterizing smooth pointed stable curves of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
. We denote by
\overline{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
,
M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
the coarse moduli spaces of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
,
\mathcal{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}
, respectively.

4.6.2.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be an arbitrary positive natural number prime to
p
, and let
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
be an effective divisor on
X
such that
\text{deg}(D)=(n_{X}-1)m
if
n_{X}\neq 0
.

Definition 4.13
.

We shall say that
X^{\bullet}
is
(m,n_{X})
-ordinary
if, for
all

\alpha\in{\rm Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
, we have

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Note that, if
n_{X}=0
and
X^{\bullet}
is non-singular, then the definition of
(m,n_{X})
-ordinary coincides with the definition of
m
-ordinary
introduced by Nakajima (
[
N
, §4]
).

On the other hand, let
t\in\mathbb{N}
be a positive natural number such that
p^{t}=1
in
(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. Write
n
for
p^{t}-1
,
m^{\prime}
for
n/m
, and
D^{\prime}
for the divisor
m^{\prime}D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
when we identify
\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}
with the unique subgroup of
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
of order
m
. If
n_{X}\neq 0
and
X^{\bullet}
is
(m,n_{X})
-ordinary, then Lemma
4.1
(ii) implies that

{\rm deg}((D^{\prime})^{(i)})={\rm deg}(D^{\prime})=(n_{X}-1)n,\ i\in\{0,1,\dots,t-1\}.

4.6.3.

We denote by

\overline{U}_{(m,n_{X})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{q\in\overline{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}\ |\ \text{curves corresponding to}\ q\ \text{is}\ (m,n_{X})\text{-ordinary}\}\subseteq\overline{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}.

Moreover, we put
U_{(m,n_{X})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\overline{U}_{(m,n_{X})}\cap M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
(i.e., the set of points corresponding to
smooth

(m,n_{X})
-ordinary curves). Then we have the following result.

Proposition 4.14
.

(i) The set
U_{(m,n_{X})}
is a non-empty open subset of
M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
.

(ii) Suppose that
n_{X}\leq 1
. Then we have

M_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\rm cl}\cap(\bigcap_{m\in\mathbb{N}\ {\text{\rm s.t.}}\ (m,p)=1}U_{(m,n_{X})})=\emptyset,

where
M_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\rm cl}
denotes the set of closed points of
M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
.

(iii) Let
q\in M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
be an arbitrary point. We denote by
\Pi_{q}
the admissible fundamental group of a smooth pointed stable curve corresponding to a geometric point over
q
. Note that the isomorphism class of
\Pi_{q}
(as a profinite group) does not depend on the choices of geometric points over
q
. Suppose that
n_{X}\leq 1
. Let
U\subseteq M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
be an arbitrary non-empty open subset. Then there exist closed points
q_{1},q_{2}\in U\cap M_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\rm cl}
such that
\Pi_{q_{1}}\not\cong\Pi_{q_{2}}
.

Proof.

(i) By applying similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of
[
N
, Theorem 2]
, (i) follows from Theorem
4.12
(i).

(ii) Suppose that
k=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, and that
X^{\bullet}
is a smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over
k
. To verify (ii), we only need to prove that, if
n_{X}\leq 1
,
X^{\bullet}
is not
(m,n_{X})
-ordinary for some positive natural number
m\in\mathbb{N}
prime to
p
.

Since
n_{X}\leq 1
, we have
(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}=\{0\}
. We denote by
\Theta_{X}
the Raynaud-Tamagawa divisor associated to
\mathcal{B}^{1}_{0}
(
2.4.2
,
2.4.3
) and by
\Theta^{\prime}
an arbitrary irreducible component of
\Theta_{X}
. Write
J_{X}^{1}
for the pulling back of the Jacobian
J_{X}
of
X
by the absolute Frobenius morphism
F_{k}
of
k
. If
X^{\bullet}
is
(m,n_{X})
-ordinary for all prime-to-
p
positive natural numbers, then a property of Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisors concerning new-ordinary coverings (e.g.
[
PS
, §1.2 and §1.3]
) implies

J^{1}_{X}\{p^{\prime}\}\cap\Theta_{X}(k)\subseteq\{0_{J^{1}_{X}}\},

where
0_{J^{1}_{X}}
denotes the zero point of
J^{1}_{X}
, and
J^{1}_{X}\{p^{\prime}\}
denotes the set of prime-to-
p
torsion points of
J^{1}_{X}(k)
. Since
\text{dim}(\Theta^{\prime})>0
, we have

\Theta^{\prime}\{p^{\prime}\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}J^{1}_{X}\{p^{\prime}\}\cap\Theta^{\prime}(k)\subseteq J^{1}_{X}\{p^{\prime}\}\cap\Theta_{X}(k)

is not dense in
\Theta^{\prime}
.

On the other hand, since
\Theta^{\prime}
is defined over
k=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
, by applying a result of Anderson-Indik (
[
T3
, §5]
), we have that
\Theta^{\prime}
is a subvariety of a translate of a proper sub-abelian variety of
J^{1}_{X}
. But this contradicts a result of Raynaud (
[
R2
, Proposition 1.2.1]
) which says that there exists an irreducible component
\Theta^{\prime}
of
\Theta_{X}
such that
\Theta^{\prime}
is not contained in a translate of a proper sub-abelian variety of
J^{1}_{X}
. This completes the proof of (ii).

(iii) Let
q
be an arbitrary closed point of
U
and
q^{\rm gen}
the generic point of
M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
. Suppose that (iii) does not hold. Thus there exists a closed point
q\in U^{\rm cl}
such that
\Pi_{q^{\rm gen}}\cong\Pi_{q}
. Then there exist a discrete valuation ring
R
and a morphism
c_{R}:\mathrm{Spec}\,R\rightarrow M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
such that
c_{R}(\eta_{R})=q^{\rm gen}
and
c_{R}(s_{R})=q
, where
\eta_{R}
is the generic point of
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
and
s_{R}
is the closed point of
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
. By replacing
R
by a finite extension of
R
, we have a smooth pointed stable curve
\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}
of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over
\mathrm{Spec}\,R
determined by
c_{R}
. Moreover, we obtain a specialization surjective homomorphism

sp_{R}:\Pi_{q^{\rm gen}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{q}.

Since
\Pi_{q^{\rm gen}}
and
\Pi_{q}
are topologically finitely generated,
sp_{R}
is an isomorphism.

On the other hand, (ii) implies that there exist a positive integer
m
prime to
p
and a connected Galois étale covering
\mathcal{Y}^{\bullet}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}^{\bullet}
over
R
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}
such that the geometric generic fiber of
\mathcal{Y}
is ordinary and the geometric special fiber of
\mathcal{Y}
is not ordinary. Namely,
sp_{R}
is not an isomorphism. We complete the proof of (iii).
∎

Remark 4.14.1
.

Proposition
4.14
(iii) gives an answer of a question of D. Harbater (
[
H
, 4.2]
) which was first solved by Pop and Sa
\rm\ddot{\char 16\relax}
di (
[
PS
, Corollary]
). In
[
PS
]
, Pop and Sa
\rm\ddot{\char 16\relax}
di proved a result which says that specialization homomorphisms of geometric étale fundamental groups of smooth projective curves in positive characteristic is not an isomorphism under certain assumptions. Then together with a result of C-L. Chai and F. Oort, and a result of J-P. Serre, they obtained Proposition
4.14
(iii).

4.6.4.

If
n_{X}\leq 1
, then Proposition
4.14
(i) was proved by Nakajima (
[
N
, Theorem 2]
). Then Proposition
4.14
(i) is a generalized version of Nakajima’s result to the case of admissible coverings of smooth pointed stable curves. Moreover, Nakajima (
[
N
, §4 Remark]
) asked whether or not

\bigcap_{m\in\mathbb{N}\ {\text{\rm s.t.}}\ (m,p)=1}U_{(m,n_{X})}

is a non-empty open subset of
M_{g_{X},n_{X}}
. Then Proposition
4.14
(ii) gives a
negative
answer of Nakajima’s question. Furthermore, we may ask the following question:

Problem 4.15
.

(i) Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is a component-generic pointed stable curve over
k
. Can we find a necessary and sufficient condition that
X^{\bullet}
is
(m,n_{X})
-ordinary for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
prime to
p
?

(ii) Does

M_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\rm cl}\cap(\bigcap_{m\in\mathbb{N}\ {\text{\rm s.t.}}\ (m,p)=1}U_{(m,n_{X})})=\emptyset

hold for an arbitrary non-negative integer
n_{X}
? Moreover, does

\overline{M}_{g_{X},n_{X}}^{\rm cl}\cap(\bigcap_{m\in\mathbb{N}\ {\text{\rm s.t.}}\ (m,p)=1}\overline{U}_{(m,n_{X})})=\emptyset

hold for an arbitrary non-negative integer
n_{X}
?

5.
Maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariants for arbitrary curves

In the present section, we discuss the maximum generalized Hasse-Witt invariants of cyclic admissible coverings for arbitrary pointed stable curves. The main result of this section is Theorem
5.4
.

5.1.
Idea

We briefly explain the idea of our proof of Theorem
5.4
.

5.1.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
.

5.1.2.
An easy case

Firstly, let us prove an easy case (i.e.,
X^{\bullet}
is irreducible) of the main result of the present section.

Proposition 5.1
.

Suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is irreducible. Then there exist a positive natural number
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1\in\mathbb{N}
, an effective divisor
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
(
2.2.5
) on
X
of degree
(n_{X}-1)n
if
n_{X}\neq 0
(resp. degree
0
if
n_{X}=0
), and an element
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
(Definition
2.4
(i)) such that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum (Definition
3.10
(ii)). Namely, the following holds:

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Since
X^{\bullet}
is irreducible, we write
\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}
for the smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{\widetilde{X}},n_{\widetilde{X}})
over
k
associated to the unique vertex of the dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
of
X^{\bullet}
(
2.1.3
). Note that we have

g_{\widetilde{X}}=g_{X}-\#(X^{\rm sing}),\ n_{\widetilde{X}}=n_{X}+2\#(X^{\rm sing}).

Moreover,
D_{X}\subseteq D_{\widetilde{X}}
implies
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}\subseteq(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{\widetilde{X}}]^{0}
.

By applying Theorem
3.9
, to verify the proposition, it is sufficient to prove that there exist a positive natural number
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1\in\mathbb{N}
, an effective divisor
\widetilde{D}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
of degree
(n_{X}-1)n
if
n_{X}\neq 0
(resp. degree
0
if
n_{X}=0
) on
\widetilde{X}
, and an element
\widetilde{\alpha}\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{\widetilde{D}}(\widetilde{X}^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{D})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{\widetilde{X}}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{\widetilde{X}}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Suppose that
n_{X}\leq 1
. Then the proposition follows immediately from Proposition
3.11
(ii).

Suppose that
n_{X}\geq 2
. Let
D_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n_{X}}\}
and
n_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t_{i}}-1
,
i\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}-1\}
, such that the following conditions are satisfied:

•

n_{i}>\text{max}\{C(g_{X})+1,\#(e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))\}
.

•

0<a_{i,1},a_{i,2}<n_{i}
and
a_{i,1}+a_{i,2}=n_{i}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}-1\}
.

Furthermore, we put

D_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}a_{i,1}x_{i}+a_{i,2}x_{i+1}+\sum_{x\in D_{X}\setminus\{x_{i},x_{i+1}\}}n_{i}x,\ i\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}-1\},

which is an effective divisor on
\widetilde{X}
of degree
(n_{X}-1)n_{i}
. Moreover, we put

\widetilde{D}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{X}-1}p^{\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}t_{j}}D_{i},

n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{\sum_{i=0}^{n_{X}-1}t_{j}}-1=\sum_{i=1}^{n_{X}-1}p^{\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}t_{j}}(p^{t_{i}}-1),

where
t_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}0
. We see that
\widetilde{D}
is an effective divisor on
\widetilde{X}
of degree
(n_{X}-1)n
such that
\widetilde{D}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D}}
be a line bundle on
\widetilde{X}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D}}^{\otimes n}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}(-\widetilde{D})
, and
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D},t}
the pulling back of
\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D}}
by the natural morphism
\widetilde{X}_{t}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}
defined in
2.4.2
. Then by applying
[
T2
, Corollary 2.6, Lemma 2.12 (ii), and Corollary 2.13]
, we see that the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor associated to
\mathcal{B}^{t}_{\widetilde{D}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D},t}
exists (Definition
2.7
). Moreover, Proposition
2.8
implies that there exists a line bundle
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}
of degree
0
on
\widetilde{X}
such that
[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}]\neq[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}]
, that
[\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\otimes n}]=[\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}}]
, and that (see
2.3.4
for
\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}],\widetilde{D})}
)

\displaystyle\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}],\widetilde{D})}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{\widetilde{X}}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{\widetilde{X}}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Let
\widetilde{\alpha}\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{\widetilde{D}}(\widetilde{X}^{\bullet})
be the element corresponding to the pair
([\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}],\widetilde{D})\in\widetilde{\mathscr{P}}_{\widetilde{X}^{\bullet},n}
(
2.3.2
). Then we have
\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha},\widetilde{D})}=\gamma_{([\mathcal{L}_{\widetilde{D}}\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}],\widetilde{D})}
. This completes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Remark 5.1.1
.

We maintain the notation introduced in the proof of Proposition
5.1
. By choosing a suitable
a_{i,1}
(or
a_{i,2}
) for each
i\in\{1,\dots,n_{X}-1\}
, we obtain that the Galois multi-admissible covering induced by
\alpha
is connected.

5.1.3.
Strategy of the proof of Theorem
5.4

In the remainder of this section, we will generalize Proposition
5.1
to the case where
X^{\bullet}
is an arbitrary pointed stable curve over
k
(i.e., Theorem
5.4
below).

Let
X^{\bullet}
be an arbitrary pointed stable curve over
k
. For simplicity, we assume that every irreducible component of
X^{\bullet}
is non-singular. Moreover, by Proposition
3.11
(ii), we assume
n_{X}\geq 2
. We maintain the notation introduced in the statement of Theorem
3.9
. To verify Theorem
5.4
, by applying Theorem
3.9
, it is sufficient to construct a prime-to-
p
cyclic Galois multi-admissible covering
\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{v}:\widetilde{Y}_{v}^{\bullet}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
for every
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
such that the following conditions are satisfied:

•

\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{v},D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})}
satisfies the conditions mentioned in the statement of Theorem
3.9
.

•

\{\widetilde{f}_{v}^{\bullet}\}_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}
can be glued together. Then we obtain a prime-to-
p
cyclic Galois multi-admissible covering
f^{\bullet}:Y^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.

•

The ramification divisor
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
associated to
f^{\bullet}
such that
s(D)=n_{X}-1
(
2.2.5
).

If
X^{\bullet}
is
not
irreducible, the constructions of desired Galois multi-admissible coverings of
X^{\bullet}
are very difficult in general.

The main difficulty

We
cannot determine the ramifications over nodes
of a Galois admissible covering in a unique way when the ramifications over
D_{X}
are fixed. Then we have the following:

(i) Let
f^{\bullet}
be a Galois admissible covering whose ramification divisor satisfies the third condition mentioned above. Since the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor concerning
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}
,
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
,
does not exist
in general,
\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{v},D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})}
does not satisfy the first condition mentioned above.

(ii) Even though we can construct
\widetilde{f}^{\bullet}_{v}
for every
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
(by applying Proposition
5.1
) such that
\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{v},D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}})}
satisfies the conditions mentioned in the statement of Theorem
3.9
,
\{\widetilde{f}_{v}^{\bullet}\}_{v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})}

cannot be glued together (as admissible coverings) in general.

To overcome this difficulty, we observe that, when
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a
minimal quasi-tree
, the ramifications over nodes of a Galois admissible covering can be uniquely determined if the ramifications over
D_{X}
are fixed. Then for every
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, we may construct certain effective divisors on
\widetilde{X}_{v}
for every marked point of
D_{X}\cap\widetilde{X}_{v}
and every node of
X^{\rm sing}\cap\widetilde{X}_{v}
. Furthermore, by similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of Proposition
5.1
, we may construct an effective divisor on
\widetilde{X}_{v}
such that the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor concerning this effective divisor exists. Then we can obtain Galois multi-admissible coverings for all
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
whose first generalized Hasse-Witt invariants attain maximum. On the other hand, since the ramifications over nodes of a Galois admissible covering can be uniquely determined by the ramifications of marked points when
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a minimal quasi-tree, then we also obtain an effective divisor on
X
whose restriction on
\widetilde{X}_{v}
,
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, is the effective divisor constructed above. Then we may construct a desired Galois multi-admissible covering
f^{\bullet}
(e.g. see Example
5.2
below).

In the general case (i.e.,
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is not a tree), we take a
minimal quasi-tree
\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma_{D_{X}}
associated to
D_{X}
(
4.4.3
) and the pointed stable curve
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
(
4.4.5
) associated to
\Gamma
. Then we may construct a desired Galois multi-admissible covering for
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
(see Lemma
5.3
). In fact, this is the motivation of the definition of minimal quasi-trees. Moreover, Raynaud’s theorem (i.e.,
[
R1
, Théorème 4.1.1]
or Theorem
2.9
) implies that we may construct a Galois (étale) multi-admissible covering for each irreducible component corresponding to
w\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})\setminus v(\Gamma)
whose first generalized Hasse-Witt invariant (
2.2.4
) attains maximum. Then by gluing the Galois multi-admissible coverings together, we obtain a desired Galois multi-admissible covering of
X^{\bullet}
.

5.2.
A key lemma

5.2.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
.

5.2.2.

In order to convince the reader to follow the constructions given in the proof of Lemma
5.3
below, we give an example for constructing effective divisors
D_{\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{\widetilde{X}_{v}}]^{0}
,
v\in v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
, and
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
.

Example 5.2
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},3)
over
k
. Suppose that
X
has two non-singular irreducible components
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, that
X_{1}\cap X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x^{-}\}
, that
D_{X}\cap X_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1,1},x_{1,2}\}
, and that
D_{X}\cap X_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{2,1}\}
. Let
n_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t_{0}}-1>>0
,
D_{X_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1,1},x_{1,2},x^{-}\}
,
D_{X_{2}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{2,1},x^{-}\}
,
X^{\bullet}_{1}=(X_{1},D_{X_{1}})
a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X_{1}},3)
, and
X^{\bullet}_{2}=(X_{2},D_{X_{2}})
a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X_{2}},2)
. Then we have the following:

:

Step 1:
We construct effective divisors of degree
2n
(resp.
n
) on
X_{1}
(resp.
X_{2}
) such that the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisors concerning the divisors exist.

We put

Q_{1,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}a_{1}x_{1,1}+a_{2}x_{1,2}+n_{0}x^{-}\in\text{Div}(X_{1}),

Q_{1,2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}n_{0}x_{2,1}\in\text{Div}(X_{2}),

Q_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}a_{1}x_{1,1}+a_{2}x_{1,2}+n_{0}x_{2,1}\in\text{Div}(X),

where
0<a_{1},a_{2}<n_{0}
such that
a_{1}+a_{2}=n_{0}
. Moreover, we put

Q_{2,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}n_{0}x_{1,1}+b_{1}x_{1,2}+b_{2}x^{-}\in\text{Div}(X_{1}),

Q_{2,2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b_{1}x^{-}+b_{2}x_{2,1}\in\text{Div}(X_{2}),

Q_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}n_{0}x_{1,1}+b_{1}x_{1,2}+b_{2}x_{2,1}\in\text{Div}(X),

where
0<b_{1},b_{2}<n_{0}
such that
b_{1}+b_{2}=n_{0}
.

Since
n_{0}>>0
, by applying
[
T2
, Corollary 2.6, Lemma 2.12 (ii), and Corollary 2.13]
, the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisors concerning
Q_{1}
,
Q_{2}
,
Q_{1,1}
,
Q_{1,2}
,
Q_{2,1}
,
Q_{2,2}
exist, respectively.

Note that since
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a
tree
,
Q_{1,1}
and
Q_{1,2}
(resp.
Q_{2,1}
and
Q_{2,2}
) can be completely determined by
Q_{1}
(resp.
Q_{2}
). On the other hand, at present, we
cannot
construct Galois multi-admissible coverings whose ramification divisors are the above divisors since
Q_{1}
,
Q_{1,1}
,
Q_{1,2}
(resp.
Q_{2}
,
Q_{2,1}
,
Q_{2,2}
)
are not contained in

(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{1}}]^{0}
(resp.
(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{2}}]^{0})
.

Step 2:
By using the effective divisors constructed in Step 1, we construct a global effective divisor
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
on
X
and
D_{i}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{i}}]^{0}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, on
X_{i}
such that
D_{i}
is completely determined by
D
.

Write
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
for the vertices of
v(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}})
corresponding to
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
, respectively. We define the following sets of effective divisors

\text{Div}_{v_{i}}^{\rm irr\text{-}mp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{Q_{1,i}\},\ \text{Div}_{v_{i}}^{\rm irr\text{-}nd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{Q_{2,i}\},

\text{Div}_{X}^{\rm irr}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{i=1,2}(\text{Div}_{v_{i}}^{\rm irr\text{-}mp}\sqcup\text{Div}_{v_{i}}^{\rm irr\text{-}nd}),\ \text{Div}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{Q_{1},Q_{2}\},

where
\sqcup
means disjoint union. Let
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t^{0}}-1+p^{t_{0}}(p^{t_{0}}-1)=p^{2t_{0}}-1
. We obtain

D_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}Q_{1,1}+p^{t_{0}}Q_{2,1}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{1}}]^{0},

D_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}Q_{1,2}+p^{t_{0}}Q_{2,2}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{2}}]^{0},

D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}Q_{1}+p^{t_{0}}Q_{2}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}.

Then we have

D|_{\{x_{1,1},x_{1,2}\}}=D_{1}|_{\{x_{1,1},x_{1,2}\}},\ D|_{\{x_{2,1}\}}=D_{2}|_{\{x_{2,1}\}}.

Step 3:
We construct a desired Galois multi-admissible covering of
X^{\bullet}
.

By applying
[
T2
, Corollary 2.6, Lemma 2.12 (ii), and Corollary 2.13]
, the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisors concerning
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
exist. By Proposition
2.8
, if
t_{0}>>0
, then there exists
\alpha_{v_{i}}\in\text{Rev}_{D_{i}}^{\rm adm}(X_{i}^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
,
i\in\{1,2\}
, such that
\gamma_{(\alpha_{v_{1}},D_{1})}=g_{X_{1}}+1
and
\gamma_{(\alpha_{v_{1}},D_{1})}=g_{X_{2}}
. Moreover, since
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
is a tree, we have that

D_{1}|_{x^{-}}=[\sum_{x\in D_{X}\setminus\{x_{1,1},x_{1,2}\}}\text{ord}_{x}(D)]x^{-}=\text{ord}_{x_{2,1}}(D)x^{-},

D_{2}|_{x^{-}}=\text{ord}_{x^{-}}(D_{2})x^{-}=(n-\text{ord}_{x_{2,1}}(D_{2}))x^{-}=[\sum_{x\in D_{X}\setminus\{x_{2,1}\}}\text{ord}_{x}(D)]x^{-}

=(n-\text{ord}_{x_{2,1}}(D))x^{-},

where
[(-)]
denotes the image of
(-)
in
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Namely, the Galois multi-admissible coverings induced by
\alpha_{v_{1}}
and
\alpha_{v_{2}}

can be glued
(as admissible coverings). Then we obtain a desired Galois multi-admissible covering of
X^{\bullet}
.

5.2.3.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be a connected semi-graph and
v\in v(\mathbf{G})
an arbitrary vertex. Moreover, we suppose that
\mathbf{G}
is a
tree
. For each
v^{\prime}\in v(\mathbf{G})
, there exists a path
\delta_{v,v^{\prime}}
connecting
v
and
v^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{G}
. We define

\text{leng}(\delta_{v,v^{\prime}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\#\{\delta_{v,v^{\prime}}\cap v(\mathbf{G})\}-1

to be the
length
of the path
\delta_{v,v^{\prime}}
. Moreover, since
\mathbf{G}
is a tree, there exists a unique path connecting
v
and
v^{\prime}
whose length is equal to
\text{min}\{\text{leng}(\delta_{v,v^{\prime}})\}_{\delta_{v,v^{\prime}}}
. We shall write

\delta(\mathbf{G},v,v^{\prime})

for this unique path connecting
v
and
v^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{G}
, and call
\delta(\mathbf{G},v,v^{\prime})
the
minimal path
connecting
v
and
v^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{G}
.

5.2.4.

Now, we are going to prove the key lemma of the present section.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma_{D_{X}}
be a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
,

X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}=(X_{\Gamma},D_{X_{\Gamma}})

the pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X_{\Gamma}},n_{X_{\Gamma}})
associated to
\Gamma
(
4.4.3
), and
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}}
the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
. Suppose that
n_{X}\geq 2
. Then there exist a positive natural number
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1\in\mathbb{N}
, an effective divisor
D_{\Gamma}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}\subseteq(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{\Gamma}}]^{0}
on
X_{\Gamma}
, and an element
\alpha_{\Gamma}\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D_{\Gamma}}(X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
such that the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma},D_{\Gamma})}=g_{X_{\Gamma}}+n_{X}-2.

Proof.

Since
\Gamma
is a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
, we obtain that
\Gamma^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma)
is a tree. Then we have
v(\Gamma)=v(\Gamma^{\prime})
. Note that
D_{X}\subseteq D_{X_{\Gamma}}
. Let
v\in v(\Gamma)
be an arbitrary vertex and
n_{0}=p^{t_{0}}-1\in\mathbb{N}
a positive natural number satisfying

n_{0}>\text{max}\{C(g_{X})+1,\#(e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))\}.

Step 1 (mp):
Let
v\in v(\Gamma)
. We construct a family of effective divisors
\text{Div}_{v}^{\rm irr\text{-}mp}
on the irreducible component
X^{\bullet}_{v}
associated to the set of marked points
D_{X}\cap X_{v}
, and construct a family of effective divisors
\text{Div}_{v}^{\rm mp}
on
X_{\Gamma}
, where “mp” means “marked point”.

We put

D^{\prime}_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{X}\cap X_{v},\ m_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\#(D^{\prime}_{v}),\ \text{and}\ D^{\prime}_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{v,1},\dots,x_{v,m_{v}}\}\ \text{if}\ m_{v}\neq 0.

Note that
D_{v}^{\prime}\neq D_{X_{\Gamma}}\cap X_{v}
in general. Moreover, we put

D_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D^{\prime}_{v}\cup(X_{v}\cap(\overline{X_{\Gamma}\setminus X_{v}})),

where
\overline{X_{\Gamma}\setminus X_{v}}
denotes the topological closure of
X_{\Gamma}\setminus X_{v}
in
X_{\Gamma}
. Note that since
n_{X}>0
, we have
\#(D_{v})>0
. Let
w\in v(\Gamma)\setminus\{v\}
be an arbitrary vertex distinct from
v
. Since
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a tree, there exists a unique node

x_{v,w}^{-}\in D_{w}\subseteq X_{w}

such that the closed edge of
\Gamma^{\prime}
corresponding to
x_{v,w}^{-}
is contained in the minimal path
\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w)
(i.e., the minimal path connecting
v
and
w
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
defined in
5.2.3
). On the other hand, we define a set of nodes to be

\text{Node}_{v,w}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{X_{w}\cap X_{w^{\prime}},\ w^{\prime}\in v(\Gamma)\ |\ \text{leng}(\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w^{\prime}))=\text{leng}(\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w))+1\}.

Note that
\text{Node}_{v,w}^{+}
may possibly be an empty set, and that
D_{w}=\{x^{-}_{v,w}\}\cup\text{Node}^{+}_{v,w}\cup D^{\prime}_{w}.

We define two sets of effective divisors

\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-mp}},\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{mp}}

associated to
v
on
X_{v}
and
X_{\Gamma}
, as follows. Let
i\in\{1,\dots,m_{v}-1\}
and
0<a_{v,i,1},a_{v,i,2}<n_{0}
such that
a_{v,i,1}+a_{v,i,2}=n_{0}
. Suppose that
m_{v}\leq 1
. Then we put

\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-mp}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\emptyset,\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{mp}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\emptyset.

Suppose that
m_{v}\geq 2
. We define

Q_{v,v,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}a_{v,i,1}x_{v,i}+a_{v,i,2}x_{v,i+1}+\sum_{x^{\prime}\in D^{\prime}_{v}\setminus\{x_{v,i},x_{v,i+1}\}}n_{0}x^{\prime}+\sum_{x\in D_{v}\setminus D^{\prime}_{v}}n_{0}x,\ i\in\{1,\dots,m_{v}-1\},

to be an effective divisor on
X_{v}
whose support is
D_{v}
, and whose degree is equal to
(\#(D_{v})-1)n_{0}
. We define

Q_{v,w,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{x\in D_{w}\setminus\{x_{v,w}^{-}\}}n_{0}x,\ w\in v(\Gamma)\setminus\{v\},

to be an effective divisor on
X_{w}
whose support is
D_{w}\setminus\{x_{v,w}^{-}\}
, and whose degree is equal to
(\#(D_{w})-1)n_{0}
.

Moreover, we define

Q^{v}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}a_{v,i,1}x_{v,i}+a_{v,i,2}x_{v,i+1}+\sum_{x\in D_{X}\setminus\{x_{v,i},x_{v,i+1}\}}n_{0}x,

to be an effective divisor on
X_{\Gamma}
whose support is
D_{X}
, and whose degree is
(n_{X}-1)n_{0}
. Note that
Q_{i}^{v}|_{D_{u}^{\prime}}=Q_{v,u,i}|_{D_{u}^{\prime}}
for all
u\in v(\Gamma)
, and that
Q_{v,w,i}=Q_{v,w,i^{\prime}}
for all
i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,\dots,m_{v}-1\}
.

We put

\text{Div}_{v,i}^{\text{irr-mp}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{u\in v(\Gamma)}\{Q_{v,u,i}\},\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-mp}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{m_{v}-1}\text{Div}_{v,i}^{\text{irr-mp}},\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{mp}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{m_{v}-1}\{Q_{i}^{v}\},

where
\sqcup
means disjoint union.

Step 1 (nd):
Let
v\in v(\Gamma)
. We construct a family of effective divisors
\text{Div}_{v}^{\rm irr\text{-}nd}
on the irreducible component
X^{\bullet}_{v}
associated to the set of nodes
D_{v}\setminus D^{\prime}_{v}=X_{v}\cap(\overline{X_{\Gamma}\setminus X_{v}})
, and construct a family of effective divisors
\text{Div}_{v}^{\rm nd}
on
X_{\Gamma}
, where “nd” means “node”.

We define two families of effective divisors

\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-nd}},\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{nd}}

associated to
v
on
X_{v}
and
X_{\Gamma}
, respectively, as follows. Let
z\in D_{X}\setminus D^{\prime}_{v}
and
0<b_{v,z,1},b_{v,z,2}<n_{0}
such that
b_{v,z,1}+b_{v,z,2}=n_{0}
. Suppose that
m_{v}=0
. Then we put

\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-nd}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\emptyset,\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{nd}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\emptyset.

Suppose that
m_{v}\neq 0
. Let
w_{z}
be the vertex such that the irreducible component
X_{w_{z}}
corresponding to
w_{z}
contains
z
(i.e.,
z\in D^{\prime}_{w_{z}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{X}\cap X_{w_{z}}
). Note that
w_{z}\neq v
. Moreover, let
\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z})
be the minimal path connecting
v
and
w_{z}
in
\Gamma^{\prime}
and
w\in v(\Gamma)
an arbitrary vertex distinct from
w_{z}
such that
w\subseteq\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z})
. Since
\Gamma^{\prime}
is a tree, we have
\#(\text{Node}_{v,w}^{+}\cap\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z}))=1
. Then we put

x_{v,w,w_{z}}^{+}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{Node}_{v,w}^{+}\cap\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z})\in D_{w}\subseteq X_{w}.

For
v
and
w_{z}
, we define

Q_{v,v,z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b_{v,z,1}x_{v,m_{v}}+b_{v,z,2}x_{v,v,w_{z}}^{+}+\sum_{x\in D_{v}\setminus\{x_{v,m_{v}},x_{v,v,w_{z}}^{+}\}}n_{0}x,

Q_{v,w_{z},z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b_{v,z,1}x_{v,w_{z}}^{-}+b_{v,z,2}z+\sum_{x\in D_{w_{z}}\setminus\{x_{v,w_{z}},z\}}n_{0}x

to be effective divisors on
X_{v}
and
X_{w_{z}}
whose supports are
D_{v}
and
D_{w_{z}}
, and whose degrees are equal to
(\#(D_{v})-1)n_{0}
and
(\#(D_{w_{z}})-1)n_{0}
, respectively.

Let
w\in v(\Gamma)\setminus\{v,w_{z}\}
be an arbitrary vertex such that
w\subseteq\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z})
. Then we define

Q_{v,w,z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b_{v,z,1}x_{v,w}^{-}+b_{v,z,2}x_{v,w,w_{z}}^{+}+\sum_{x\in D_{w}\setminus\{x_{v,w}^{-},x_{v,w,w_{z}}^{+}\}}n_{0}x

to be an effective divisor on
X_{w}
whose support is
D_{w}
, and whose degree is equal to
(\#(D_{w})-1)n_{0}
.

Let
w^{\prime}\in v(\Gamma)
be an arbitrary vertex such that
w^{\prime}\not\subseteq\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z})
. Then we define

Q_{v,w^{\prime},z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{x\in D_{w^{\prime}}\setminus\{x_{v,w^{\prime}}^{-}\}}n_{0}x

to be an effective divisor on
X_{w^{\prime}}
whose support is
D_{w^{\prime}}\setminus\{x_{v,w^{\prime}}^{-}\}
, and whose degree is equal to
(\#(D_{w^{\prime}})-1)n_{0}
. Note that, if
w^{\prime\prime}\not\subseteq\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z})\cup\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v,w_{z^{\prime}})
for
z,z^{\prime}\in D_{X}\setminus D_{v}^{\prime}
, we have
Q_{v,w^{\prime\prime},z}=Q_{v,w^{\prime\prime},z^{\prime}}
.

Moreover, we define

Q_{z}^{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}b_{v,z,1}x_{v,m_{v}}+b_{v,z,2}z+\sum_{x\in D_{X}\setminus\{x_{v,m_{v}},z\}}n_{0}x

to be an effective divisor on
X_{\Gamma}
whose support is
D_{X}
, and whose degree is equal to
(n_{X}-1)n_{0}
. Note that
Q_{z}^{v}|_{D_{u}^{\prime}}=Q_{v,u,z}|_{D_{u}^{\prime}}
for all
u\in v(\Gamma)
.

We put

\text{Div}_{v,z}^{\text{irr-nd}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{u\in v(\Gamma)}\{Q_{v,u,z}\},\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-nd}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{z\in D_{X}\setminus D^{\prime}_{v}}\text{Div}_{v,z}^{\text{irr-nd}},\ \text{Div}_{v}^{\text{nd}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{z\in D_{X}\setminus D^{\prime}_{v}}\{Q_{z}^{v}\}.

Step 2:
We construct an effective divisor
P_{u}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{u}]^{0}
,
v\in v(\Gamma)
, on
X_{u}
and an effective divisor
P_{\Gamma}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{\Gamma}}]^{0}
on
X_{\Gamma}
for some
n
.

We put

\text{Div}^{\text{irr}}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{v\in v(\Gamma)}(\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-mp}}\sqcup\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{irr-nd}}),

\text{Div}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\bigsqcup_{v\in v(\Gamma)}(\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{mp}}\sqcup\text{Div}_{v}^{\text{nd}}).

We denote by
\text{Div}^{\text{irr}}_{X}(X_{u})\subseteq\text{Div}_{X}^{\text{irr}}
,
u\in v(\Gamma)
, the subset whose elements are effective divisors on
X_{u}
. Note that the above constructions imply
d\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\#(\text{Div}_{X}^{\text{irr}}(X_{u_{1}}))=\#(\text{Div}_{X}^{\text{irr}}(X_{u_{2}}))=\#(\text{Div}_{X})
for all
u_{1},u_{2}\in v(\Gamma)
. Moreover, let

o_{u}:\{1,\dots,d\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\text{Div}_{X}^{\text{irr}}(X_{u})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{P_{u,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}o_{u}(1),\dots,P_{u,d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}o_{u}(d)\},\ u\in v(\Gamma),

be a bijective (as sets) such that, for all
u_{1},u_{2}\in v(\Gamma)
and all
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
, the following conditions are satisfied:

•

if
P_{u_{1},j}\in\text{Div}_{v,i}^{\text{irr-mp}}
for some
v\in v(\Gamma)
and some
i\in\{1,\dots,m_{v}-1\}
, then
P_{u_{2},j}\in\text{Div}_{v,i}^{\text{irr-mp}}
.

•

If
P_{u_{1},j}\in\text{Div}_{v,z}^{\text{irr-nd}}
for some
v\in v(\Gamma)
and some
z\in D_{X}\setminus D_{v}^{\prime}
, then
P_{u_{2},j}\in\text{Div}_{v,z}^{\text{irr-nd}}
.

Then, by the construction of
\text{Div}_{X}
, we obtain a bijection

o:\{1,\dots,d\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\text{Div}_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{P_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}o(1),\dots,P_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}o(d)\}

induced by
o_{u}
,
u\in v(\Gamma)
.

Let
t\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}dt_{0}
and
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}p^{(j-1)t_{0}}(p^{t_{0}}-1)=p^{t}-1
. We define

P_{u}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}p^{(j-1)t_{0}}P_{u,j}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{u}]^{0},\ u\in v(\Gamma),

P_{\Gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}p^{(j-1)t_{0}}P_{j}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}

to be effective divisors of degrees
\text{deg}(P_{u})=(\#(D_{u})-1)n
and
\text{deg}(P_{\Gamma})=(n_{X}-1)n
on
X_{u}
and
X_{\Gamma}
, respectively. We see that the support of
P_{u}
,
u\in v(\Gamma)
, is
D_{u}
, and that the support of
P_{\Gamma}
is
D_{X}
.

Let
u\in v(\Gamma)
and
x^{\prime}\in D^{\prime}_{u}
. Then the above constructions implies
P_{\Gamma}|_{x^{\prime}}=P_{u}|_{x^{\prime}}
. Moreover, let
x\in D_{u}\setminus D_{u}^{\prime}
. Since
\Gamma\setminus\{e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma)\}
is a tree,
X_{\Gamma}\setminus\{x\}
has two connected components
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
. Let
C_{2}
be the connected component such that
X_{u}\setminus\{x\}
is not contained in
C_{2}
. We denote by
C_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\overline{\{C_{2}\}}
the topological closure of
C_{2}
in
X_{\Gamma}
and
M_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}C_{x}\cap D_{X}
. Then the above constructions imply

P_{u}|_{x}=\text{ord}_{x}(P_{u})x=[\sum_{x^{\prime}\in M_{x}}\text{ord}_{x^{\prime}}(P_{\Gamma})]x,

where
[(-)]
denotes the image of
(-)
in
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.

Step 3:
We construct a desired Galois multi-admissible covering of
X^{\bullet}
.

Let
u\in v(\Gamma)
and
\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{u}
the smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{u},n_{u})
over
k
(
2.1.3
). Write
\text{norm}_{u}:\widetilde{X}_{u}\rightarrow X_{u}
for the normalization morphism. Then we obtain the pulling back divisor
\text{norm}_{u}^{*}(P_{u})
on
\widetilde{X}_{u}
. Note that since
\text{Supp}(P_{u})
is contained in the smooth locus of
X_{u}
, we have
\text{norm}_{u}^{*}(P_{u})=P_{u}
. Let
\mathcal{L}_{P_{u}}
be a line bundle on
\widetilde{X}_{u}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{P_{u}}^{\otimes n}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{u}}(-P_{u})
, and
\mathcal{L}_{P_{u},t}
the pulling back of
\mathcal{L}_{P_{u}}
by the natural morphism
\widetilde{X}_{u,t}\rightarrow\widetilde{X}
defined in
2.4.2
. Then by applying
[
T2
, Corollary 2.6, Lemma 2.12 (ii), and Corollary 2.13]
, we see that the Raynaud-Tamagawa theta divisor associated to
\mathcal{B}^{t}_{P_{u}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{P_{u},t}
exists (Definition
2.7
). Moreover, Proposition
2.8
implies that there exists
\widetilde{\alpha}_{u}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{P_{u}}(\widetilde{X}_{u}^{\bullet})
such that

\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{u},P_{u})}=g_{u}+\#(D_{u})-2.

Then Theorem
3.9
implies that the element
\alpha_{u}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{P_{u}}(X_{u}^{\bullet})
induced by
\widetilde{\alpha}_{u}
such that the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha_{u},P_{u})}=g_{X_{u}}+\#(D_{u})-2,

where
g_{X_{u}}
denotes the genus of
X_{u}
. Write

f_{u}^{\bullet}:Y_{u}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{u}^{\bullet}

for the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
\alpha_{u}
.

Let
u^{\prime}\in v(\Gamma)\setminus\{u\}
such that
X_{u}\cap X_{u^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
. We denote by
x_{u,u^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}X_{u}\cap X_{u^{\prime}}
the unique node. Then the above constructions imply

0<\text{ord}_{x_{u,u^{\prime}}}(P_{u}),\text{ord}_{x_{u,u^{\prime}}}(P_{u^{\prime}})<n,\ \text{ord}_{x_{u,u^{\prime}}}(P_{u})+\text{ord}_{x_{u,u^{\prime}}}(P_{u^{\prime}})=n.

This means that we may glue (as admissible coverings)
\{Y_{u}^{\bullet}\}_{u\in v(\Gamma)}
along
\{f^{-1}_{u}(D_{u}\setminus D^{\prime}_{u})\}_{u\in v(\Gamma)}
in a way that is compatible with the gluing of
\{X_{u}^{\bullet}\}_{u\in v(\Gamma)}
that gives rise to
X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
. Then we obtain a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}:Y_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Note that the construction of
f_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
implies that
f_{\Gamma}
is étale over
D_{X_{\Gamma}}\setminus D_{X}
.

Let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{u}}
,
u\in v(\Gamma)
, be the admissible fundamental group of
X_{u}^{\bullet}
.
We denote by
\alpha_{\Gamma}\in\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
an element induced by
f_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
such that the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi_{X_{u}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\rightarrow\Pi_{X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\overset{\alpha_{\Gamma}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is equal to
\alpha_{u}
for all
u\in v(\Gamma)
. We put
D_{\Gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}P_{\Gamma}
. Then we see
\alpha_{\Gamma}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D_{\Gamma}}(X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
. Theorem
3.9
implies

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma},D_{\Gamma})}=g_{X_{\Gamma}}+s(P_{\Gamma})-1=g_{X_{\Gamma}}+n_{X}-2.

We complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

5.3.
The second main theorem

Now, we prove the second main result of the present paper.

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be an arbitrary pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p>0
. Then there exist a positive natural number
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1\in\mathbb{N}
, an effective divisor
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
(
2.2.5
) on
X
of degree
(n_{X}-1)n
if
n_{X}\neq 0
(resp. degree
0
if
n_{X}=0
), and an element
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
(Definition
2.4
(i)) such that
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}
attains maximum (Definition
3.10
(ii)). Namely, the following holds (see Definition
3.10
(i) for
\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}
):

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=\gamma^{\rm max}_{X^{\bullet}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
g_{X}+n_{X}-2,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

Suppose that
n_{X}\leq 1
. Then the theorem follows from Proposition
3.11
(ii). To verify the theorem, we may assume
n_{X}\geq 2
.

Let
\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma_{D_{X}}
be a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
,
\Gamma^{\rm im}
the image of the natural morphism
\phi_{\Gamma}:\Gamma\rightarrow\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
,
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}=(X_{\Gamma},D_{X_{\Gamma}})
,
X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}=(X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}},D_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})
the pointed stable curves over
k
of types
(g_{X_{\Gamma}},n_{X_{\Gamma}})
,
(g_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}},n_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})
associated to
\Gamma
,
\Gamma^{\rm im}
, respectively (see
4.4.3
,
4.4.5
for the definitions of
\Gamma^{\rm im}
,
\phi_{\Gamma}
,
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
, and
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
), and
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}},

\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
the admissible fundamental groups of
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
,
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
, respectively.

Lemma
5.3
implies that there exist a natural number

n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1>\text{max}\{C(g_{X})+1,\#(e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))\},

an effective divisor
D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{\Gamma}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}\subseteq(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{\Gamma}}]^{0}
on
X_{\Gamma}
of degree
(n_{X}-1)n
, and an element
\alpha_{\Gamma}\in\text{Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma})\setminus\{0\}
such that the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma},D)}=g_{X_{\Gamma}}+n_{X}-2.

We denote by
f_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}:Z_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
induced by
\alpha_{\Gamma}
. Note that
f_{\Gamma}
is étale over
D_{X_{\Gamma}}\setminus D_{X}
. By gluing
Z_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
along

f_{\Gamma}^{-1}(D_{X_{\Gamma}}\setminus(D_{X}\cup\{x_{e}\}_{e\in\phi^{-1}_{\Gamma}(e^{\rm op}(\Gamma^{\rm im}))}))

in a way that is compatible with the gluing of
X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
that gives rise to
X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}
, we obtain a pointed stable curve
Z_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}
over
k
. Moreover,
f_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
induces a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}:Z_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Write
\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
for an element of
\text{Hom}(\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
induced by
f^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
such that the composition of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}}^{\rm ab}\rightarrow\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}\overset{\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
is equal to
\alpha_{\Gamma}
. Note that we have
D_{\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}=D
. Then Theorem
3.9
implies

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}},D)}=g_{X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}+n_{X}-2.

On the other hand, we write
\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})
for the set of connected components of the topological closure
\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}
of
X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
in
X
. We define the following pointed stable curve

E^{\bullet}=(E,D_{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}E\cap X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}),\ E\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}),

over
k
.
Proposition
3.11
(ii) implies that there exists a Galois
étale
covering

f^{\bullet}_{E}:Z^{\bullet}_{E}=(Z_{E},D_{Z_{E}})\rightarrow E^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\gamma_{(\alpha_{E},0)}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{E},&\text{if}\ g_{E}=0,\\
g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ g_{E}\neq 0,\end{array}\right.

where
g_{E}
denotes the genus of
E
, and
\alpha_{E}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{0}(E^{\bullet})
is an element induced by
f^{\bullet}_{E}
.

We may glue
Z_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}^{\bullet}
and
\{Z_{E}^{\bullet}\}_{E\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}
along
f^{-1}_{\Gamma}(X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}\cap(\bigcup_{E\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}E))
and
\{f^{-1}_{E}(X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}\cap E)\}_{E\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}
in a way that is compatible with the gluing of
\{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}\}\cup\{E^{\bullet}\}_{E\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})}
that gives rise to
X^{\bullet}
, then we obtain a Galois multi-admissible covering

f^{\bullet}:Z^{\bullet}\rightarrow X^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
,
\Pi_{E^{\bullet}}
be the admissible fundamental groups of
X^{\bullet}
,
E^{\bullet}
,
E\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}})
, respectively. We write
\alpha\in\text{Hom}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab},\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})
for an element induced by
f^{\bullet}
such that the compositions of the natural homomorphisms
\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}\rightarrow\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},

\Pi^{\rm ab}_{E^{\bullet}}\rightarrow\Pi^{\rm ab}_{X^{\bullet}}\overset{\alpha}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\ E\in\pi_{0}(\overline{X\setminus X_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}}),
are equal to
\alpha_{\Gamma^{\rm im}}
and
\alpha_{E}
, respectively. We see
\alpha\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
. By applying Theorem
3.9
, we obtain

\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=g_{X}+n_{X}-2.

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

5.4.
A stronger version when
n_{X}=3

In the remainder of the present section, we prove a slightly stronger version of Theorem
5.4
when
n_{X}=3
(i.e., we prove Theorem
5.4
for a certain fixed effective divisor
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
). The stronger version will be used in the proof of reconstructions of field structures associated to inertia subgroups (cf. Section
6.2
). The main result of this subsection is Theorem
5.7
.

5.4.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
. Moreover,
we suppose that
n_{X}=3
.

5.4.2.

We generalize Lemma
5.3
to the case of certain fixed ramification divisors when
n_{X}=3
. Let
D_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}
, and let
\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma_{D_{X}}
be a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
,

X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}=(X_{\Gamma},D_{X_{\Gamma}})

the pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X_{\Gamma}},n_{X_{\Gamma}})
associated to
\Gamma
, and
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}}
the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
. Let
D_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]\subseteq\mathbb{Z}[D_{X_{\Gamma}}]
,
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, be an effective divisor on
X_{\Gamma}
such that the following conditions are satisfied:

•

{\rm deg}(D_{j})=2(p^{t_{j}}-1)
.

•

{\rm ord}_{x}(D_{j})\leq p^{t_{j}}-1
for each
x\in D_{X}
.

•

\#\{x\in D_{X}\ |\ {\rm ord}_{x}(D_{j})=p^{t_{j}}-1\}\geq 1
.

Let
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}}-1
, and let

D_{\Gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{1}+p^{t_{1}}D_{2}+p^{t_{1}+t_{2}}D_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]\subseteq\mathbb{Z}[D_{X_{\Gamma}}]

be an effective divisor on
X_{\Gamma}
with degree
2n
.
Then we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.5
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Suppose that
D_{\Gamma}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}\subseteq(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X_{\Gamma}}]^{0}
(i.e.,
{\rm ord}_{x}(D_{\Gamma})<n
for all
x\in D_{X}
), and that

n>{\rm max}\{C(g_{X})+1,\#(e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))\}.

Then there exists an element
\alpha_{\Gamma}\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D_{\Gamma}}(X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
such that the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma},D_{\Gamma})}=g_{X_{\Gamma}}+1.

Proof.

Since
\Gamma
is a minimal quasi-tree associated to
D_{X}
, we obtain that
\Gamma^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\Gamma\setminus e^{\rm lp}(\Gamma)
is a tree. If
D_{X}\subseteq X_{v}
for some
v\in v(\Gamma)
, then the lemma follows from Proposition
5.1
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that one of the following conditions holds:

(i) Let
w_{1}
,
w_{2}\in v(\Gamma)
be vertices distinct from each other such that
x_{1}
,
x_{2}\in X_{w_{1}}
, and
x_{3}\in X_{w_{2}}
(see Example
5.6
(i) below).

(ii) Let
v_{1}
,
v_{2}
,
v_{3}\in v(\Gamma)
be vertices distinct from each other such that
x_{1}\in X_{v_{1}}
,
x_{2}\in X_{v_{2}}
, and
x_{3}\in X_{v_{3}}
(see Example
5.6
(ii) below).

We put

D^{\prime}_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{X}\cap X_{v},\ D_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D^{\prime}_{v}\cup(X_{v}\cap(\overline{X_{\Gamma}\setminus X_{v}})),\ v\in v(\Gamma)

where
\overline{X_{\Gamma}\setminus X_{v}}
denotes the topological closure of
X_{\Gamma}\setminus X_{v}
in
X_{\Gamma}
. Next, we construct an effective divisor
P_{v}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{v}]^{0}
on
X_{v}
for all
v\in v(\Gamma)
.

Suppose that (i) holds. Let
y_{w_{1},w_{2}}\in D_{w_{1}}
be the unique node of
X_{\Gamma}
such that the closed edge of

Let

Q_{w_{1},j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})x_{1}+\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})x_{2}+\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})y_{w_{1},w_{2}},

Q_{w_{2},j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})]z_{w_{1},w_{2}}+\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})x_{3},

Q_{w,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})]x_{w_{1},w}+\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})x_{w_{2},w},\ w\in v(\Gamma)\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2}\},

where
[(-)]
denotes the image of
(-)
in
\mathbb{Z}/(p^{t_{j}}-1)\mathbb{Z}
. Then
Q_{v,j}
,

(\#(D_{v})-1)(p^{t_{j}}-1)

P_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}Q_{v,1}+p^{t_{1}}Q_{v,2}+p^{t_{1}+t_{2}}Q_{v,3}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{v}]^{0},\ v\in v(\Gamma).

, and whose support is equal to
D_{v}
.

Suppose that (ii) holds. Then one of the following conditions is satisfied:

(1) There exist

Suppose that (1) holds. Without loss of generality, we may assume that

Let

Q_{v_{1},j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})x_{1}+[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})]y_{v_{1},v_{3}},

Q_{v_{2},j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})x_{v_{1},v_{2}}+\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})x_{2}+\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})x_{v_{3},v_{2}}

Q_{v_{3},j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})]z_{v_{1},v_{3}}+\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})x_{3}

Q_{v,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})x_{v_{1},v}+[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})]x_{v_{3},v}

for all
v\in(v(\Gamma)\cap\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v_{1},v_{2}))\setminus\{v_{1},v_{2}\}
,
and

Q_{v,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})]x_{v_{1},v}+\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})x_{v_{3},v}

Suppose that (2) holds. Then we have

\{v_{0}\}=v(\Gamma)\cap\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v_{1},v_{2})\cap\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v_{2},v_{3})\cap\delta(\Gamma^{\prime},v_{3},v_{1}).

Let
j\in\{1,2,3\}

Q_{v_{1},j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})x_{1}+[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})]y_{v_{1},v_{0}},

Q_{v_{2},j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})x_{2}+[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})]y_{v_{2},v_{0}},

Q_{v,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})x_{v_{1},v}+[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})]x_{v_{0},v}

Q_{v,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})x_{v_{2},v}+[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})]x_{v_{0},v}

,
and

Q_{v,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{ord}_{x_{3}}(D_{j})x_{v_{3},v}+[\text{deg}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{1}}(D_{j})-\text{ord}_{x_{2}}(D_{j})]x_{v_{0},v}

for all

,

P_{v}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}Q_{v,1}+p^{t_{1}}Q_{v,2}+p^{t_{1}+t_{2}}Q_{v,3}\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{v}]^{0},\ v\in v(\Gamma).

Then
P_{v}
is an effective divisor on
X_{v}
whose degree is equal to
(\#(D_{v})-1)n
, and whose support is equal to
D_{v}
.

Let
v\in v(\Gamma)
, and let
\widetilde{X}^{\bullet}_{v}
be the smooth pointed stable curve of type
(g_{v},n_{v})
over
k
associated to
v
(
2.1.3
). Since
D_{X}
is contained in the smooth locus of
X_{v}
,
P_{v}
can be also regarded as an effective divisor on
\widetilde{X}_{v}
. By applying similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of Proposition
5.1
, there exists
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{P_{v}}(\widetilde{X}_{v}^{\bullet})
such that

\gamma_{(\widetilde{\alpha}_{v},P_{v})}=g_{v}+\#(D_{v})-2.

Then Theorem
3.9
implies that the element
\alpha_{v}\in\text{Rev}^{\rm adm}_{P_{v}}(X_{v}^{\bullet})
induced by
\widetilde{\alpha}_{v}
such that the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha_{v},P_{v})}=g_{X_{v}}+\#(D_{v})-2,

where
g_{X_{v}}
denotes the genus of
X_{v}
. Write
f_{v}^{\bullet}:Y_{v}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{v}^{\bullet}
for the Galois multi-admissible covering over

with Galois group

induced by
\alpha_{v}
.

By applying similar arguments to the arguments given in Step 3 of the proof of Lemma
5.3
, we may glue
\{Y_{v}^{\bullet}\}_{v\in v(\Gamma)}
along
\{f^{-1}_{v}(D_{v}\setminus D^{\prime}_{v})\}_{v\in v(\Gamma)}
(as admissible coverings) in a way that is compatible with the gluing of
\{X_{v}^{\bullet}\}_{v\in v(\Gamma)}
that gives rise to
X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
. Then we obtain a Galois multi-admissible covering

f_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}:Y_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}\rightarrow X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}

over
k
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. Note that the construction of
f_{\Gamma}^{\bullet}
implies that
f_{\Gamma}
is étale over
D_{X_{\Gamma}}\setminus D_{X}
. Moreover, there exists an element
\alpha_{\Gamma}\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D_{\Gamma}}(X_{\Gamma}^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
induced by
f^{\bullet}_{\Gamma}
such that the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha_{\Gamma},D_{\Gamma})}=g_{X_{\Gamma}}+1.

We complete the proof of the lemma.
∎
