5.4.3.

We generalize Theorem
5.4
to the case of certain fixed ramification divisors as follows:

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be an arbitrary pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},3)
over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p>0
. Let
D_{X}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{1},x_{2},x_{3}\}
, and let
D_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]
,
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, be an effective divisor on
X
such that the following conditions are satisfied:

•

{\rm deg}(D_{j})=2(p^{t_{j}}-1)
.

•

{\rm ord}_{x}(D_{j})\leq p^{t_{j}}-1
for each
x\in D_{X}
.

•

\#\{x\in D_{X}\ |\ {\rm ord}_{x}(D_{j})=p^{t_{j}}-1\}\geq 1
.

Let
n=p^{t}-1\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t_{1}+t_{2}+t_{3}}-1
, and let
D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{1}+p^{t_{1}}D_{2}+p^{t_{1}+t_{2}}D_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}[D_{X}]
be an effective divisor on
X
with degree
2n
. Moreover, suppose that
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
(
2.2.5
), and that

n>{\rm max}\{C(g_{X})+1,\#(e(\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}))\}.

Then there exists an element
\alpha\in{\rm Rev}^{\rm adm}_{D}(X^{\bullet})\setminus\{0\}
(Definition
2.4
(i)) such that the following holds:

\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=g_{X}+1.

Proof.

By applying Lemma
5.5
and similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of Theorem
5.4
, we obtain the theorem.
∎

6.
Applications to anabelian geometry

In this section, we give some applications of results obtained in previous sections. The main results of the present section are Theorem
6.3
and Theorem
6.4
.

6.1.
An anabelian formula for topological types

In this subsection, by using Theorem
5.4
, we prove a group-theoretical formula for the topological type of an arbitrary pointed stable curve over an algebraically closed field of characteristic
p>0
.

6.1.1.
Settings

We maintain the notation introduced in
2.1.2
. Moreover, let
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
be the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
.

6.1.2.

Let
\Delta
be an arbitrary profinite group, and let
m\in\mathbb{N}
be a positive natural number and
\ell
a prime number. We denote by
D_{\ell}(\Delta)\subseteq\Delta
the topological closure of
[\Delta,\Delta]\Delta^{\ell}
, where
[\Delta,\Delta]
denotes the commutator subgroup of
\Delta
. Moreover, we define the closed normal subgroup
D^{(m)}_{\ell}(\Delta)
of
\Delta
inductively by
D^{(1)}_{\ell}(\Delta)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{\ell}(\Delta)
and
D_{\ell}^{(i+1)}(\Delta)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}D_{\ell}(D^{(i)}_{\ell}(\Delta))
,
i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
. Note that
\#(\Delta/D_{\ell}^{(m)}(\Delta))\leq\infty
when
\Delta
is topologically finitely generated. On the other hand, we denote by
F_{r,m}^{\ell}
the finite group
\widehat{F}_{r}/D_{\ell}^{(m)}(\widehat{F}_{r})
, where
\widehat{F}_{r}
denotes the free profinite group of rank
r
.

6.1.3.

Let
\Pi
be
an abstract profinite group
which is isomorphic to
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
as profinite groups. Moreover, we denote by
\pi_{A}(\Pi)
the set of finite quotients of
\Pi
. We put

b^{1}_{\Pi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{max}\{r\ |\ \text{there exists a prime number}\ \ell\ \text{such that}\ (\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus r}\in\pi_{A}(\Pi)\},

\displaystyle b^{2}_{\Pi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0,&F_{b_{\Pi}^{1},2}^{\ell}\in\pi_{A}(\Pi)\ \text{for some prime number}\ \ell,\\
1,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Note that
b^{i}_{\Pi},\ i\in\{1,2\}
, is a group-theoretical invariant associated to
\Pi
(i.e., depends only on the isomorphism class of
\Pi
). Firstly, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

(i) We have

b^{1}_{\Pi}=2g_{X}+n_{X}-1+b^{2}_{\Pi},

\displaystyle b^{2}_{\Pi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
0,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

(ii) There exists a unique prime number
p_{\Pi}
such that
(\mathbb{Z}/p_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus b^{1}_{\Pi}}\not\in\pi_{A}(\Pi)
. In particular, we have
p=p_{\Pi}
.

Proof.

(i) We put
r_{\Pi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{\ell}}(\Pi^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{F}_{\ell})
, where
\ell
is an arbitrary prime number
\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{p\}
, and
\mathfrak{P}
denotes the set of prime numbers. Then the structures of maximal prime-to-
p
quotients of admissible fundamental groups (
2.1.7
) imply that (see
2.2.2
for
\sigma_{X}
)

\Pi^{\rm ab}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\sigma_{X}}\times\prod_{\ell\in\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{p\}}\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}^{r_{\Pi}}.

Since
X^{\bullet}
is a pointed stable curve, we have that
\sigma_{X}<r_{\Pi}.
This implies that
b^{1}_{\Pi}=r_{\Pi}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle b^{1}_{\Pi}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2g_{X},&\text{if}\ n_{X}=0,\\
2g_{X}+n_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\neq 0.\end{array}\right.

Suppose that
n_{X}>0
. Let
\ell_{1}\in\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{p\}
. The specialization theorem of maximal pro-
\ell_{1}
quotients of admissible fundamental groups (
[
V
, Théorème 2.2 (c)]
) implies that the maximal pro-
\ell_{1}
quotient
\Pi^{\ell_{1}}
of
\Pi
is a free pro-
\ell_{1}
profinite group of rank
b_{\Pi}^{1}
. Then we have
F^{\ell_{1}}_{b^{1}_{\Pi},2}\in\pi_{A}(\Pi).
Thus, we obtain
b_{\Pi}^{2}=0
if
n_{X}>0
.

Conversely, we assume that
F^{\ell_{2}}_{b^{1}_{\Pi},2}\in\pi_{A}(\Pi)
for some prime number
\ell_{2}
. Then we have
\ell_{2}\neq p
. The Schreier index formula (
[
S2
, Chapter I §3 3.4 Corollary]
) implies the following natural exact sequence

1\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/\ell_{2}\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(b_{\Pi}^{1}-1)+1}\rightarrow F_{b_{\Pi}^{1},2}^{\ell_{2}}\rightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/\ell_{2}\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus b_{\Pi}^{1}}\rightarrow 1.

Let
\phi:\Pi\twoheadrightarrow F^{\ell_{2}}_{b^{1}_{\Pi},2}
be a surjection. We denote by
X_{\ell_{2}}^{\bullet}
the pointed stable curve over
k
corresponding to the kernel of the natural surjection
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\Pi\overset{\phi}{\twoheadrightarrow}F^{\ell_{2}}_{b^{1}_{\Pi},2}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/\ell_{2}\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus b_{\Pi}^{1}}
and by
\Pi_{\ell_{2}}\subseteq\Pi
the kernel of the surjection
\Pi\overset{\phi}{\twoheadrightarrow}F^{\ell_{2}}_{b^{1}_{\Pi},2}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/\ell_{2}\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus b_{\Pi}^{1}}
. Then we have
(\mathbb{Z}/\ell_{2}\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(b_{\Pi}^{1}-1)+1}\in\pi_{A}(\Pi_{\ell_{2}}).
This implies
b^{1}_{\Pi_{\ell_{2}}}\geq\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(b_{\Pi}^{1}-1)+1
. If
n_{X}=0
, the Riemann-Hurwitz formula implies
g_{X_{\ell_{2}}}=\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(g_{X}-1)+1,
where
g_{X_{\ell_{2}}}
denotes the genus of
X_{\ell_{2}}^{\bullet}
. Then we have
b_{\Pi_{\ell_{2}}}^{1}=2(\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(g_{X}-1)+1)=\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(b_{\Pi}^{1}-2)+2.
On the other hand,
\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(b_{\Pi}^{1}-2)+2<\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(b_{\Pi}^{1}-1)+1.
This contradicts the fact that
b^{1}_{\Pi_{\ell_{2}}}\geq\ell_{2}^{b_{\Pi}^{1}}(b_{\Pi}^{1}-1)+1
. Then we obtain
n_{X}>0
if
b_{\Pi}^{2}=0
. Moreover, we see

b^{1}_{\Pi}=2g_{X}+n_{X}-1+b^{2}_{\Pi}.

(ii) This follows immediately from the structure of
\Pi^{\rm ab}
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.1.4.

Let
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}
be an arbitrary algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{p_{\Pi}}
. Let
\chi\in\text{Hom}(\Pi,\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}^{\times})
. We denote by
\Pi_{\chi}\subseteq\Pi
the kernel of
\chi
. The profinite group
\Pi_{\chi}
admits a natural action of
\Pi
via the conjugation action. We put

N_{\chi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{\pi\in H_{\chi,p_{\Pi}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{Hom}(\Pi_{\chi},\mathbb{Z}/p_{\Pi}\mathbb{Z})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p_{\Pi}}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}\ |\ \tau\cdot\pi=\chi(\tau)\pi\ \text{for all}\ \tau\in\Pi\},

\gamma_{N_{\chi}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}}(N_{\chi}),

where
(\tau\cdot\pi)(x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\pi(\tau^{-1}\cdot x)
for all
x\in\Pi_{\chi}
. We define a group-theoretical invariant associated to
\Pi
as follows:

\gamma_{\Pi}^{\rm max}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{max}\{\gamma_{N_{\chi}}\ |\ \chi\in\text{Hom}(\Pi,\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}^{\times})\ \text{and}\ \chi\neq 1\}.

Let
\mu_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\chi(\Pi)\subseteq\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}^{\times}
be the image of
\chi
which is the group of
m
th roots of unity for some
m
prime to
p_{\Pi}
, and
X^{\bullet}_{\chi}=(X_{\chi},D_{X_{\chi}})\rightarrow X^{\bullet}
the Galois multi-admissible covering over
k
with Galois group
\mu_{m}
induced by
\chi
. Then we have a natural
\Pi
-equivariant isomorphism

H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X_{\chi},\mathbb{F}_{p_{\Pi}})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p_{\Pi}}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}\cong H_{\chi,p_{\Pi}}.

Moreover, since the actions of
\Pi
on
H^{1}_{\text{\'{e}t}}(X_{\chi},\mathbb{F}_{p_{\Pi}})\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p_{\Pi}}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p_{\Pi}}
and
H_{\chi,p_{\Pi}}
factor through
\Pi/\Pi_{\chi}\cong\mu_{m}
, the isomorphism above is also a
\mu_{m}
-equivariant. Namely,
\gamma_{N_{\chi}}
is a generalized Hasse-Witt invariant of
\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}
-cyclic admissible coverings of
X^{\bullet}
(
2.2.4
). Then we have the following result.

Lemma 6.2
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Then we have
\gamma_{\Pi}^{\rm max}=\gamma_{X^{\bullet}}^{\rm max}
(see Definition
3.10
(i) for
\gamma_{\Pi}^{\rm max}
). In particular, we have

\gamma_{\Pi}^{\rm max}=g_{X}+n_{X}-2+b_{\Pi}^{2}.

Proof.

The first part of the lemma follows from the above explanation concerning
\gamma_{N_{\chi}}
. The “in particular” part of the lemma follows immediately from Theorem
5.4
and Lemma
6.1
(i).
∎

6.1.5.

We have the following anabelian formula for
(g_{X},n_{X})
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
X^{\bullet}
be an arbitrary pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
p>0
,
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
the admissible fundamental group of
X^{\bullet}
, and
\Pi
an abstract profinite group such that
\Pi\cong\Pi_{X^{\bullet}}
as profinite groups. Then we have

g_{X}=b^{1}_{\Pi}-\gamma_{\Pi}^{\rm max}-1,

n_{X}=2\gamma^{\rm max}_{\Pi}-b_{\Pi}^{1}-b_{\Pi}^{2}+3.

In particular,
g_{X}
and
n_{X}
are group-theoretical invariants associated to
\Pi
.

Proof.

The theorem follows immediately from Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
6.2
.
∎

6.1.6.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Moreover, suppose that
X^{\bullet}
is
smooth
over
k
. In the remainder of this subsection, we discuss a formula for
(g_{X},n_{X})
which was essentially obtained by Tamagawa. Let
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
, and let
K_{n}
be the kernel of the natural surjection
\Pi\twoheadrightarrow\Pi^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. In
[
T2
]
, Tamagawa introduced

\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\text{dim}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}(K_{n}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p})}{\#(\Pi^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})}

which is called the limit of
p
-averages associated to
\Pi
. Note that since
p=p_{\Pi}
(Lemma
6.1
(ii)), we have that
\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi)
is a group-theoretical invariant associated to
\Pi
. Then the main theorem of
[
T2
]
(i.e., Tamagawa’s
p
-average theorem, see
[
T2
, Theorem 0.5]
) is the following:

\displaystyle\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}g_{X}-1,&\text{if}\ n_{X}\leq 1,\\
g_{X},&\text{if}\ n_{X}\geq 2.\end{array}\right.

Let
\ell>>0
be an arbitrary prime number distinct from
p_{\Pi}
. Write
\text{Nom}_{\ell}(\Pi)
for the set of normal subgroups of
\Pi
of index
\ell
. Suppose that
b_{\Pi}^{2}=0
(i.e.,
n_{X}\neq 0
). It is well-known that

\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi(\ell))-1=\ell(\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi))

for every
\ell\in\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{p_{\Pi}\}
and every
\Pi(\ell)\in\text{Nom}_{\ell}(\Pi)
if and only if
n_{X}=1
. We may define a group-theoretical invariant associated to
\Pi
as follows:

\displaystyle c_{\Pi}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1,&\text{if}\ b_{\Pi}^{2}=1,\\
1,&\text{if}\ b_{\Pi}^{2}=0,\ \text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi(\ell))-1=\ell(\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi))\ \text{for}\\
&\ \text{all}\ \ell\in\mathfrak{P}\setminus\{p_{\Pi}\}\ \text{and all}\ \Pi(\ell)\in\text{Nom}_{\ell}(\Pi),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{array}\right.

Then Tamagawa’s
p
-average theorem implies immediately the following formula:

g_{X}=\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi)+c_{\Pi},\ n_{X}=b_{\Pi}^{1}-2\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi)-2c_{\Pi}-b^{2}_{\Pi}+1.

In particular,
g_{X}
and
n_{X}
are group-theoretical invariants associated to
\Pi
when
X^{\bullet}
is smooth over
k
.

Before Tamagawa proved the above result, he also obtained an étale fundamental group version formula for
(g_{X},n_{X})
in a completely different way (by using wildly
ramified coverings) which is much simpler than the case of tame fundamental groups (see
[
T1
, §1]
). Note that, for any smooth pointed stable curve over an algebraically
closed field of positive characteristic, since the tame fundamental group can be reconstructed
group-theoretically from the étale fundamental group (
[
T1
, Corollary 1.10]
), the tame
fundamental group version is stronger than the étale fundamental group version. On the other hand, tame fundamental groups are much better than étale fundamental groups if we study anabelian geometry in positive characteristic from the point of view of moduli spaces (e.g.
[
T3
]
,
[
Y2
]
).

6.1.7.

The approach to finding a group-theoretical formula for
(g_{X},n_{X})
by applying the limit of
p
-averages associated to
\Pi
explained above
is difficult to be generalized
to the case where
X^{\bullet}
is an arbitrary (possibly singular) pointed stable curve. The reason is as follows. In
[
Y4
]
, the author generalized Tamagawa’s
p
-average theorem to the case of pointed stable curves (
[
Y4
, Theorem 1.3 and Theorem 1.4]
). The generalized formula concerning the limit of
p
-averages associated to
\Pi
is very complicated in general when
X^{\bullet}
is not smooth over
k
, and
\text{Avr}_{p}(\Pi)
depends not only on the topological type
(g_{X},n_{X})
but also on the structure of dual semi-graph
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}}
.

6.2.
Field structures associated to inertia subgroups of marked points

In this subsection, we prove that the field structures associated to inertia subgroups of marked points of arbitrary pointed stable curves can be reconstructed group-theoretically from admissible fundamental groups and inertia subgroups of marked points, and that a surjective open continuous homomorphism of admissible fundamental groups induces a field isomorphism of the fields associated to inertia subgroups of marked points.

6.2.1.
Settings

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
, and let
X_{i}^{\bullet}
be a pointed stable curve of type
(g_{X},n_{X})
over an algebraically closed field
k_{i}
of characteristic
p>0
,
\Gamma_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}
the dual semi-graph of
X_{i}^{\bullet}
, and
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}
the admissible fundamental group of
X_{i}^{\bullet}
. Let
\widehat{X}_{i}^{\bullet}=(\widehat{X}_{i},D_{\widehat{X}_{i}})
be the universal admissible covering of
X_{i}^{\bullet}
corresponding to
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}
(
2.2.6
),
\Gamma_{\widehat{X}_{i}^{\bullet}}
the dual semi-graph of
\widehat{X}_{i}^{\bullet}
,
e_{i}\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{X_{i}^{\bullet}})
an open edge, and
x_{e_{i}}
the closed point of
X_{i}
corresponding to
e_{i}
. Note that we have
\text{Aut}(\widehat{X}_{i}^{\bullet}/X_{i}^{\bullet})=\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}
.

Let
\widehat{e}_{i}\in e^{\rm op}(\Gamma_{\widehat{X}_{i}^{\bullet}})
be an open edge over
e_{i}
. We denote by

I_{\widehat{e}_{i}}\cong\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}(1)^{p^{\prime}}\subseteq\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}

the stabilizer subgroup of
\widehat{e}_{i}
.

6.2.2.

Write
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p,i}
for the algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
in
k_{i}
. We put

\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}(I_{\widehat{e}_{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{i}^{p^{\prime}})\sqcup\{*_{\widehat{e}_{i}}\},

where
\{*_{\widehat{e}_{i}}\}
is an one-point set, and
(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})_{i}^{p^{\prime}}
denotes the prime-to-
p
part of
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
which can be canonically identified with

\bigcup_{(p,m)=1}\mu_{m}(k_{i}).

Moreover,
\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{i}}
can be identified with
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p,i}
as sets, hence, admits
a structure of field
, whose multiplicative group is
I_{\widehat{e}_{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})^{p^{\prime}}_{i}
, and whose zero element is
*_{\widehat{e}_{i}}
. We have the following important result.

Theorem 6.4
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. Let
\phi:\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{1}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{X_{2}^{\bullet}}
be an arbitrary surjective open continuous homomorphism of admissible fundamental groups. Suppose that
\phi(I_{\widehat{e}_{1}})=I_{\widehat{e}_{2}}
, and that
n_{X}=3
. Then there exists a group-theoretical algorithm whose input data are
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}
and
I_{\widehat{e}_{i}}
, and whose output datum is
\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{i}}
(as a field). Moreover,
\phi
induces a field isomorphism

\phi^{\rm fd}_{\widehat{e}_{1},\widehat{e}_{2}}:\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{2}},

where “fd” means “field”.

Proof.

Let
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. We denote by
\mathbb{F}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}}
the unique subfield of
\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{i}}
whose cardinality is equal to
p^{t}
. On the other hand, we fix a finite field
\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}}
of cardinality
p^{t}
and an algebraic closure
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
of
\mathbb{F}_{p}
containing
\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}}
. Note that the field structure of
\mathbb{F}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}}
is equivalent to a subset

\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}})\subseteq\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}}^{\times},\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}}^{\times}),

where “gp” means “group”, and “fd” means “field”. Then in order to prove the first part of the theorem, it is sufficient to prove that there exists a group-theoretical algorithm whose input data are
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}
and
I_{\widehat{e}_{i}}
, and whose output datum is the subset
\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}})
for
t
in a cofinal subset of
\mathbb{N}
with respect to division.

Let
n\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}p^{t}-1
and
\chi_{i}\in\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p^{t}}).
Write
H_{\chi_{i}}
for the kernel of
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\overset{\chi_{i}}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}}^{\times}
,
M_{\chi_{i}}
for
H^{\rm ab}_{\chi_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{F}_{p}
, and
X^{\bullet}_{H_{\chi_{i}}}=(X_{H_{\chi_{i}}},D_{X_{H_{\chi_{i}}}})
for the pointed stable curve over
k_{i}
induced by
H_{\chi_{i}}
. Note that
M_{\chi_{i}}
admits a natural action of
\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{i}}
via the conjugation action. Moreover, this action factors through
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
. We put

M_{\chi_{i}}[\chi_{i}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{a\in M_{\chi_{i}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\ |\ \sigma\cdot a=\chi_{i}(\sigma)a\ \text{for all}\ \sigma\in\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\},

\gamma_{\chi_{i}}(M_{\chi_{i}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\text{dim}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(M_{\chi_{i}}[\chi_{i}]).

Note that
\gamma_{\chi_{i}}(M_{\chi_{i}})
is a generalized Hasse-Witt invariant of a cyclic multi-admissible covering of
X_{i}^{\bullet}
with Galois group
\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
, and that Lemma
2.5
implies that
\gamma_{\chi_{i}}(M_{\chi_{i}})\leq g_{X}+1
if
n_{X}=3
. Moreover, we define two maps

\text{Res}_{i,t}:\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p^{t}})\rightarrow\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}}^{\times}),

\Gamma_{i,t}:\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p^{t}})\rightarrow\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0},\ \chi_{i}\mapsto\gamma_{\chi_{i}}(M_{\chi_{i}}),

where the map
\text{Res}_{i,t}
is the restriction with respect to the natural inclusion

\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}}=I_{\widehat{e}_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\hookrightarrow\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}.

Let
m_{0}
be the product of all prime numbers
\leq p-2
if
p\neq 2,3
and
m_{0}=1
if
p=2,3
. Let
t_{0}
be the order of
p
in the multiplicative group
(\mathbb{Z}/m_{0}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. We have the following claim (see
[
T2
, Claim 5.4]
for the case where
X^{\bullet}_{i}
is smooth over
k_{i}
):

Claim A:
There exists a constant
C(g_{X})
which depends only on
g_{X}
such that, for each natural number
t>\text{max}\{\text{log}_{p}(C(g_{X})+1),\log_{p}(\#(e^{\rm cl}(\Gamma_{X_{i}^{\bullet}})))\}
divisible by
t_{0}
, we have

\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}})=\text{Hom}^{\rm surj}_{\rm gp}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{t},\widehat{e}_{i}}^{\times},\mathbb{F}_{p^{t}}^{\times})\setminus\text{Res}_{i,t}(\Gamma_{i,t}^{-1}(\{g_{X}+1\})),\ i\in\{1,2\},

where
\text{Hom}^{\rm surj}_{\rm gp}(-,-)
denotes the set of surjections whose elements are contained in
\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(-,-)
.

By applying similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of
[
T2
, Claim 5.4]
, Claim A is equivalent to the following claim:

Claim B:
Let
m\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}
(
2.2.5
). Then the following statements are equivalent:

(i) We have
m\in\{p^{r}\ |\ r=0,\dots,t-1\}
.

(ii) We have that
(m,n)=1
, and that, there does not exist
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
(
2.2.5
) and
\alpha\in\text{Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet}_{i})
(Definition
2.4
(i)) such that
\text{ord}_{x_{e_{i}}}(D)=m
and
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=g_{X}+1
.

Let us prove Claim B. Firstly, we prove (i)
\Rightarrow
(ii). If
s(D)=1
(
2.2.5
), then Lemma
2.5
implies
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}\leq g_{X}
. Thus, we may assume that
s(D)=2
. We put

D_{X_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}\{x_{i,1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{def}}}{{=}}x_{e_{i}},x_{i,2},x_{i,3}\}.

By
[
T2
, Proposition 2.21 (iv-a)]
, either
\text{ord}_{x_{i,1}}(D)=n
or
\text{ord}_{x_{i,3}}(D)=n
holds. This is impossible as
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
. Next, we prove (ii)
\Rightarrow
(i). Suppose that
m\not\in\{p^{r}\ |\ r=0,\dots,t-1\}
, and that
(m,n)=1
. Since
t
is divisible by
t_{0}
,
n
is divisible by all prime numbers
\leq p-2
. Then the assumption
(m,n)=1
implies that
m\not\in\{ap^{r}\ |\ a=0,\dots,p-2,r=0,\dots,t-1\}
. Then
[
T2
, Proposition 2.21 (iv-c)]
and Theorem
5.7
imply that there exist
D\in(\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z})^{\sim}[D_{X}]^{0}
and
\alpha\in\text{Rev}_{D}^{\rm adm}(X^{\bullet}_{i})
such that
\text{ord}_{x_{e_{i}}}(D)=m
and
\gamma_{(\alpha,D)}=g_{X}+1
. This completes the proof of the claim. Then we complete the proof of the first part of the theorem.

Next, we prove the “moreover” part of the theorem. Let
\kappa_{2}\in\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{2}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p^{t}}).
Then we obtain a character

\kappa_{1}\in\text{Hom}_{\rm gp}(\Pi_{X^{\bullet}_{1}}^{\rm ab}\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{F}^{\times}_{p^{r}})

induced by
\phi
. Moreover, the surjection
\phi|_{H_{\kappa_{1}}}
induces a surjection
M_{\kappa_{1}}[\kappa_{1}]\twoheadrightarrow M_{\kappa_{2}}[\kappa_{2}].
Suppose that
\kappa_{2}\in\Gamma_{2,r}^{-1}(\{g_{X}+1\})
. The surjection
M_{\kappa_{1}}[\kappa_{1}]\twoheadrightarrow M_{\kappa_{2}}[\kappa_{2}]
implies that
\gamma_{\kappa_{1}}(M_{\kappa_{1}})=g_{X}+1
. Namely, we have
\kappa_{1}\in\Gamma_{1,r}^{-1}(\{g_{X}+1\})
. On the other hand, the isomorphism
\phi|_{I_{\widehat{e}_{1}}}:I_{\widehat{e}_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}I_{\widehat{e}_{2}}
induces an injection

\text{Res}_{2,r}(\Gamma_{2,r}^{-1}(\{g_{X}+1\}))\hookrightarrow\text{Res}_{1,r}(\Gamma_{1,r}^{-1}(\{g_{X}+1\})).

Since
\#(\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{r},\widehat{e}_{1}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{r}}))=\#(\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{r},\widehat{e}_{2}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{r}}))
, Claim A implies that
\phi|_{I_{\widetilde{e}_{1}}}
induces a bijection
\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{r},\widehat{e}_{2}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{r}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{p^{r},\widehat{e}_{1}},\mathbb{F}_{p^{r}}).
Thus,
\phi|_{I_{\widehat{e}_{1}}}
induces a bijection

\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{2}},\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\text{Hom}_{\rm fd}(\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{1}},\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}).

If we choose
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}=\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{2}}
, then the image of
\text{id}_{\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{2}}}
via the above bijection induces a field isomorphism

\phi^{\rm fd}_{\widehat{e}_{1},\widehat{e}_{2}}:\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{F}_{\widehat{e}_{2}}.

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Remark 6.4.1
.

We maintain the notation introduced above. In fact, by applying Theorem
5.4
, we can prove that
I_{\widehat{e}_{i}}
can be reconstructed group-theoretically from
\Pi_{X_{i}^{\bullet}}
, and that
\phi(I_{\widehat{e}_{1}})
is always a stabilizer of an open edge of
\Gamma_{\widehat{X}^{\bullet}_{2}}
over an open edge of
\Gamma_{X^{\bullet}_{2}}
(
[
Y5
, Theorem 4.11]
). Moreover, by applying similar arguments to the arguments given in the proof of
[
Y2
, Proposition 6.1]
, to reconstruct the field structures, we may assume that
n_{X}=3
. Thus, Theorem
6.4
implies that the field structures associated to inertia subgroups of marked points can be reconstructed group-theoretically from surjective open continuous homomorphisms of admissible fundamental groups (
[
Y5
, Theorem 4.13]
).
